| 1 | Switch Macs faster than iCloud delivers, and type on the stale copy | Reload finds local edits → merge (A or B) | 2 (no loss); 3 said honestly in the strip |
| 2 | The laptop lid closes before iCloud uploads | Saved on the way to sleep (§4.3). The desktop can't know yet; when the laptop wakes and uploads, the desktop merges. The laptop's menu bar item showed ▲ before it closed | 1, 2 |
| 3 | Both Macs offline, both edited | iCloud keeps a conflict version or makes a duplicate → D or C | 2 |
| 4 | Both Macs changed the same line | Word-level retry; otherwise conflict markers and a conflict block (B) | 2 |
| 5 | Another app on this Mac writes the file (Sublime, vim, `echo >> scratch.md`) | FSEvents or the presenter → reload or merge, as for another Mac | 2 |
| 6 | The debounce fires just as a new version lands | Compare-and-swap aborts the write and merges first | 2 (L4) |
| 7 | Evicted by Optimize Mac Storage | Never read on the main thread: in M0 a coordinated read of an evicted 200 KB file blocked 1.1 s while it downloaded (an explicit download took 0.45 s). Download started; ◐ shown; F if offline. First run suggests Finder's "Keep Downloaded" for the folder; the SDK has no API to keep one file downloaded (M0 checked) | 1, 3 |
| 8 | Deleted or moved to the Trash elsewhere | E (*built 2026-09-30, by another app on this Mac; §8, Failure modes*) | 2 |
| 9 | Renamed or moved elsewhere | The presenter follows the move; the title updates; the scratch binding follows too (*built 2026-09-30 for files other than the scratch file, which only says where it went; §8, Failure modes*) | 2 |
| 10 | The Rune folder is deleted or renamed | As 8 or 9, for the folder | 2 |
| 11 | iCloud Drive turned off, or signed out, on this Mac (the folder may disappear) | The buffer stays; saves go to `Application Support/nib/Recovery/`; the strip reads `✕ ICLOUD DRIVE IS OFF ON THIS MAC · SAVING TO RUNE'S RECOVERY FOLDER`; merged back when the file returns | 1, 2 |
| 12 | iCloud storage full, or another upload error | Red `✕ ICLOUD STORAGE IS FULL · SAVED LOCALLY ONLY`; keeps saving locally; retries when the error clears | 1 |
| 13 | This Mac's disk is full | The journal and the save both fail. The one case where text lives only in memory: red strip `✕ DISK FULL · YOUR LATEST CHANGES ARE ONLY IN MEMORY · FREE SPACE OR COPY THEM`, a notification, a retry every 15 s, and **Empty Trash** offered when the Trash holds enough to help (borrowed from Blit's WAITING FOR SPACE, R5.8 and R5.9) | 1 degrades, loudly |
| 14 | Rune crashes, is force-quit, or the Mac panics | G at next launch; at most 300 ms lost | 1 |
| 14a | …and leaves a file paused | At launch, Rune resumes every file on its paused list that it won't open (`preserveLocalChanges` in M1; `afterUploadWithFailOnConflict` with the merge flow in M2). The file it will open is resumed (fail-on-conflict first), uploaded if iCloud may lack its last save, and paused fresh. A pause never outlives Rune's next launch (M1: tested with `kill -9`) | 2 |
| 14b | Rune isn't running on a Mac where a file is still paused (quit during a crash loop, or removed) | That Mac neither uploads nor downloads the file until it's resumed. `rune --resume-sync` resumes everything Rune paused; removing Rune means quitting it first, which resumes. Stated in the README | 2 |
| 14c | `pauseSync` answers "resource busy" (iCloud is still applying a change) | Retry after 0.5, 1, 2… s (M0 hit it right after an upload); meanwhile the file runs unheld | n/a |
| 14d | Another app on the same Mac reads the file while Rune holds it | It sees what Rune last saved; newer versions from other Macs arrive only when Rune fetches (§4.4) | 3 |
| 15 | Two Rune windows on the same file | One buffer per file (ED2) | n/a |
| 16 | Clocks differ between Macs | Order by lineage and hashes, never by modification date alone; each side labeled with its own device's time | 2 |
| 17 | Line endings or encoding differ between versions | Compared after normalizing; written back in the file's original form | 1 |
| 18 | IME composition open when a change arrives | Applied after the text is committed (§4.4) | n/a |
| 19 | A remote change lands while typing | Held until 300 ms after the last keystroke (2 s at most), then merged; a change touching the caret's own lines becomes a conflict block instead of moving text under the cursor | 2 |
| 20 | Both Macs create scratch.md on first run | A duplicate appears → C, unrelated → two-way merge view | 2 |
| 21 | Older Rune on the other Mac | Receipts and lineage carry a format version; unknown versions are ignored, not misread | 2 |
| 22 | Low Power Mode or a constrained network slows iCloud | Waiting states say why (W4) | 3 |
| 23 | Secrets in scratch.md (API tokens in terminal commands) | History and the journal are local only, never synced. The file itself is in iCloud Drive, which is end-to-end encrypted only with Advanced Data Protection; Settings says so in one sentence | privacy |
| 24 | A scratch file past 1 MB, or a huge paste | Edit-log journal above 1 MB (§4.3), so typing writes bytes, not megabytes | 1 |

### 4.10 Is iCloud Drive enough? A V2 recommendation

**What iCloud Drive gives:** durable storage with no account, server or cost beyond the user's plan; a real file that Finder, Terminal, other editors and the iPhone's Files app all see; optional end-to-end encryption.

**What it can't give:** a bound on delivery time. Apple publishes no latency figure, and a Mac that sleeps before its upload finishes holds its changes until it wakes. On its own schedule iCloud is slow for this job (M0: saves every second were never uploaded until typing stopped). macOS 26's sync controls fix most of that: Rune uploads within about 5 s of typing and fetches the latest version when you switch to a Mac (§4.3, §4.4). What remains is the gap between the last keystroke and the end of its upload (about 2.5 s), plus whatever S1b measures on the receiving side. V1's merge engine makes all of it safe (guarantee 2). V2 can only make it faster (guarantee 3).

| Option | Latency | Covers "switched Macs within seconds"? | Covers "lid closed before upload"? | Cost and fit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1. iCloud Drive alone** (V1), with sync controls | S1b: save to load on the other Mac in 2.5 s median, 3.6 s p95 (plain iCloud: 3.6 s and 6.2 s) | Yes, unless the switch takes under about 3 s | No, if the lid closes within the 2 s upload | None. Fits the family (no servers) |
| **2. LAN peer-to-peer express lane** (Network.framework, Bonjour, TLS with keys exchanged through iCloud Keychain or key-value storage) | Under 100 ms, both Macs awake on the same network | Yes, when on the same network | Yes: the edit leaves within one debounce, before the lid closes | Moderate. Needs local-network permission, which Blit already asks for (`NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription`). Useless when the Macs are apart |
| **3. CloudKit express lane** (each save as a record in the private database, pushed to the other Mac) | Usually seconds; pushes can be coalesced or throttled | Mostly | Partly: it still has to leave before sleep | Moderate. Adds a CloudKit container and schema; still Apple's infrastructure, with no guarantee |
| **4. Handoff** (`NSUserActivity` with continuation streams: the other Mac pulls the text directly) | About a second, on demand | Yes, if the user clicks the Handoff icon | Only if the first Mac is awake | Small. Explicit UX; historically unreliable. Good as a P2 extra, not as the mechanism |
| **5. CRDT document model** (Automerge, Yjs, Loro) | Doesn't change latency | No | No | High. Fixes merge semantics, which V1's diff3 already handles for one person; state would live outside the plain file |
| **6. Third-party sync** (Dropbox, Syncthing, a Rune server) | Varies | Varies | No | Accounts, money or servers. Against the family's no-server stance (Blit licenses with "no Blit server") |

**Recommendation:**

- **Ship V1 on iCloud Drive alone**, instrumented: the Sync Log records how long each version took to arrive (from lineage times and receipts), so the need for V2 is measured rather than guessed (T3).
- **Build a V2 express lane only if the numbers call for it.** The sync controls make this less likely than it looked before M0, and S1b's first numbers (2.5 s median, 3.6 s p95 from save to the other Mac's disk, with the upload itself taking 2.1 s of it) make it less likely still. Build it only if, over two weeks of real use, the 95th percentile from save on one Mac to load on the other exceeds the time it actually takes to switch Macs (open question 3), or if any stale-copy merge shows up at all in the log.
- **If the Macs are usually on the same network, choose option 2 (LAN peer).** It's the only option that also covers the lid-closed-before-upload case, and it answers exactly the failure described: sequential switching between Macs that sit near each other. **If they're usually apart** (home and office), choose option 3 (CloudKit). Open question 2 decides.
- **Either way, the express lane only delivers versions early.** iCloud Drive stays the file of record, and an early version enters the same merge pipeline as any other remote version, so no new conflict rules. Lineage (§4.6) lets a later iCloud copy of the same version be recognized as already applied.
- **Skip options 5 and 6.** Handoff (4) is a P2 nicety on top of whichever lane is chosen.

---

## 5. UX and visual design

### 5.1 The scratch window

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ● ● ●   scratch.md                                                   │
│         iCloud Drive › Rune                                           │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│   ## Deploy                                                          │
│   ```bash                                                            │
│   ssh me@seedbox 'df -h ~/files'                                     │
│   ```                                                                │
│                                                                      │
│   ## Email · Q4 vendor                                               │
│   Hi Sam, thanks for the quick turnaround on the…▌                   │
│                                                                      │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ✓ SYNCED · 10:42:07 · ON STUDIO      LN 7, COL 48 · MARKDOWN · LF    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   editor area: An Old Hope (A1)          strip: always-dark HUD (A2, V2)
```

- **Chrome (V1, V12, A17):** always the dark appearance; a standard title bar showing the file name, with its folder as the subtitle, as Blit uses `navigationTitle`/`navigationSubtitle`. No toolbar buttons. Liquid Glass comes from the system. Opens at 720×560; the frame is remembered per Mac.
- **Editor area (A1):** the scheme's background runs edge to edge. Text inset 12 pt at the sides and 8 pt at the top; line spacing from Sublime's `line_padding`. Line numbers are off by default in the scratch window and on for code files (both settings).
- **Empty state (W6):** in an empty scratch file, one dim line in the scheme's comment color, "Paste or type. Saved as you go, on every Mac.", gone at the first keystroke.
- **No "Edited" marker.** Nothing stays unsaved long enough to need one; the strip shows the state (ED4).

### 5.2 The status strip

A 24 pt, always-dark bar at the bottom of every editor window (A2, A3). Blit's strip tokens: #0A0A0A background, 0.5 pt #2B2B2B top border, 12 pt side padding (V2, V12).

- **Left: sync state,** as in §4.5. Glyph from Blit's 7×7 set; words in VT323 16; the red tag, when there is one, in Silkscreen 9 on #B35C5C with black text (V3). While uploading, the ▲ glyph's stem marches like Blit's menu bar arrow (V7), but only after 3 s pending (A10).
- **Right, fixed-width slots (V10):** `LN 7, COL 48 · MARKDOWN · 2 SPACES · UTF-8 · LF`. Clicking the line slot goes to a line (⌃G); the syntax, indentation and line-endings slots open their menus, as View's do (indentation: tabs or spaces, the width, Guess Indentation from the File, Convert Indentation; 2026-10-07: F50, §8); the encoding is shown, kept as found.
- **Click the sync state** to expand the sync HUD (§5.3). Right-click: Save Now (File › Save's own, with ⌘S's tidying; Save… for an untitled buffer), Show Sync Log, Show History, Show in Finder (not for an untitled buffer). File has Show in Finder, Show Sync Log for This File, Restore and, while the strip offers it, Empty Trash… too (2026-10-07, the QA review's F44, F45).
- **Always shown:** View › Hide Status Bar, with the sync glyph moving to the title bar, is dropped (2026-10-07: F50, §8).
- **VoiceOver:** one summary, buttons reachable (AX1), as Blit's strip does.

### 5.3 The sync HUD (P2)

Clicking the sync state opens the strip upward into a HUD with Blit's CRT switch-on (V7). Escape or a second click collapses it. It never opens by itself (A2).

```
┌ ▼ SYNC · SCRATCH.MD ──────────────────────────────────────── RUNE://SYNC ┐
│ ┌ FILE ────────────────────┐ ┌ ICLOUD ───────────────┐ ┌ MACS ───────────────┐ │
│ │ SCRATCH.MD               │ │ STATE      ✓ SYNCED   │ │ AIR     THIS MAC    │ │
│ │ ICLOUD DRIVE › RUNE       │ │ SAVED      10:42:05   │ │ STUDIO  ✓ 10:42:31  │ │
│ │ 4.1 KB · 212 LINES       │ │ UPLOADED   10:42:07   │ │ —                   │ │
│ │ SHA256 9F3A1C…           │ │ ON DISK    CURRENT    │ │ —                   │ │
│ └──────────────────────────┘ └───────────────────────┘ └─────────────────────┘ │
│ ┌ LOG ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ 10:41:58 ▼ STUDIO'S VERSION ARRIVED · MERGED 2 CHANGES                    │ │
│ │ 10:42:05 = SAVED LOCALLY · 4.1 KB                                         │ │
│ │ 10:42:06 ▲ UPLOADING                                                      │ │
│ │ 10:42:07 ✓ SYNCED                                                         │ │
│ │ 10:42:31 ✓ STUDIO HAS THIS VERSION                                        │ │
│ │ —                                                                         │ │
│ └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Blit's HUD rules, unchanged (V10, V12): boxed panels with 1 pt borders and Silkscreen titles, the same rows every time with "—" where one doesn't apply, a six-line log at 18 pt per line, a fixed height, and `RUNE://SYNC` echoing `BLIT://HUD` (W8). *Built 2026-10-06 (§8, "The sync HUD"); View › Show Sync Details (⇧⌘Y, §5.12) opens it too.* An unresolved conflict adds Blit's WARNING panel in #B35C5C with black text, at exactly the log's height: `WARNING · 1 CONFLICT · LINES 14-16 · BOTH SIDES KEPT IN THE FILE · MERGE VIEW ⌥⌘R` (W1; RESOLVE until 2026-10-07, F52).

### 5.4 Conflict banner and conflict blocks (P1)

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ [✕ CONFLICT] Studio and this Mac both changed 1 paragraph. Next Conflict │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│   Hi Sam, thanks for the quick turnaround.                           │
│ ✕ ┌ AIR · 10:42 ─────────────────────── ⌘1 USE · ⌘3 BOTH ┐           │
│   │ We can ship on Friday if QA signs off.               │           │
│   ├ STUDIO · 10:41 ───────────────────────────── ⌘2 USE ┤           │
│   │ We can ship Thursday.                                │           │
│   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘           │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

- The banner is native text with the red tag (V3, V9). It stays until every block is resolved, and Escape hides it for the session.
- A block's frame and labels are HUD-style, 1 pt border and Silkscreen labels; the text inside uses the editor's font and scheme, since it's for reading (V1). The gutter ✕ is #B35C5C with a word label for VoiceOver (V4, AX2).
- The underlying text is the conflict markers (§4.7). Other editors see them as plain markers.

### 5.5 Merge view (P1)

A sheet on the editor window for more than three conflicts, a duplicate file, or a two-way comparison.

```
┌ RUNE://MERGE · SCRATCH.MD · 2 CONFLICTS · 5 MERGED ─────────────────────┐
│ ┌ AIR · THIS MAC · 10:42 ─────────┐ ┌ STUDIO · 10:41 ─────────────────┐ │
│ │   Hi Sam, thanks for…           │ │   Hi Sam, thanks for…           │ │
│ │ ~ We can ship on Friday if QA…  │ │ ~ We can ship Thursday.         │ │
│ │ + Also: invoice attached.       │ │                                 │ │
│ └─────────────────────────────────┘ └─────────────────────────────────┘ │
│ ┌ RESULT (EDITABLE) ──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ Hi Sam, thanks for…                                                 │ │
│ │ ✕ [conflict 1 of 2: ⌘1 Air · ⌘2 Studio · ⌘3 both]                   │ │
│ │ Also: invoice attached.                                             │ │
│ └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
│  ⌥⌘↓ next conflict    Cancel            Keep Both Files     Done ⌘↩     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

- The panes use the editor's font and scheme. Changes are marked with glyphs, `+` added, `−` removed, `~` changed, and tinted with the scheme's own `markup.inserted`, `markup.deleted` and `markup.changed` backgrounds (An Old Hope defines all three: #253B22, #420E09, #4A4A0F), so color never carries it alone (V4) and the view matches the theme (A1).
- The header is HUD grammar (W5); buttons are native (V1). Keyboard: ⌘1, ⌘2, ⌘3 and ⌥⌘↓/⌥⌘↑ as in the blocks; ⌘↩ finishes; Escape cancels and keeps the conflict markers in the file.
- **Done** writes the result, records both inputs in History, marks iCloud conflict versions resolved, and moves a duplicate file to the Trash. When everything is resolved: `ALL CONFLICTS RESOLVED · ⌘↩ TO FINISH` (W6).

### 5.6 Go to File and the command palette (P1)

Blit's `CommandPalette`, copied and generalized (`nib/Vendor/FromBlit/CommandPalette.swift`): native SwiftUI, 520 pt wide, SF Symbols, a group label on the right of each row (V1, V12, A6).

- **Go to File (⌘P, ⌘K):** prompt "Go to a file, a recent file or the scratch". Groups: Scratch, Open, Recent (newest first, Blit's R2.4 default), Rune folder. Empty result: "No matches" (W6).
- **Commands (⇧⌘P):** every menu item, named as in the menu (W3), with its shortcut on the right.

### 5.7 Menu bar item and panel (P1)

- **Item (V11, A10):** a 9-pixel-wide pixel ᚱ (the rune raidō, R for Rune; a pen nib until 2026-10-07) over an underline, a template image of fixed width. Still when idle. When an upload has been pending for more than 3 s, the underline's dashes march left to right. The muted-red ✕ mark sits beside it while a conflict or save problem is unresolved.
  ```
  .##......
  .####....
  .##.##...
  .####....
  .##.##...
  .##..##..
  .##...##.
  .##....##
  .........
  #########    ← the line: marches while uploading
  ```
  (Drawn 2026-10-07, the user's pick: `NibMark.rune` in `nib/Views/MenuBar.swift`, eight rows over a blank one, as the nib's were.)
- **Panel:** Blit's 320 pt column of HUD cards (V12). One card for scratch.md: state, saved and uploaded times, other Macs and their receipts. Then actions in native rows: Show Scratch ⌥Space, Clean Clipboard ⌃⌥⌘V, Append Clipboard to Scratch, recent files, Settings…, Quit Rune.

### 5.8 Settings

Blit's format (V12, W7): tabs, grouped forms that size to fit, 480 pt wide, a caption note under each control.

- **General:** launch at login; show Rune in the menu bar; Show Scratch shortcut; Clean Clipboard shortcut; Paste Clean options (smart quotes, dashes; moved from Editor on 2026-10-07, which they made too tall for the Air's screen, the QA review's F22); notifications (conflicts and problems only; the note says saves never notify; in Scratch & Sync since 2026-10-07).
- **Editor:** font and size; line numbers (scratch, code files); soft wrap; tab width, spaces or tabs; show invisibles; trim trailing whitespace; ensure final newline. Note under trimming: "Only in files you edit in Rune. Files you just open are never rewritten."
- **Themes:** the scheme in use (An Old Hope · Rune by default); other imported schemes; Import from Sublime Text…; Import File…; the last import report (HL4). Note: "Rune is always dark."
- **Sync** ("Scratch & Sync" until 2026-10-08, the user's call: one word, as the other tabs): scratch file location (Reveal, Change…); this Mac's name; History retention (default 30 days); Clear History…. Note: "History and the recovery journal stay on this Mac. scratch.md itself is in iCloud Drive, which is end-to-end encrypted only with Advanced Data Protection."
- **Files** (added 2026-10-06, open question 15): "Reopen the files that were open when Rune quit" (2026-10-07, on by default); "Open with Rune", a switch for each kind of file a double-click in Finder opens: Markdown, plain text, JSON, CSS, JavaScript, shell scripts (HTML too until 2026-10-07: see §8, "HTML out of Settings › Files"). Each says its extensions and, while off, the app that has it now (".md · now Xcode"). Note: "A double-click in Finder opens these files in Rune, and so does the open command in Terminal. Turning one off gives its files back to the app they opened in before. Shell scripts are files macOS can run, so it asks you to confirm each of .sh, .zsh and .bash: three times. For one file alone, use Finder's Get Info › Open With."
- **Updates** and **License** (DS2).

### 5.9 First run (P1)

One window, three steps, native controls (V1):

1. **Scratch file.** "Where's your scratch.md?" Buttons: Choose… · Create “nib/scratch.md” in iCloud Drive. The note explains that macOS may ask for access to iCloud Drive and why.
2. **Look.** Rune already uses An Old Hope · Rune. "Use your Sublime Text font and tab settings?" Import from Sublime (showing what it found, e.g. "Menlo 13 · 2 spaces") · Skip.
3. **Keys.** The two global shortcuts, and "Open Rune when you log in".

### 5.10 About (P2)

Blit's About, in Rune's words (V5, V7): the pixel icon at 96 pt; `RUNE` in Silkscreen 34; the tagline in VT323 with the blinking `_`; a dashed divider; `V0.1 · BUILD 1`; `SILKSCREEN · VT323 · SIL OPEN FONT LICENSE 1.1`. *Built 2026-10-06 (§8): as Blit's About is now, the fonts' line gave way to ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS ›, where the fonts' licenses are with the rest (DS3); under Reduce Motion the `_` stays lit (A16).*

Tagline proposal, in the pattern of Blit's "MOVE BIG FILES FAST. VERIFY EVERY BYTE" (two short imperatives): **"WRITE IT DOWN. LOSE NOTHING_"**

### 5.11 App icon (P2)

Blit's recipe (V11): an Icon Composer `.icon` (`nib/AppIcon.icon`) with a solid near-black fill (0.045) and one pixel layer, glass off, rendered by `scripts/make-icon.swift` (Blit's script with Rune's art and name). **Since 2026-10-07 (the rename, the user's pick):** the rune ᚱ, raidō ("journey", R for Rune), its stave, bow and leg in two-cell strokes, over the family mark:

```
...##.........
...####.......
...##.##......
...##..##.....
...##.##......
...####.......
...##.##......
...##..##.....
...##...##....
...##....##...
..............
##.##.##.##.::
```

**Built 2026-09-28, as Nib:** on Blit's 14×12 grid, a fountain-pen nib, diagonal, with its breather hole and a one-cell slit down to the tip, writing on the family mark:

```
........####..
.......#####..
......######..
.....######...
....##..##....     ← the breather hole
...###.##.....     ← the slit, down to the tip
..###.##......
..##.##.......
.##.#.........
.#............     ← the tip
..............
##.##.##.##.::     ← the same bottom row as Blit's icon: the family mark
```

The first draft was upright, tip down; drawn at size it read as a keyhole or a shield. Diagonal, it reads as a pen writing the dashed line, as Blit's arrow points down into its progress cells.

### 5.12 Keyboard

Sublime's defaults where Sublime has the command (KB4); Apple's where it's a standard Mac command (W3).

| Command | Keys | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| Show Scratch (global) | ⌥Space (proposed) | P0 |
| Clean Clipboard (global) | ⌃⌥⌘V | P1 |
| Save now · Close · Quit | ⌘S · ⌘W · ⌘Q | P0 |
| Find · Find next / previous | ⌘F · ⌘G / ⇧⌘G (Return / ⇧Return in the field) | P0 |
| New · New Tab · Open · Open Recent | ⌘N · ⌘T · ⌘O · File menu | P1 |
| Reopen closed file | ⇧⌘T | P1 |
| Go to File · Commands | ⌘P (⌘K) · ⇧⌘P | P1 |
| Find and replace · Use selection for find | ⌥⌘F · ⌘E | P1 |
| Replace · Replace all (Sublime) | ⌥⌘E · ⌃⌥Return | P1 |
| Go to line | ⌃G | P1 |
| Select line · Join lines | ⌘L · ⌘J | P1 |
| Duplicate line · Move line up / down | ⇧⌘D · ⌃⌘↑ / ⌃⌘↓ | P1 |
| Toggle comment · Indent / outdent | ⌘/ · ⌘] / ⌘[ | P1 |
| Add next occurrence · Select all occurrences · Split into lines | ⌘D · ⌃⌘G · ⇧⌘L | P1 (ED8) |
| Add a caret · a column · a caret above / below | ⌘-click · ⌥-drag · ⌃⇧↑ / ⌃⇧↓ | P1 (ED8) |
| Find All (every match, a caret each) | ⌥Return, with the find bar open | P1 (ED8) |
| Undo selection · Redo selection (the carets' last change; Sublime's soft undo) | ⌘U · ⇧⌘U | P1 (ED8) |
| Skip to next occurrence (Sublime's ⌘K ⌘D) | none yet: the ⌘K chords are open question 10 | P1 (ED8) |
| Paste Clean | ⌥⇧⌘V | P1 |
| Copy Block | ⇧⌘C | P1 |
| Merge view · Use first / second / both · Next / previous conflict | ⌥⌘R · ⌘1 / ⌘2 / ⌘3 · ⌥⌘↓ / ⌥⌘↑ | P1 |
| Select tab 1-9 (Sublime) | ⌘1…⌘9, except with the caret inside a conflict block | P1 |
| Sync Log | ⌥⌘L (Blit's Transfer Log key) | P1 |
| Show Sync Details | ⇧⌘Y | P2 |
| Go to Heading (Sublime's Goto Symbol): a Markdown file's headings | ⌘R | P2 (done in M2) |
| Close what's open over the text, one thing a press: with several carets, one caret first (Sublime); then the sync HUD, the find bar, and last the conflict banner (until the blocks change); nothing else (not the word completion list, which is ⌥Esc). One order with KB1 since 2026-10-07 (the QA review's F32) | Esc | P0 |

⇧⌘T is "Reopen Closed File" here because that's what it means in Sublime and browsers; Blit uses it for Show Transfer Details, which Rune moves to ⇧⌘Y.

### 5.13 Copy deck

The voice (W1-W7), applied to Rune's common moments.

| Moment | Copy |
|---|---|
| Strip, clean merge | `✓ MERGED 2 CHANGES FROM STUDIO · ⌘Z UNDOES` |
| Strip, reload | `▼ UPDATED FROM STUDIO · 10:42:31` |
| Strip, upload not starting | `= SAVED LOCALLY · ICLOUD HASN'T STARTED UPLOADING (2 MIN)` |
| Strip, save failed | `✕ COULDN'T SAVE “INDEX.HTML” · PERMISSION DENIED · YOUR TEXT IS IN RUNE'S RECOVERY FOLDER` |
| Strip, large file | `HIGHLIGHTING OFF · 42 MB` |
| Menu bar panel, clipboard | `CLIPBOARD CLEANED · 3 INVISIBLES REMOVED` |
| Banner, deleted | `SCRATCH.MD WAS MOVED TO THE TRASH ON STUDIO` · Restore · Close |
| Notification, conflict | "Scratch changed on two Macs" / "scratch.md" / "Studio and Air both edited the same paragraph. Nothing is lost; open Rune to choose." |
| Notification, save failed | "Couldn't save “notes.md”" / "iCloud Drive" / "The disk is full. Rune is keeping your changes and trying again every 15 seconds." |
| Recovery | `RECOVERED 12 WORDS TYPED BEFORE RUNE QUIT UNEXPECTEDLY` |
| Empty scratch | "Paste or type. Saved as you go, on every Mac." |
| Palette, nothing found | "No matches" |
| Strip, trial's last day | `TRIAL · 1 DAY LEFT · SETTINGS › LICENSE` |
| Banner, trial ended (DS4) | `RUNE'S 7-DAY TRIAL HAS ENDED · WHAT YOU'VE TYPED IS SAVED AND STILL SYNCS` · Buy Rune… · Enter License Key… |
| Strip, typing after the trial | `READ ONLY · TO TYPE, BUY RUNE OR ENTER A KEY IN SETTINGS › LICENSE` |
| Settings note, sync | "Rune saves a second after you stop typing, and whenever you switch away. There is no unsaved state to lose." |

---

## 6. Technical architecture

### 6.1 Platform (matching Blit, T1 and T6)

| | |
|---|---|
| OS | macOS 27 only (both Macs: open question 4) |
| Hardware | Apple silicon only |
| Language and UI | Swift 6 with strict concurrency; SwiftUI app lifecycle, AppKit for the text view and window-level behavior |
| Appearance | Dark only, always (`NSApp.appearance = .darkAqua`, A17) |
| Distribution | Developer ID signed, Hardened Runtime, notarized and stapled; not sandboxed |
| Name and bundle ID | **Rune** (since 2026-10-07, the user's call: §10, question 14): the app (`Rune.app`), its iCloud Drive folder and the `rune` command. Nib, capitalized like Blit, from 2026-09-28; lowercase `nib` from 2026-09-27. Inside, the code's names (NibKit, NibText, the `nib` scheme and folder) and `Application Support/nib/` keep Nib. The bundle ID `com.banastas.nib` and container `iCloud.com.banastas.nib` stay as registered (Blit is `com.banastas.Blit`) |
| Category | Developer tools (`public.app-category.developer-tools`) |
| Build | Xcode 27; hand-written project with a folder-synced group; `NibKit` as a local package (T2) |

**Why not sandboxed** (as Blit): Rune opens any file on any volume, is driven from a CLI, reads Sublime's settings and packages, and follows iCloud Drive paths it didn't create.

### 6.2 Structure

```
┌─────────────────────────────── SwiftUI app (Rune) ─────────────────────────────────┐
│ Scratch window · file windows (native tabs) · merge sheet · palettes · Settings    │
│ Menu bar extra · status strip and sync HUD (Blit's pixels) · hotkeys · CLI bridge  │
└──────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
               │ @Observable @MainActor models, async/await (T9)
┌──────────────▼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Editor (AppKit, in the app): NibTextView (NSTextView) · highlight bridge · carets  │
└──────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
┌──────────────▼──────────────── NibKit (Swift package, no UI) ───────────────────────┐
│ NibSync                     │ NibSyntax                │ NibTheme                   │
│ • Document: buffer, base,   │ • tree-sitter parsing,   │ • .tmTheme, .sublime-      │
│   fingerprint               │   incremental            │   color-scheme parsers     │
│ • Journal · History ·       │ • injections (script,    │ • scope selectors, ranking │
│   Shadow · Sync Log         │   style, code fences)    │ • color() adjusters        │
│ • compare-and-swap saver    │ • captures → TextMate    │ • Sublime importer (zip    │
│ • presenter + FSEvents      │   scope map              │   read in memory, L1)      │
│ • CloudStatus (resource     │                          │ • import report            │
│   values, NSMetadataQuery)  │                          │                            │
│ • Merge (diff3, words)      │                          │                            │
│ • Receipts                  │                          │                            │
├─────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┤
│ NibText: encodings · line endings · paste cleanup · SHA-256 fingerprints (CryptoKit)│
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ rune (CLI): open · wait · stdin · append                                            │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### 6.3 Key decisions

**Text view: `NSTextView`, wrapped for SwiftUI.** SwiftUI's `TextEditor` offers no control over highlighting attributes, carets or performance. A custom renderer (the Zed approach) would mean rebuilding input methods, accessibility and bidirectional text for none of the four jobs. **TextKit 2, decided by S2 (2026-09-27).** At 2.4 MB its main-thread cost per keystroke was a tenth to a half of TextKit 1's (p99 2 ms without highlighting and ≤ 10 ms with a stand-in highlighter, against 20-21 ms and 17-19 ms), and it used 16 MB less memory after opening, 100 MB less with the whole document highlighted ([M0 results](#m0-results)). The price is the one other editors report: TextKit 2 estimates the height of text it hasn't laid out, so the scroll bar's size and position shift as you move through a large file (the estimate varied by 175,000 pt at 2.4 MB). Laying out the whole document doesn't fix it in TextKit 2 (it made the estimate vary more and mid-document keystrokes 41 ms), so Rune doesn't. Rules that follow: nothing ever touches `layoutManager` on a TextKit 2 view (that silently switches it to TextKit 1; debug builds assert the engine), and the editor module keeps TextKit behind one type, so falling back to TextKit 1 stays a contained change if the scroll bar proves unacceptable in use. Highlighting colors are rendering attributes, which never touch the text storage, undo or layout; bold, italic and underline change glyphs, so they're font and underline attributes set on the text storage in the visible range, outside undo and never counted as edits.

**Highlighting: tree-sitter, with a capture-to-scope map.** Two ways to reach J3:

| | tree-sitter (SwiftTreeSitter + C grammars) | Sublime's own grammars (syntect, Rust) |
|---|---|---|
| Speed on edits | Incremental reparse, fast | Line-by-line regex with cached state; fine at these sizes |
| Match with Sublime | Approximate: tree-sitter captures (`keyword`, `string`, `function`) are mapped to TextMate scopes (`keyword.control.js`, `string.quoted.double.json`, `entity.name.function.js`) | Exact scopes, if Sublime's default `.sublime-syntax` files can be bundled (licensing unknown) |
| Stack | SwiftPM C targets; stays Swift + SPM (T7) | A Rust toolchain and an xcframework in a Swift-only family |
| Theme side | Rune's own parser either way: syntect reads `.tmTheme` but not `.sublime-color-scheme` | same |

**Highlight the viewport, parse the document.** S2 showed that applying rendering attributes to a whole 2.4 MB document costs 308 ms and a lot of memory, while re-highlighting a window of ±3,000 characters around each edit stays within a few milliseconds. So the parser (below) runs over the whole document off the main thread, but attributes are applied only to what's visible plus a margin, and again as the view scrolls. Built so in M2: the tree is kept across edits and reparsed incrementally, and queries, embedded languages and styles cover only the text in view with a margin (§8, Incremental highlighting). Since 2026-09-30 a big Markdown, JavaScript or CSS file (over 256K characters) keeps a tree of only the stretch around the view, parsed from a place where a parse of the whole is in its starting state too, so it colors exactly as the whole does (§8, Idle memory with a big file).

**Recommendation: tree-sitter.** The grammars for all six languages exist (HTML, CSS, JavaScript, JSON, Markdown block and inline, Bash) under permissive licenses, and injections cover `<script>`, `<style>` and code fences. The scope map is written against Sublime's actual scopes for these languages, including the `text source` context An Old Hope's "Embedded Source" rule relies on. **Fallback:** syntect behind the same highlighter protocol, only if the fidelity harness (S3) can't reach 98%. That would be the one "strong reason" to leave the pure Swift stack, and would be recorded here with the numbers.

**Documents: own layer over `NSFilePresenter`, `NSFileCoordinator` and FileManager's sync controls, not `NSDocument` or `DocumentGroup`.** `NSDocument` gives autosave and iCloud handling, but its autosave timing and its conflict window (pick a version, no merge) are opaque, and it saves by replacing the file, which M0 showed drops a sync pause. Rune needs a 1 s debounce, in-place compare-and-swap saves, a journal, uploads it times itself and a merge flow, so it owns the pipeline: pause, save in place, upload with fail-on-conflict, fetch, resume (§4.3, §4.4). The sync controls need an app identity (a bare command-line binary gets "feature not supported"), so the `rune` CLI never writes synced files itself; it asks the running app.

**App lifecycle: SwiftUI `App`, as Blit** (`Window` for scratch, `WindowGroup(for:)` for files, `MenuBarExtra`, `Settings`, `Window` for About and History), with the `NSApplicationDelegate` adaptor for opening files, termination, sleep and Services. S2 measured the cost: first window at a median 201 ms after process start with SwiftUI, 139 ms with plain AppKit, and 25.3 against 22.4 MB idle. About 60 ms and 3 MB, under the 100 ms bar set here, so the SwiftUI lifecycle stays. (The launch that matters is the Show Scratch shortcut into a running app, which S2 didn't measure; M1 does.)

**Global hotkeys: Carbon `RegisterEventHotKey`,** which needs no Accessibility permission, wrapped in about 100 lines of Rune's own rather than adding a dependency (T7). **Launch at login:** `SMAppService.mainApp`.

**CLI:** `rune` opens files through the running app (launching it if needed): it leaves a request file with a one-time token in Rune's support folder and says so with a distributed notification, and Rune answers with one. `-w` waits for a distributed notification carrying the token, posted when that file's window has closed and its text is in the file. Standard input travels in the request, and Rune makes an untitled buffer of it (built 2026-09-29, §8).

**Dependencies:** swift-tree-sitter (BSD-3-Clause; `tree-sitter/swift-tree-sitter`, formerly ChimeHQ's SwiftTreeSitter) and the grammars for HTML, CSS, JavaScript, JSON, Markdown, Bash, XML and HL8's list (MIT, but two: gitcommit's WTFPL, INI's Apache 2.0), Sparkle (MIT; DS2). All but HTML's, JSON's, Bash's and Markdown's are vendored in `NibKit/Vendor/` (CSS's and JavaScript's manifests drop the external scanner under SwiftPM's sandbox; most others name SwiftTreeSitter at its old address, a second package with the same module, or have no manifest; `NibKit/Vendor/README.md`). Everything else is Apple's SDK: CryptoKit for SHA-256, Compression for reading zip entries, Network for path monitoring.

**Entitlements:** Hardened Runtime; nothing else by default. iCloud Documents or key-value storage only if S1 shows they help and work with Developer ID signing (§4.2, §4.5).

### 6.4 Storage

| What | Where | Synced? |
|---|---|---|
| scratch.md and opened files | where they are | by iCloud, if they're in iCloud Drive |
| Recovery journal | `~/Library/Application Support/nib/Journal/` | no |
| History (every version opened, merged, received, restored or closed with, and the newest save of each 5 minutes; thinned as a file opens and once a day while it stays open: all for a day, hourly for a week, daily to 30 days) | `…/nib/History/<file>/` plus an index in JSON Lines | no |
| Shadow copies (last version seen of each synced file, for offline) | History's newest version of the file (no store of its own, §8 M2) | no |
| Recovery folder (when iCloud Drive is off) | `…/nib/Recovery/` | no |
| Sync Log | `…/nib/sync.jsonl` (T8) | no |
| Built-in scheme | An Old Hope · Rune, in the app bundle (from `themes/`) | no |
| Imported themes | `…/nib/Themes/` (converted to one internal format) | no |
| Receipts | iCloud key-value storage, or `.nib/devices/` beside the file | yes |
| Paused files | `…/nib/paused.json`: every file Rune has paused, settled at each launch (§4.9, 14a) | no |
| Lineage | extended attribute on the file (if S1b confirms it syncs; M0 showed it survives eviction on one Mac, which doesn't prove it reaches another) | yes |
| Preferences, caret and scroll per file | `UserDefaults` | no |

The test harness's Rune (a launch with `-NibScenario`: the suite, the two-Mac test, `run-perf.sh`) keeps all of this apart, in `~/Library/Application Support/nib-scenarios/` and the defaults domain `com.banastas.nib.scenarios` (`NibPaths.support`, `NibDefaults.store`), so a test run never touches the user's (§8, M2).

### 6.5 Performance targets

Baselined against Sublime Text on the same Mac in M0, in the manner of Blit's performance table.

| Measure | Target | Notes | Measured (2026-09-30, Mac mini) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Show Scratch shortcut to caret ready (app running) | ≤ 50 ms, p95 | The launch that matters: Rune is always running | **Met, Rune's side:** p50 13 ms, p95 25 ms, from the call to the window key with the caret in it, Rune in front. macOS's activation isn't in it: that needs a real key press, which a scenario can't make |
| Cold launch to editable scratch window | ≤ 300 ms, p95 | S2: 167-217 ms with SwiftUI and a 197 KB file once cached; 478 ms on the first launch after a build. Sublime baseline still to take | **Met on a quiet Mac, not while it's busy:** the suite's 34 launches (Debug, back to back, 17:13 on 2026-09-30) median 267 ms, p95 274, at most 282; ten launches while the Mac was in use (other apps busy) 312-393 ms, median 330 (optimized). The window is up at about 225 ms; the text comes about 100 ms later. Where it goes (warm Debug, from the process's start): Rune's code starts at 13 ms, fonts registered by 22, SwiftUI and AppKit set up until the scenes at 96, the scratch document and its editor made by 117, launching finished at 240; Rune's own setup then (shortcuts, the menu bar item) about 20 ms; AppKit's own work about 40 ms before the launch task runs; the open about 20 ms (iCloud's status 5-10 ms, reading 2-4, showing 5-7). Holding the file comes after, 1.0-1.8 s more, the text editable meanwhile. Tried: the setup queued after the open's start (it landed as iCloud answered and held the text back, 30 ms worse); handing back paused files beside the open (nothing to hand back: no gain). Left to try: reading the scratch file in the background while AppKit launches (about 40 ms) |
| Keystroke to glyph on screen | ≤ 1 frame (8.3 ms at 120 Hz), p99 ≤ 16 ms | S2 measured Rune's main-thread share only: p99 2 ms at 2.4 MB without highlighting, ≤ 10 ms with the stand-in highlighter. Glass-to-glass still needs Typometer, Sublime alongside | **Met for Rune's share:** a small file p50 1.4 ms, p99 3.1-6.7 ms; at 2.4 MB the slowest of a line's keystrokes 7.6-10.9 ms. Glass to glass still needs Typometer |
| Idle memory, a small scratch file, its window open or not | ≤ 45 MB physical footprint (was ≤ 40 MB with the window, ≤ 25 MB without; revised 2026-09-30, the user's call: below) | S2: 25.3 MB idle with SwiftUI and a 197 KB file. A bare SwiftUI app with one text view is 24.5 MB (2026-09-30), and the scratch file stays open with its window closed (SC1, so Show Scratch is instant, SC2), so a window-less target well under that can't be met. **Watch:** after 400 synthetic edits in two places of a 2.4 MB file, TextKit 2 had grown to 113 MB; M1 measures real use against this target | **Met with a small scratch file (43 MB against the revised 45 MB; the 40 MB before the revision it missed by 3 MB):** 43 MB, three launches alike (optimized; 2 KB test file). A bare SwiftUI app with one TextKit 2 text view is 24.5 MB on this Mac (macOS 27, measured the same day), so Rune's own share is about 18 MB, its highlighting 3 of it (40 MB as plain text). **With the scratch file at its real size, 2.4 MB of Markdown: 182-201 MB**, and growing slowly while idle (about 0.3 MB every 5 s, as plain text too; explained 2026-10-02: the scratch file's note went back and forth to iCloud every second or two, and the notes queue, never idle, kept what each read made. The Release in daily use grew 3 MB a minute that way, idle, to 219 MB with the 223 KB scratch file; fixed, §8 Disk writes). As plain text the same file is 64-70 MB: **the Markdown syntax tree is about 130 MB** (tree-sitter: 354,216 nodes a cursor sees, 58,903 named, more hidden; 4.95 MB of JavaScript makes a 175 MB tree, so whole-file trees cost 35-55 times the text). Nothing frees when the parser is reset or released: it's the tree |
| Idle memory, the scratch file at 2.4 MB | ≤ 100 MB (added 2026-09-30, the user's call) | The size scratch files reach before archiving (§2): the case that matters, where a small file's number doesn't show it | **Met:** 67.8 MB once settled, 77.6 MB in the first 40 s (optimized, the harness's 2.4 MB of Markdown viewed at its end; flat over 3 minutes); 64.5 MB as plain text. Was 182-201 MB, about 130 MB of it the syntax tree: big files now keep a tree of the stretch around the view only (§8) |
| Idle CPU and wakeups | 0.0%, no timer wakeups | A11; checked with `powermetrics` | **Met once settled:** 0.00-0.05% CPU after launch's own work tails off (5 s slices after the file is synced: 0.63%, 0.10, 0.25, 0.03, then 0.01 and below from 20 s on), about 0.5 wakeups a second, none from Rune's timers: over 20 s of `sample` nothing of Rune's ran, only AppKit's saving of window state and one spell-checker setup (the `idle` scenario, optimized; `powermetrics` needs sudo). The first `perf` measured during the tail-off (0.7-0.9%); it now waits for two quiet slices |
| Open a 5 MB JavaScript file | first paint ≤ 300 ms, fully highlighted ≤ 1 s | | **Met:** 4.95 MB (ED9 highlights up to 5,000,000 characters) shown in 120-156 ms, the text at the top colored at 242-247 ms (optimized, an untitled buffer; only the stretch in view is parsed; before the fixes of 2026-09-30, 1.3 and 1.9 s, then 658-743 ms) |
| Keystroke to journal | ≤ 300 ms | SY1 | **Met:** 235-260 ms; 259-262 ms in a 2.4 MB buffer, where it's an edit log (most of it the 250 ms wait for a pause) |
| Keystroke to file | ≤ 1 s after a pause, ≤ 5 s while typing | SY2 | **Met:** 1.0 s after a pause (the debounce), when nothing else holds the file queue (saves had waited seconds behind note reads until the 2026-09-29 fix, §8); ≤ 5 s by the schedule while typing |
| New version on disk to screen | ≤ 200 ms | §4.4 | **Met:** another app's coordinated write 7-12 ms; an uncoordinated one (`echo >>`) 75 ms |
| Scratch at 2.4 MB, 50,000 lines | opens ≤ 300 ms; typing latency as for a small file; a reload or merge ≤ 100 ms | The size scratch files reach before archiving (§2, Reference setup). S2: opens in 8-10 ms (only the visible part is laid out) | **Met:** shown in 69-98 ms (41,091 lines, untitled), the top colored at 171-183 ms, and in iCloud Drive at 207-226 ms; the end, jumped to, colored 481-529 ms later (the text read a chunk at a time for places to start); a reload 71-89 ms (in iCloud Drive, timed to the millisecond); a three-way merge 25-27 ms; typing: the slowest of a line's keystrokes 6.5-10.1 ms on the main thread, a heading typed at the end colored 18 ms after typing stops (optimized; before the fixes of 2026-09-30: shown 650-690 ms, the top colored at 545-549 ms, reload 303 ms in Debug and then 154-167 ms timed exactly, merge 117 ms) |
| Save on one Mac to load on the other, iCloud only | measured: p50 and p95 | The input to the V2 decision (§4.10) | **Round trip only so far:** 15 and 18 s from this Mac's upload to the other Mac's receipt coming back (the two-Mac test at 18:30, 2026-09-29); p50 and p95 need more runs (the Sync Log's `receipt` lines collect them) |

How: `scripts/run-perf.sh` builds the app optimized (-O Swift, -Os C, as a release) with the scenarios in it, runs `perf` (idle memory, CPU and wakeups; Show Scratch's side; disk to screen; keystrokes; a 5 MB JavaScript file and 2.4 MB of Markdown as untitled buffers; a merge at 2.4 MB) and ten cold launches; `bigfile` measures the reload in iCloud Drive; every scenario's first log line gives its launch, editable and held times.

### 6.6 Testing

As Blit (T9): `swift test` for NibKit, plus debug-only harnesses in the app driven by launch arguments.

- **Merge:** property tests (random edits on two sides of a random base: output is deterministic, and every inserted line appears either merged or inside markers); a corpus of real prose conflicts.
- **Themes:** golden tests for An Old Hope · Rune and the original (`.tmTheme`), including a contrast test that fails if any text color in the built-in scheme drops under 4.5:1, and a `.sublime-color-scheme` exercising variables, `color()` adjusters and exclusions; scope-ranking tests.
- **Fidelity harness (S3):** a small Sublime plugin dumps every token's scope and color for the reference files (`view.extract_tokens_with_scopes` and `view.style_for_scope`); Rune renders the same files and compares characters. This runs on every change to the scope map.
- **Crash recovery:** a harness types continuously, is killed with `kill -9` at random moments, relaunches, and checks that at most the last 300 ms is missing.
- **iCloud simulation:** a `CloudStatusProvider` protocol faked in tests, to inject conflict versions, `name 2` duplicates, dataless files, upload errors and deletions.
- **Two-Mac probe:** `scripts/sync-probe.sh` (in the style of Blit's `sftp-probe.sh`) writes numbered versions on one Mac and logs arrival times on the other.
- **Paste corpus:** saved clipboards from Safari, Mail, Slack, Notes, Word and Preview (PDF), each with a known clean result.
- **Render harness:** Rune's pixel art (the menu bar item and panel, the welcome window) rendered to PNGs beside the scenario logs, to compare.

Before 1.0, a QA and UX review in the format of Blit's (`qa/blit-qa-ux-review.md`), weighted toward sync and data loss.

---

## 7. Code shared with Blit

**Decided 2026-09-29 (the user): no shared package.** Each app's code lives entirely in its own repository. Either may read the other's code and copy or adapt what it needs; a copy's header names where it came from (the repository, the file and the commit), and from then on it's that app's own code, changed as that app needs. Nothing keeps the copies in step: when one app fixes a piece the other copied, the other decides whether to take the fix. (The earlier plan, a `FamilyKit` package in its own repository with `PixelUI` and `AppShell` libraries, Blit switched to it first and then Rune, is dropped; so is open question 20.)

- **Rune's copies from Blit:** `nib/Vendor/FromBlit/` (the HUD's colors and fonts, the pixel glyphs, the pixel-art renderer and the fixed-width menu bar image, the command palette, generalized, and emptying the Trash, `TrashService`) and `scripts/make-icon.swift` (Blit's script with Rune's art and name). The rest of Blit's inventory ([Reusable pieces](#reusable-pieces-from-blit)) is copied when Rune needs it.
- **What Blit may want from Rune:** the global shortcuts (`nib/Model/HotKey.swift`: Carbon hot keys, changeable, with the warnings), launch at login (`nib/Model/LoginItem.swift`), the menu bar item made with AppKit's `NSStatusItem` (`nib/Views/MenuBar.swift`; SwiftUI's `MenuBarExtra` on macOS 27 ignores being turned off and shows only its label's first image, §8), and Reduce Motion (A16) once Rune honors it.

## 8. Milestones

Blit's pattern (T3): measure first, give each milestone a "done when", and write the results back here with dates.

**Safety rule while building:** until M1's "done when" has passed on both Macs, no build of Nib and no spike opens the live `_scratch2.md`. They work in `iCloud Drive/nib-spike/`, on generated files. *Lifted 2026-10-06 (the user's call), but for the test harness's Nib: M2 progress, "The safety rule lifted".*

| | Milestone | Scope | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M0** | **Spikes** (an evening or two, before the weekend) | **S1 · iCloud probe** (one-Mac half done 2026-09-27, [results](#m0-results); two-Mac half is S1b): save-to-load latency (p50, p95) for small edits and for continuous typing; reproduce on purpose: simultaneous offline edits (conflict versions, or `name 2` files?), sleep before upload, eviction (`brctl evict`) and reading a dataless file, deletion and rename; whether a `#S` extended attribute syncs; whether `NSMetadataQuery` sees files outside Nib's container; the privacy prompt for iCloud Drive; whether iCloud Documents and key-value storage entitlements work with Developer ID signing; whether macOS 27 can keep one file downloaded. **S2 · Text view** (done 2026-09-27, [results](#m0-results)): TextKit 1 vs TextKit 2 at 197 KB and 2.4 MB: open time, typing cost, scrolling, memory; SwiftUI vs AppKit launch. **S3 · Fidelity:** the Sublime dump plugin, An Old Hope · Nib installed in Sublime, six reference files; tree-sitter plus a first scope map. Sublime baselines for §6.5. | Each question has a written answer here. §4.2 and §6.3's open choices are decided. Tree-sitter reaches ≥ 90% on S3 with a first-pass map (≥ 98% is M2's bar) |
| **M1** | **Weekend MVP** (in progress; one-Mac checks passed 2026-09-27, two-Mac checks 2026-09-28, [progress](#m1-progress)) | All P0: scratch window and Show Scratch shortcut; plain paste and plain copy; `NSTextView` editor with native find; journal; autosave with every trigger; compare-and-swap saves; never writing a clean buffer; reload when clean; the Keep Mine / Keep Theirs / Keep Both safety net; the status strip with sync states (vendored pixel pieces); dark appearance and An Old Hope · Nib's editor colors; nothing on the main thread; stays running with no windows. Installed on both Macs with `release.sh`. | Five scripted scenarios on two Macs lose nothing: stale typing, lid closed before upload, both offline, `kill -9` mid-typing, another app writing the file. Rich text pasted from five sources comes out plain. Scratch has been used on both Macs for a day |
| **M2** | **Daily driver** | All P1: files, tabs, CLI; tree-sitter highlighting for the six languages; An Old Hope · Nib's rules, theme import (`.tmTheme` and `.sublime-color-scheme`), Import from Sublime, the import report; Sublime keys; find and replace with regex; multiple carets if kept (open question 10); three-way merge, conflict blocks and merge view; duplicates, conflict versions, eviction, deletion, iCloud off, errors; History and Sync Log; receipts; Clean Clipboard, Paste Clean, Copy Block, Append to Scratch; menu bar extra; launch at login; first run; VoiceOver; Reduce Motion. | Fidelity ≥ 98%. §6.5 targets met or their gap written down. Sublime not opened for 14 consecutive days (§9) |
| **M3** | **Polish** | All P2: sync HUD with CRT; pixel app icon and menu bar art; About; Dock ✕ badge; Services and Shortcuts; hide the Dock icon; performance tuning; QA and UX review; Sparkle, `nib.banast.as`, licensing and the notices (DS2, DS3). | Notarized release on both Macs; an update verified end to end through Sparkle, as Blit's 0.2.5 → 0.2.6 was |
| **V2** | **Faster sync** (conditional) | The express lane from §4.10, LAN or CloudKit per open question 2; Handoff | The decision rule in §4.10 is met. After: save-to-load p95 under 2 s when both Macs are awake |

### M0 results

**S1, one Mac (2026-09-27).** `spike/` (`nib-spike`, Blit's spike layout), run on this Mac (MacBook Air, M4, macOS 27.0) against generated files in `iCloud Drive/nib-spike/`. Raw output is in `spike/results/`; `spike/run-s1.sh` repeats it.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| Plain save, 200 KB: time until iCloud reports it uploaded | 2.7-4.1 s; the upload starts within about 220 ms |
| Saves every second (continuous typing), iCloud on its own | **Never uploaded while typing**: each save restarted the upload; the last one finished 6.1 s after typing stopped |
| Does iCloud Drive offer the macOS 26 sync controls for a plain file? | **Yes:** `pauseSync` and `failUploadOnConflict`, no entitlement needed |
| …from a command-line binary? | No: "feature not supported". From an app bundle (ad-hoc signed): yes |
| Pause, then save by renaming a temp file over the original (atomic write, or `replaceItemAt`) | The rename makes a new file (new inode): the pause is lost, iCloud uploads on its own, and "upload now" and "resume" report "not supported". File presenters see the rename as a **deletion** |
| Pause, then save in place (truncate, write, fsync) | Same inode, still paused, iCloud uploads nothing by itself |
| Upload now with fail-on-conflict, while paused | Works: 2.2 s for 200 KB, returns the uploaded version |
| Paused, saving every second, uploading at most every 3 s | Every upload succeeded, 2.2-2.9 s each (200 KB) and 2.4-3.0 s (2.4 MB): the server stays within about 5 s of the keyboard |
| Fetch latest remote version | 3-11 ms when nothing is newer (whether it beats push for another Mac's version: S1b) |
| Pause right after an upload | Once answered "resource busy" (EBUSY): needs a retry |
| Resume | 0.4-1.0 s; state returns to uploaded |
| `isUploaded` while paused | Stays false even after a successful upload: for a held file, the upload call's result is the truth |
| iCloud's error fields | Every ordinary upload briefly reports 4355 "servers might be unreachable" while succeeding; paused files report "Sync is paused". Noise, not problems |
| Push notifications for sync state | Yes: the presenter's `presentedItemDidChangeUbiquityAttributes` fires as uploading and uploaded change. No polling needed |
| Uncoordinated write from another process (`>>` append) | The presenter hears about it about 1.1 s later; a folder watch doesn't (no entry changes) |
| Evicted file | Dataless (0 blocks, `SF_DATALESS`, size kept). A coordinated read blocks 1.1 s while it downloads; an explicit download takes 0.45 s. Never read on the main thread |
| Extended attributes (`…lineage#S` and a plain one) through eviction | Both survive on this Mac. That doesn't show they reach another Mac: S1b |
| `NSMetadataQuery` without an iCloud entitlement | No results in any scope |
| An API to keep one file downloaded | None in the macOS 27 SDK |

What changed in this PRD because of it: the save pipeline now holds the file with a sync pause, saves in place, and uploads with fail-on-conflict (§4.3); the Mac you switch to fetches on activation (§4.4); sync state is pushed, not polled, and iCloud's error noise is ignored (§4.5); the server's refusal is the main conflict detector (§4.6); new failure cases for pauses left behind (§4.9, 14a-14d); option (a) for the file's location is decided (§4.2); V2 looks less necessary (§4.10).

**S1b, two Macs, first run (2026-09-27, 23:10).** `spike/run-s1b.sh` on this MacBook Air (Mac A, writing) and the Mac mini (Mac B, reading), both awake and online; 16 versions per phase, 5 s apart; latency is from the write on Mac A to the new text being readable on Mac B (clocks via NTP).

| Phase | p50 | p95 | Max | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Plain iCloud (atomic writes, iCloud's own schedule) | 3.6 s | 6.2 s | 6.2 s | 16 of 16 arrived |
| …and Mac B fetches every 5 s | 3.4 s | 6.8 s | 6.8 s | 15 of 16: one version was skipped. Fetching doesn't speed up an unpaused file |
| **Nib's pipeline on Mac A: held, saved in place, upload now** | **2.5 s** | **3.6 s** | 3.6 s | 16 of 16; upload-now took 2.1 s median of that |
| …and Mac B fetches every 5 s | 2.8 s | 5.2 s | 5.2 s | Fetching still doesn't help |

What it means:

1. **iCloud on its own is far better than the PRD assumed** ("seconds to minutes"): a few seconds when both Macs are awake. The failure the PRD is built around is still real, but it comes from edits that never leave Mac A (unsaved, or a lid closed before the upload), not from slow delivery.
2. **Nib's upload-now shaves about 1 s at the median and 2.6 s at p95, and makes delivery predictable.** Once the upload returns, iCloud gets the version to the other Mac in well under a second. The upload is the bottleneck.
3. **Fetching adds nothing for a file that isn't paused.** For a paused one (Nib's scratch file on Mac B), it may be the only way in; that's what the paused-file test answers.
4. **New files from another Mac can arrive as placeholders** that download when read (the Mac mini behaves this way, as Optimize Mac Storage does). The readers above read every 100 ms, which downloaded each version; so the latencies include that download. The probe's "wait for the file" step didn't read, so the paused-file, conflict and attribute tests reported "never arrived" and have to be run again (`run-s1b.sh a|b HH:MM part2`, fixed). A later run on the old probe confirmed it: a file that had been on the server for 7 minutes still wasn't downloaded on the Mac mini, because nothing had read it.

**S1b, second run (2026-09-28, 07:35).** The paused-file, conflict and attribute tests, with the fixed probe on both Macs.

| Test | Result |
|---|---|
| **Server refusal** (both Macs pause the same file; Mac A saves and uploads first; Mac B saves from the old version and uploads 10 s later) | **Works.** Mac A's upload accepted; Mac B's refused as a conflict (`-2015`); Mac B fetched the newer version (it came to a side location), put it in place with `replaceItem` (the file **stayed paused**), merged its line in, and the merged upload was **accepted** |
| **Lineage attribute** (`com.banastas.nib.lineage#S`, set on Mac A) | **Arrived** on Mac B with Mac A's value. The same attribute without `#S` didn't. §4.6's exact base for a three-way merge is available |
| Paused file on Mac B while Mac A uploads every 10 s | Mac B's local file didn't change while paused (iCloud held off, as intended); the presenter was called 3 times and the iCloud state changed twice; a fetch at the end failed with `GSLibraryErrorDomain 3` ("Generation not found"). What the callbacks were is still to read from Mac B's log |
| **After resuming** (both Macs, 30 s after the merge, with `.preserveLocalChanges`) | **Mac A's copy went stale and stayed stale.** 26 minutes later Mac A still held its own two lines while Mac B held all three; on Mac A iCloud called the file current and uploaded, a fetch returned Mac A's own version in 7 ms, and there was no conflict copy. Evicting and re-downloading on Mac A brought the three-line version: **the server had the merge all along.** The newer version reached the server while Mac A had the file paused, and after resuming Mac A never picked it up |

What it means:

1. **No text was lost, and a stale copy can't overwrite the newer one:** the server's refusal (row one) turns typing on a stale copy into a merge. Guarantees 1 and 2 hold.
2. **But a file Nib has held can show old text indefinitely, while iCloud calls it current,** and `fetchLatestRemoteVersionOfItem` doesn't catch it (it answered in 7 ms, from local state). Guarantee 3 (honest freshness) needs Nib's own check: delivery receipts in a separate small file that isn't paused (SY14 moves from nice-to-have to required), and a forced refresh (evict and re-download: 1.3 s here) when another Mac reports a version this Mac doesn't have. To test in S1b's next run.
3. **Resume mode:** `.preserveLocalChanges` is what both Macs used, and Apple documents it "may automatically pick the winner". Nib no longer uses it: a file whose every save is uploaded is resumed with `.dropLocalChanges` (take the server's version, which should also bring down one that arrived while paused; iCloud keeps any local change as an alternate version), and one with unsent saves with `.afterUploadWithFailOnConflict`, where a refusal takes the server's version and puts this Mac's text in front of the user through the journal and the conflict sheet (`CloudControl.ResumeMode`, `Document.close`, `hold`, `settlePausedFiles`). The M1 suite passes with it.

**S1b, third run (2026-09-28, 08:05), and a follow-up on Mac A:** the same race, with the new resume modes. Mac A resumed with `.afterUploadWithFailOnConflict`: accepted, no conflict, and its copy stayed stale again (two lines, "current", "uploaded"). Then, on Mac A: pausing and fetching said "nothing newer" in 1 ms; resuming with `.dropLocalChanges` returned ok and left the file unchanged after 15 s. Evicting and re-downloading brought the three-line version in 0.6 s. **Confirmed: a version that reaches the server while a Mac has the file paused is never applied on that Mac afterwards, and none of fetch, either resume mode or the downloading status reveal it.** Files that aren't paused don't have the problem (the first run's readers got every version).

**What Nib does about it (design change, §4.4 and SY14):**

- **An announcement that isn't paused.** Every upload also rewrites a tiny `.nib/latest-<file>.json` beside the file (version SHA-256, lineage parent, Mac, time). No Mac ever pauses it, so it travels like the first run's unpaused files, in about 3-6 s, and its presenter fires on arrival.
- **Trust the announcement over iCloud's "current".** When a Mac sees an announced version it doesn't have: with no unsaved edits, it refreshes (resume taking the server's version, evict, download, pause again: about 1-2 s) and reloads; with edits, it does nothing special, because its next upload is refused and the merge runs (§4.7).
- **Delivery receipts (SY14) move from P1 to P0,** as this announcement; the per-Mac "on Studio" receipts build on it in M2.
- Whether a paused file gives any signal of its own: the 08:05 run repeated 07:35 exactly (presenter called 3 times, state changed twice, file unchanged, fetch failed with "Generation not found"); Mac A's copy, meanwhile, said "current" for 26 minutes while stale. Not a signal to rely on; the note is.

**Built (2026-09-28):** `NibSync/Announcement.swift` (the note, and `CloudControl.refetch`: resume taking the server's version, evict with a retry while iCloud is busy, download, return the bytes) and, in `Document`, writing the note after every upload, a presenter on the `.nib` folder (a presenter isn't told when a file it presents is first created, so it watches the folder), and checks on the note's arrival, on coming forward, every 30 s in front, on wake and on the network's return. One Mac, with a note from a pretend other Mac: noticed within 1 s, refreshed in 2.8 s (Nib's own file presenter has to step aside during the eviction, which otherwise answers "busy"), held again, text intact. `scripts/run-m1-two-mac.sh` checks it for real on two Macs.

**S1b, still to do:**

1. ~~Latency, iCloud on its own~~ (above).
2. ~~Latency, Nib's pipeline~~ (above).
3. **Does a paused file hear about a newer server version** (presenter or `downloadingStatus` turning stale), or only when fetched? Partly answered above; the callbacks' details, and why the fetch failed, are still open.
4. ~~Server refusal~~ (works, above). Still to do: the same race with the new resume modes, to show the merged version survives on both Macs.
5. ~~Lineage~~ (arrives, above).
6. **Lid closed right after a save:** how long until the other Mac sees it after the lid opens?

**S2, text view and lifecycle, one Mac (2026-09-27).** `spike/Sources/nib-textspike` puts an `NSTextView` with either engine in a real window (on screen at 1% opacity, never key), loads generated scratch-shaped Markdown (short lines, code fences, and every 25th line a long email paragraph that wraps), inserts 300 keystrokes at the end and 300 in the middle (every 17th a newline, every 23rd a delete), displays synchronously after each, and times it. "Highlight" is a regular-expression stand-in re-applied to ±3,000 characters around each edit, as rendering attributes; tree-sitter is S3. Times are Nib's main-thread share of a keystroke, not glass to glass. `nib-launch-appkit` and `nib-launch-swiftui` time process start to first window, 8 runs each. Raw output in `spike/results/s2-*`.

| | TextKit 1 | TextKit 2 |
|---|---|---|
| Open 197 KB / 2.4 MB (set text, first display) | 14.8 / 12.3 ms | 8.3 / 8.1 ms |
| Keystroke, 2.4 MB, no highlighting (end · middle), p99 | 21.4 · 20.1 ms | 2.0 · 2.0 ms |
| Keystroke, 2.4 MB, stand-in highlighting (end · middle), p99 | 16.5 · 18.5 ms | 3.5 · 10.0 ms |
| Keystroke, 197 KB, stand-in highlighting (end · middle), p99 | 16.2 · 23.2 ms | 3.5 · 6.9 ms |
| Scroll jump to a random place, p95 (2.4 MB) | 15.3 ms | 12.1 ms |
| Estimated document height across 40 jumps (2.4 MB) | varied by 27,380 pt | **varied by 174,704 pt** |
| …after laying out the whole document | exact (311 ms to lay out) | still varied (232,178 pt), and mid-document keystrokes rose to 41 ms p50 |
| Memory after opening 2.4 MB, no highlighting | 47.6 MB | 31.5 MB |
| …with the whole document highlighted | 146 MB | 46 MB |
| Applying highlighting to the whole 2.4 MB document | 498 ms | 308 ms |

| Lifecycle (first window, 197 KB file, TextKit 2) | Median | Range | Idle memory |
|---|---|---|---|
| Plain AppKit | 139 ms | 124-166 ms | 22.4 MB |
| SwiftUI `App` | 201 ms | 167-217 ms (478 ms on the first launch after building) | 25.3 MB |

Decided: TextKit 2, the SwiftUI lifecycle, and viewport-only highlighting (§6.3). Open: whether TextKit 2's shifting scroll bar is acceptable in real use (M1 will show it at once), and why memory grows while typing in a large file (§6.5).

**S3:** not started.

### M1 progress

**Built (2026-09-27), against the test file `iCloud Drive/nib-spike/scratch-test.md` only (the §8 safety rule is enforced in code: any other path is refused until `allowLiveScratch` is set):**

- `NibKit` (Swift package, 28 tests): `NibText` (encodings, BOM, line endings, fingerprints, Keep Both conflict text, paste cleanup) and `NibSync` (journal, in-place compare-and-swap saves, iCloud sync controls, paused-files list, Sync Log, save schedule).
- `nib.xcodeproj`, hand-written with a folder-synced group like Blit's, `com.banastas.nib`, SwiftUI lifecycle, dark only (A17). Blit's HUD tokens, fonts and pixel glyphs are copied into `nib/Vendor/FromBlit/` with their source commit (§7).
- P0 in place: the scratch window (SC1) and ⌥Space to show or hide it (SC2); caret per Mac (SC3); plain paste and copy (PT1, PT2); a TextKit 2 editor with native find, undo and An Old Hope's colors and current-line highlight (ED1, HL6, HL10); the journal (SY1); saves 1 s after typing pauses, 5 s at most, and at once on window or app switch, sleep, screens off, lock, user switch, ⌘S and quit (SY2); compare-and-swap in place (SY3); no write without an edit (SY4); reload when clean (SY5); the Keep Mine / Keep Theirs / Keep Both sheet (SY6); the status strip (SY7); no file work on the main thread (SY8); the pause, upload-now and fetch pipeline (§4.3, §4.4); staying open with no windows (AP1).
- `scripts/run-m1-scenarios.sh` builds everything and runs the debug harness's scenarios (`nib/Model/TestHarness.swift`).

**One-Mac results** (all 23 scenario checks and 28 unit tests pass; times from the last keystroke):

| Scenario | Result |
|---|---|
| Type, then wait | Journal 245 ms · file 1,002 ms · iCloud has it 4.0-4.5 s (1 s wait + an upload of about 3 s). The file holds exactly the buffer |
| Paste rich text (RTF, bold, red, CRLF) | Plain text with LF, the editor's font; copying writes plain text only |
| Undo, redo | Both work, and an undo is saved and synced like any edit |
| `kill -9` 600 ms after typing, saving held off | The journal had the typing, the file didn't; the next launch took the file back (resume, pause), restored the typing, said so in the strip, saved and synced it |
| Another app appends to the file, no edits in Nib | Reloaded, "▼ UPDATED FROM ANOTHER APP", and uploaded (a held file doesn't upload on its own) |
| …with unsaved edits in Nib | Conflict sheet; the file keeps the other app's version; Keep Both writes both between markers and syncs |
| Quit | Saves, uploads, resumes iCloud's syncing in 0.6-1.2 s; nothing left on the paused list |

**Found while building, and fixed:**

- **A pause belongs to the bundle ID** (it's recorded on the file as `com.apple.file-provider-sync_paused_bundle_id#PX`); **pausing an already-paused file answers "not supported"**, contrary to the documentation. The first build treated that as "can't pause" and ran unheld, the worst case: a paused file that nothing uploads. Now a file left paused is resumed, then paused fresh (§4.3).
- **Quit asked for from inside a main-queue job hung** until a watchdog that was itself queued behind it; AppKit waits for the reply in a mode where the main queue doesn't drain. A menu's Quit doesn't hit this, but the watchdog is now a run-loop timer that fires regardless, so quitting always ends within 10 s.
- **Inside SwiftUI's window the text view had no undo manager.** The editor now owns one, which also keeps the undo history when the window is closed and reopened.

**Second Mac (2026-09-27, 23:53):** `scripts/run-m1-scenarios.sh` passed on the Mac mini too: all 23 scenario checks and 28 unit tests.

**Two-Mac test, first run (2026-09-28, 09:25; `scripts/run-m1-two-mac.sh`):** both Macs had the test file open and held. Mac A added a line; then both added a line at the same moment.

- **Found a data-loss bug, now fixed.** Mac A saved its clash line and uploaded it; the upload was refused (Mac B's landed first); Mac A fetched Mac B's version and, because its buffer matched its saved file, treated itself as clean and **replaced the buffer with Mac B's version**, dropping its own line. Both Macs converged on the same bytes (the same SHA-256), with one line missing and nothing to say so. The rule now: a buffer may be replaced only when nothing on this Mac is unsent: no unsaved edits, and for a held file, its saved version is the one iCloud has (`Document.unsent`). Anything else goes to the conflict sheet. A single-Mac regression scenario (`unsent`, uploads held back) reproduces it and passes.
- **Another app's write went unnoticed** while a saved version waited to upload (found by that regression): iCloud's presenter stayed quiet. Nib now also watches the file and the `.nib` folder directly (file-system events), as §4.4 planned for writers that don't coordinate; the watch steps aside during a refresh, since eviction refuses a file in use.
- **Mac B showed Mac A's line one second after coming forward** (the activation check and a refresh). Whether it arrives by itself, without coming forward, wasn't really tested: the harness's wait ran before Mac A typed. Fixed for the next run.

**Two-Mac test, second run (10:00):** the fix holds on the Mac that merged. Mac A's upload was refused, it raised the conflict instead of replacing its buffer, Keep Both put both lines in the file, and it synced. **But the Macs didn't converge:** Mac B stayed with only its own line (it had uploaded first), a different SHA-256 from Mac A's, although the server had the merge. Mac B also saw Mac A's lead line only after coming forward (15.9 s after it was added), not by itself. The cause matches S1b: on Mac B, reading a file can return the local copy while the server has a newer one, and that goes for the note too; checks found nothing new. **Change:** every note check now asks the server (it evicts the note and reads it back, about a second; the note is tiny and never paused), on coming forward, window focus, wake, network return and every 30 s in front. The test now counts "arrived by itself" as information (an idle Mac depends on iCloud pushing to it) and checks the promise instead: coming forward shows the newest version, and both Macs converge after you look at each.

**Two-Mac test, third run (10:40):** Mac A's lead line reached the idle Mac B by itself this time (about 20 s after it was added; Mac B hadn't come forward yet). The clash found a new failure: both Macs uploaded within about 2 s of each other; Mac A's was accepted, and **Mac B's upload call never answered** (still "uploading" 35 s later). Nib had no time limit, so everything waiting behind that call (the next upload, the fetch, the note check) stopped, Mac B never got its refusal, and the Macs ended on different versions. Nothing was lost: each Mac had its own line, and the server Mac A's. **Change:** every sync-control call has a time limit (upload and fetch 15 s, pause and resume 20 s; `CloudControl.within`, which doesn't wait for the abandoned call, since iCloud's calls can't be cancelled). No answer: Nib says "ICLOUD ISN'T ANSWERING · SAVED LOCALLY · RETRYING" and retries in 2 s; the retry is refused or accepted at once. The test's timeline now leaves room for that before the final check.

**Two-Mac test, fourth run (11:15):** **Mac B passed everything:** Mac A's line arrived by itself (about 20 s after it was added), its clash upload was refused, it merged (Keep Both), uploaded and announced. **Mac A saw the announcement 5 s later**, but its refresh failed: iCloud answered "cannot be evicted" (`-2008`), so Mac A kept the old copy while the server had the merge (checked by evicting by hand afterwards: 0.8 s). Reproduced on one Mac: intermittent, right after an upload. **Change:** the eviction retries with backoff on `-2008` and `-2007` as it did on EBUSY (about 15 s in all); four refreshes in a row then passed, in 2-5 s. iCloud's version history (`getNonlocalVersionsOfItem`) was tried as a way round eviction and doesn't help: it listed only this Mac's versions. The two-Mac runner now also starts both Macs with an empty journal.

**Two-Mac test, fifth run (11:45): passed on both Macs.** Mac A added a line; the idle Mac B showed it by itself about 20 s later (coming forward shows it at once). Both then added a line at the same moment: Mac B's upload was refused, it merged (Keep Both), and both Macs ended with both lines, the file equal to the buffer, synced, and **the same SHA-256** (`1486719e…`). Mac A got the merge from a fetch when it came forward this time, not from the note: both paths now lead to the newest version.

What the five runs fixed, in order: a saved-but-unsent version replaced by a fetched one (silent loss); another app's write unnoticed while a save waited to upload; the note read from a stale local copy; an upload call that never answered stalling everything behind it; eviction refused right after an upload. Each is covered by the single-Mac suite or the two-Mac runner now.

**Team-signed, single Mac (13:02, Mac mini): passed**, in `iCloud Drive/Nib/Test/`, scenarios in about 65 s. The first run found a harness fault, not a Nib one: in "unsent" the harness asked Nib to quit while the Keep Both sheet was still going away. AppKit ignores `terminate(_:)` while a sheet is attached, so Nib stayed open until the runner's guard killed it 120 s later, with the file still paused (the next scenario handed it back). The harness now waits for the sheet to go before quitting; a quit asked for a few seconds later was never affected. The same suite then passed team-signed on the MacBook Air.

**Two-Mac test, sixth run (13:22, team-signed, `iCloud Drive/Nib/Test/`, Mac B = Mac mini): Mac B's checks passed, its file didn't.** A's line arrived by itself 11 s after it was added; the clash merged; the file held the buffer; synced. Mac A (MacBook Air) got its clash upload in first, saw no sheet, and ended with the same SHA-256 as B (`5b063b5d…`): the Macs agreed, on the wrong text. B had merged three times, nested: "clash from a" three times in the file, "clash from b" twice. The check only asked that both lines were there. What happened on B: its upload was refused and a fetch brought A's version down. The file watch, which a fetch is meant to silence, read the download too: a second fetch that overlapped it (coming forward, or every 30 s) had ended and cleared the flag. So one version came in twice (two sheets). Resolving the second set the base back to A's version while B's first merged save was already on disk, so B's next save was refused and merged with B's own save (the third). **Change:** the flag is a count of running fetches; a version that is the buffer's base, or the one the sheet is about, isn't news; the check wants each clash line exactly once. Still possible, for M2's three-way merge: a genuinely new version arriving while a save is on its way to disk.

**Two-Mac test, seventh run (13:35, team-signed, `iCloud Drive/Nib/Test/`): passed on both Macs.** A's line reached the idle Mac B by itself 12 s after it was added (coming forward shows it at once). The clash: A's upload got in first; B's was refused and B merged once (one sheet, Keep Both). Both Macs ended with each clash line once, the file equal to the buffer, synced, and **the same SHA-256** (`b489595d…`).
