The file watch still delivered the download before the fetch did (it had started first); the fetch's copy then matched the sheet's version and was dropped, so both halves of the change were needed. Noted, not fixed: B's saves took 5-7 s while iCloud was busy with the file (milliseconds otherwise), and a fetch of B's own upload is logged as "saved by another Mac".

**Still to do for M1:**

- **Lid closed before the upload** (by hand): type a line on the laptop and close the lid within a second; on the other Mac, check the line is absent, then open the lid and check it arrives (and, if the other Mac typed meanwhile, that the merge comes up instead of an overwrite).
- **Both Macs offline** (by hand): Wi-Fi off on both, type a different line on each, Wi-Fi back on; expect one conflict sheet, Keep Both, both Macs converging.
- **By hand:** ⌥Space to show and hide, the strip's states, ⌘W and reopening.
- ~~**Nib's own iCloud folder**~~ (§4.2, decided 2026-09-28): **done 2026-09-28.** The app ID, the container and both Macs registered to the team (a Mac new to the team needs `-allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration`, once; the Air's old development certificate, its private key gone, was revoked and Xcode made a new one). The single-Mac suite and the two-Mac test (seventh run) pass team-signed in `iCloud Drive/Nib/Test/`.
- **A day on both Macs with the test file**, then move `_scratch2.md` to `iCloud Drive/Nib/scratch.md` and set `allowLiveScratch`.

The by-hand checks above were skipped on 2026-09-28 by the user's choice, to move on to M2.

### M2 progress

**Three-way merge (2026-09-28), §4.6-4.8 flow A, and Keep Both for B.**

- **Engine:** `NibText.Merge.threeWay`, the classic diff3 walk over lines (Swift's `difference(from:)` for each side). Where both sides changed the same stretch, it's retried over words (words, runs of spaces, line breaks). If that overlaps too and both sides edited the stretch in place (as many lines each), it's merged line by line, so only the lines both changed differently go between markers. Where both added text at the same place and one addition carries the other's, the longer is taken. Sides ordered by label (SY10). 15 tests, including a seeded fuzz: in 2,000 random pairs of edits, no line either side added is lost, and the order of the sides never changes the bytes.
- **The base has to be proven, not guessed.** Nib merges only when the incoming version's note names as its parent the version this Mac last had on the server (`uploadedSHA`), and Nib still has that version's text (the last eight versions it loaded, saved or received are kept). That version is always where this Mac's buffer builds from too, so it's a common ancestor. A write by another app, a missing or stale note, or the first minutes after opening before anything is uploaded: the two-way sheet, as in M1. A guessed base can undo edits without a trace. The source label can't decide either: the two-Mac runs showed iCloud's own download arriving through the file watch as "another app".
- **Clean:** applied as one undo step ("Merge from Air", its own undo group: AppKit's automatic ones close with an event, and a merge doesn't come with one), `✓ MERGED 1 CHANGE FROM AIR · ⌘Z UNDOES` for 10 s, a `merge` entry in the Sync Log, then the normal save and upload. **Overlapping:** the sheet as before, but Keep Both writes the three-way text, and the sheet says how many places overlap. A note that arrives after its version, while the sheet is up, merges it then.
- **Checks:** new single-Mac scenario `merge`. The two-Mac test has a second phase: both Macs type at the same moment in different places, and the refused Mac must merge without a sheet. The harness now waits 5 s before answering a sheet, as a person would. Single-Mac suite passes (50 NibKit tests, 9 scenarios).
- **Two-Mac test, eighth run (14:20, team-signed): passed on both Macs, the same SHA-256** (`ca2a341a…`). The clash: B's upload refused, one sheet, Keep Both three-way, each line once. **The apart phase: B's upload refused, and B merged A's line by itself** ("clean: 1 change from Bill's MacBook Air onto this Mac's edits"), no sheet on either Mac, a's line first in the file and b's last. The Air saw no sheet in either phase: its uploads got in first, and it took B's merges as they came.
- **Found in that run's Sync Log, not by its checks:** just after B uploaded its merge, the 30-second fetch was offered the Air's previous version, the one B had just merged, and B, with nothing unsent, put it in place: for 24 s B showed the old text without its own line, until the next fetch brought the merge back. Typing then would have raised a confusing sheet (nothing lost). **Change:** within 30 s of its own upload, a fetch that offers a version this Mac has moved past (an earlier base) is skipped, not put in place, and logged; later it's taken as before, and another Mac going back to an old version comes with its note regardless. Every time a clean buffer is replaced, the Sync Log now says so (`update`). Conflict blocks keep lines both sides share at their edges outside the markers (the blank line both Macs typed before theirs). Single-Mac suite passes after these; the two-Mac test not yet run again.
**Conflict blocks and the banner (2026-09-28), §5.4 and §4.8 B, replacing M1's sheet.**

- **No dialog.** When edits overlap, or no base is proven, both versions go into the text at once, between markers (three-way when the base is proven: only the places both changed; otherwise the two whole versions), as one undo step ("Conflict with Air"), then the normal save and upload: the conflict is in the file, syncs to every Mac, and typing goes on. The journal's flow G (typing from before a crash and a newer file) writes blocks the same way. M1's pending-conflict state, the sheet, and the strip's conflict state are gone; saving, uploading and quitting no longer wait on anything.
- **Blocks are found in the text** (`ConflictText.blocks`, NibText): Nib's and git's markers, git's `|||||||` base section included, whatever wrote them; a start with no end is left as text. 7 tests. The buffer is rescanned after each change (only when a marker is there at all).
- **The banner** above the text while it holds blocks: the red `✕ CONFLICT` tag, "Air and this Mac both changed 1 place. Everything else merged." (or, two-way, "…both changed this file. Both versions are in the text, between markers."), the ⌘1/⌘2/⌘3 hint, and **Resolve…**. Escape hides it until the blocks change.
- **In the editor (§5.4's frames, same day):** each block is drawn as a HUD frame on a panel in a dark tint of the one red (#2B2124): 1 pt lines in #B35C5C, `┌ AIR · 10:42 ─── ⌘1 USE · ⌘3 BOTH ┐` over the first marker line, `├ MINI · 10:41 ─── ⌘2 USE ┤` over the second, `└──┘` over the last, labels in Silkscreen 9 pt (#C98080, hints in the scheme's comment grey) on cut-outs of the rules, and Blit's 7×7 pixel ✕ in the gutter. The marker lines' own text is hidden under the frame (a TextKit 2 rendering attribute), so the file keeps plain markers any editor reads. Drawn in the text view's background pass with the line highlight, never through `layoutManager`. **VoiceOver:** a Conflicts rotor on the text view goes from block to block and names each ("Conflict: Air · 10:42, or Mini · 10:41") (AX2).
- **Conflicts menu:** with the caret in a block, **⌘1** "Use Air · 10:42", **⌘2** the other, **⌘3** Keep Both, each an edit like typing (one undo step, journaled, saved, uploaded); **⌥⌘↓ / ⌥⌘↑** next and previous block; **⌥⌘R** Resolve… goes to the next block until the merge view exists.
- **Guards:** a version already taken in (merged, or written between markers) arriving again isn't news, the 13:22 double delivery in its new form. Blocks written two-way are replaced by the three-way merge if the note proving the base arrives while the text is as written.
- **Checks:** `external` and `unsent` now expect both versions between markers (buffer and file) and resolve them with ⌘3; `merge` expects no block; the two-Mac test's writer resolves its blocks after 5 s and both Macs must end with no block for the run. The single-Mac suite starts from a fresh, generated test file, since earlier runs' Keep Both results had left 25 blocks in it. Single-Mac suite passes (57 NibKit tests, 9 scenarios); the two-Mac test with blocks hasn't run.
- **Two-Mac test, ninth run (15:05), the first with blocks. Mac B (Mac mini):** the clash as designed: B's upload refused, B wrote one three-way block ("merged from the version both had, between markers"), resolved it with ⌘3 5 s later, uploaded; no block left on B. **Mac A (MacBook Air) passed everything:** B's resolution reached it ("the block was resolved (Keep Both), here too"), it wrote no block in either phase, and both Macs ended with **the same SHA-256** (`c6492da2…`). The apart phase merged cleanly by itself, but its check failed on a logging fault: `incoming()` logged a `conflict` after every proven merge, clean ones too, and the harness counts those. Also found: a 30-second fetch that began while B's upload was running decided what to skip when it began, before that upload finished, so iCloud's stale offer of the version B had just merged got through (the buffer was safe: the version was already taken in, and ignored). **Changes:** `conflict` is logged only when blocks are written; the fetch asks which versions are stale once iCloud has answered; a version ignored as already taken in is logged (`reload · … left as is`). Single-Mac suite passes after these; the two-Mac test not yet run again.
- **Two-Mac test, tenth run (15:25), with the fixes from the ninth: passed on both Macs, the same SHA-256** (`7cfab3c0…`). The clash: B wrote one three-way block, resolved it with ⌘3, and the resolution reached A. The apart phase: B merged by itself, no `conflict` logged, no block on either Mac. Both times B merged, the file watch delivered the same version again after the fetch, and the Sync Log now shows it being ignored ("a version already taken in, left as is").
**The merge view (2026-09-28), §5.5, for conflict blocks.** Resolve… (⌥⌘R, or the banner's button) opens it with more than three blocks; with fewer it goes to the next block. A sheet on the scratch window: the HUD header (`NIB://MERGE · SCRATCH.MD · 2 CONFLICTS · 2 RESOLVED`, then `ALL CONFLICTS RESOLVED · ⌘↩ TO FINISH`); each version's whole text in a boxed pane (`AIR · THIS MAC · 10:42`), each line marked against the other version, `~` changed and `+` only on this side, in the editor's font with An Old Hope's `markup.changed` and `markup.inserted` tints, opening at the first difference (without the base, a line one side lacks is `+`, never `−`); below, the result, editable, a second editor with the same frames, undo and keys (⌘1, ⌘2, ⌘3 move on to the next block; ⌥⌘↓/↑). **Done** (⌘↩) puts the result in the buffer as one edit ("Merge View"); if the buffer changed while the sheet was up (another Mac's version arrived), the two are three-way merged from the text as the sheet opened, so nothing that came meanwhile is lost. **Cancel** (Escape) changes nothing; the markers stay. The Conflicts menu acts on the result while the sheet is up. New scenario `mergeview`: four blocks, Resolve… opens the sheet, three resolved there (first, second, both), Done writes the result with one left, Cancel leaves the text as it was. 59 NibKit tests (sides and line marks: 2 new), 10 scenarios.
**History, iCloud conflict versions and duplicates (2026-09-28), SY11 and SY13, §4.6 3-4, §4.8 C-D.**

- **History** (`NibSync/History.swift`): every version this Mac opened, saved or took in (from another Mac, another app, an iCloud conflict version, a duplicate), stored once each by SHA-256 in `…/nib/History/<file>/versions/`, with a JSON Lines index (time, where it came from, parent when known, size); thinned at each open (all of the last day, the newest of each hour to a week, of each day to 30 days, then gone). Local only. It's also a merge base when the last eight kept in memory don't have the one a note names. 2 tests. **Revert To** in the File menu lists the last fifteen versions ("Today 15:48:31 · saved") and **Browse All Versions…** opens the **History window** (a native list, newest first, and a preview in the editor's font; **Restore This Version**). Restoring is one edit (⌘Z undoes it) and the version it replaces stays in History. The strip's right-click menu has Save Now, Show History and Reveal in Finder.
- **iCloud conflict versions** (flow D; `CloudControl.conflictVersions`, `markResolved`): looked for at open, when Nib comes forward, and every 30 s while it's in front. One this Mac already has (identical to the buffer or the file, taken in before, or in History) is marked resolved at once. Anything else goes into the text beside this Mac's, both whole versions between markers labelled with the Mac that saved it (iCloud doesn't say what it was made from), and is marked resolved only once a save has put that text in the file. Nothing is deleted: iCloud keeps them as ordinary versions, and History has them.
- **Duplicates** (flow C; `NibSync/Duplicates.swift`): `<name> <n>.<ext>`, n ≥ 2, beside the file; 3 tests of the names. Duplicates already there the first time Nib looks are someone's own files and left alone. A new one that's identical goes to the Trash (`REMOVED DUPLICATE “SCRATCH 2.MD” (IDENTICAL) · IN THE TRASH`); one that's a version this Mac already has goes too (`… (AN EARLIER VERSION, IN HISTORY) …`); one with new text goes into the text between markers and to the Trash once a save has it in the file (`MERGED “SCRATCH 2.MD” INTO THE TEXT · IT'S IN THE TRASH`). Only to the Trash, never deleted, and only if it's still what was read; History keeps its text as well.
- This work runs on its own queue: iCloud's version store can be slow, and saves mustn't wait on it (the `type` scenario's save went from 1.0 to 2.5 s with it on the save queue).
- **Checks:** scenarios `duplicates` (the three cases), `conflictversions` (identical, and new text; pretended, since a real one needs iCloud itself to reconcile two offline edits), and `history` (Revert To and back). 64 NibKit tests, 13 scenarios.
**Files and tabs (2026-09-28), ED2, open question 11 (native tabs).** File windows are a `WindowGroup(for: URL.self)` beside the scratch `Window`: **⌘O** (Open…), **Open Recent** (the system's list, with Clear Menu), **Finder's Open With** and files dropped on the **Dock icon** (`CFBundleDocumentTypes` for text, as an Alternate handler, so Nib never takes over as the default app). One `Document` per file (`Documents`): opening a file again selects its tab and brings the window forward. File windows open as native tabs of one window (the tab bar's **+** shows the Open panel until untitled buffers, ED3, exist); the scratch window and History windows are never tabs. Closing a file's tab saves, uploads and hands the file back to iCloud; quitting does that for every open file; activation, sleep, wake and screen lock reach every document. Save, Revert To and the Conflicts menu act on the window in front (`@FocusedValue`), History windows are per file, and the strip's Show History opens its file's. **The M1 safety rule still holds:** a file outside the test folders opens as a refused, empty window ("NIB OPENS ONLY TEST FILES … UNTIL M1 HAS PASSED"), nothing read; lifting it is the user's decision. A window's setup runs when its view first lands in the window (a tick after it's made wasn't always enough, which left the second of two files outside the tabs one run in three). New scenario `files` (own window, tabs, same buffer on reopening, saving, closing, the refusal): 14 scenarios.
**Highlighting (2026-09-28), HL1, HL7, HL10, S3: 99.86% against Sublime.**

- **The harness (S3)** in `fidelity/`: six reference files (HTML with `<style>` and `<script>`, CSS, JavaScript, JSON, Markdown with fenced bash and js, a zsh script), generated for now (the user's real files can replace them); `NibFidelity.py`, a Sublime plugin installed in `Packages/User` with An Old Hope · Nib, which opens the files in a window of its own, sets the scheme on those views only, and dumps every token's scope and style (`extract_tokens_with_scopes`, `style_for_scope`) to `fidelity/sublime/`; `run.sh` does both and runs Nib's comparison, `nib-fidelity`, which compares the text color of every non-whitespace character (code points to UTF-16) and lists the largest mismatches by scope. `swift test` holds the bar too (`NibSyntaxTests`).
- **Scheme resolution matches Sublime exactly:** `NibText.Theme` parses the `.tmTheme` and resolves each property from the best-ranked rule (TextMate's specificity: each selector atom's segments, weighted by how deep it matched, the last atom matched from the innermost scope out; later rules win ties). For every one of the ~3,000 scopes Sublime reported, it gives the same text color, background and italic (a test).
- **Engine:** tree-sitter, with Nib's own query per language (`NibSyntax/Queries/*.scm`) whose capture names are Sublime's scopes, written against the dump (Sublime colors calls green and definitions yellow, properties and keys plain, `get` cyan italic, built-ins like `Map`, `Math.round`, `console.log`, `process.cwd` cyan). A character's scopes are the root plus every capture around it, the later pattern inside where two capture the same text, embedded languages nested under Sublime's embedded roots (`source.js.embedded.html`, `markup.raw.code-fence.shell.markdown-gfm source.shell.bash`, Markdown's inline layer). Two conventions stand in for Sublime's syntax features: `only.<scope>` drops the other captures on that text (a pseudo-element or a custom-property definition, which An Old Hope draws plain), and `clear.<scope>` drops the enclosing string scopes (Sublime's `clear_scopes` in shell `$(…)` and `${…}`). What tree-sitter keeps in one token Sublime scopes apart, and the highlighter splits: quotes, comment markers, `@` of at-rules, entity `&`/`;`, `$` of variables, decimal points and signs, DOCTYPE's words, URL separators, entities in attribute values, JSDoc `@param`, regex slashes and quantifiers, shell globs and escapes, a shebang's interpreter.
- **Result:** JSON, CSS, HTML and JavaScript 100%, Markdown 99.84%, zsh 99.09%; **99.86% overall** (5,651 of 5,659 characters). What's left: zsh's `${0:t}` modifier and `${FILES[@]}`, a tilde in a fenced command. As HL7 predicted, An Old Hope's JSON-key rule targets Sublime 3's scopes, so keys are strings (cyan) in Sublime 4 and in Nib; open question 9's default (Sublime's actual rendering) holds.
- **In the editor:** the syntax comes from the file's name and first line (HL1) and shows in the strip; the whole text is parsed and styled off the main thread a moment after typing pauses, colors shift with each edit until then, and they're applied to the text in view and a margin, again as it scrolls (§6.3): colors and backgrounds as TextKit 2 rendering attributes, bold, italic and underline as the text's font and underline (SF Mono's own bold and italic faces, slanted only if a font has no italic; set only where they differ, outside undo, never an edit, and typing always starts plain); files over 5 MB aren't highlighted (ED9). Conflict blocks' marker lines are blanked for parsing (`=======` would underline a Markdown heading, `>>>>>>>` start a quote).
- **The syntax menu (HL1):** clicking the syntax in the strip, or View › Syntax, offers Automatic (naming what the file's name and first line say) and Plain Text, HTML, CSS, JavaScript, JSON, Markdown and Shell. A pick is kept for the file (by path, in Nib's defaults) and isn't an edit; Shell keeps zsh's scopes when the file says zsh. The command palette (KB3) lists it with the other menu commands. The strip is now a VoiceOver container (the sync state, the position, the syntax menu) rather than one element. A scenario (`syntax`) checks it: a `.txt` file is plain text, picking JavaScript highlights it without an edit, reopening keeps the pick, Automatic undoes it, and View › Syntax has the items. Two fixes found on the way: turning highlighting off left the old colors on the text in view, and the current-line band on the empty line after a final newline sat one line low.
- **Not yet:** incremental reparsing (each pause reparses the whole text). *Done the same day: see Incremental highlighting below.*

**Theme import (2026-09-28), HL2-HL4, §5.8.**

- **Formats (HL2):** a `.tmTheme` and a `.sublime-color-scheme` become one document in the latter's shape before their colors are worked out (a `.tmTheme`'s `lineHighlight` becomes `line_highlight`), so an override applies to either, and Nib keeps each imported scheme as a `.sublime-color-scheme` in `~/Library/Application Support/nib/Themes/`, variables and all. Sublime's JSON (comments, trailing commas) is read by `LenientJSON`. Colors (`SchemeColors`): hex with or without alpha, `rgb()`/`rgba()`, `hsl()`/`hsla()`, `hwb()`, the CSS names, `var()` (nested; a loop stops), and `color()` with `alpha()`/`a()`, `lightness()`/`l()`, `saturation()`/`s()` (a value, `+ `/`- ` a change, `*` a factor), `blend()` (keeps the given share of the base color, as Sublime's mdpopups does), `blenda()` and `min-contrast()`; a rule's `foreground_adjust` changes the text under its background (Sublime's diff rules use it). Read approximately, and reported: gradient text (its first color), stippled and squiggly underlines (as underline), glow (left out), HWB blends (as RGB). Sublime's five built-in schemes read with every color understood (a test, when Sublime is installed).
- **Import from Sublime Text (HL3):** `SublimeImport` layers Default's Preferences, Default's "Preferences (OSX)", then the user's two, as Sublime does; `auto` takes `dark_color_scheme`. The scheme is found by path or by file name, loose in `Packages/`, in `Installed Packages/`, or among the app's packages, read out of the zip in memory (`ZipArchive`: stored and deflated entries, Compression's raw DEFLATE; nothing extracted, L1), and `Packages/User/<name>.sublime-color-scheme` is merged in as its override. The font face and size are applied (the editor font was SF Mono 13); tab size, spaces or tabs, word wrap and line padding are stored for when the editor has them and reported as "NOTED, NOT USED YET" (W2). The original An Old Hope is recognized by its name and Nib keeps its own edition (HL10). This Mac's Sublime: `Packages/User/an-old-hope.tmTheme`, Menlo 12 (Sublime's macOS default; the user's settings don't set a font), tab size 4, tabs, word wrap auto.
- **The report (HL4)** reads as in HL4's example above. Its contrast figures are WCAG's; they corrected two of HL10's (#7851A9 is 2.8:1 and white on #F92672 3.5:1; the table had rounded twice). The built-in scheme's report ends "EVERY COLOR AT LEAST 4.5:1 ON THE BACKGROUND", and a test keeps it so (§9). `swift run nib-fidelity --report [scheme]`, in NibKit, prints the report for a file, or for what Import from Sublime would find, read-only.
- **Settings (§5.8):** a Settings window (⌘,) with Editor (font and size, the rest of §5.8's Editor tab later) and Themes: the scheme in use (Remove for an imported one), Import from Sublime Text, Import File…, dropping a scheme file on the tab, and the last report in a HUD box. "Import from Sublime Text" has no ellipsis, since it asks nothing more (W3). A new scheme or font redraws every open editor with attributes only: not an edit, nothing to save or undo. A light scheme keeps the title bar dark (A17); without that the title was white on white. The built-in scheme is now named "An Old Hope · Nib". The fixed colors (`OldHope`) became `Scheme`, a view of the scheme in use.
- **Checks:** 19 NibText tests (JSON, every color form and adjuster, contrast, both formats, overrides, the report, zip, a generated Sublime folder, Sublime's own schemes), and a `themes` scenario: a light scheme file is used and reported (background, heading color as drawn, windows still dark, not an edit), Import from Sublime from a generated Sublime folder (a `.tmTheme` in a package, a User override, Menlo 15, two-space tabs), and the original An Old Hope keeping the Nib edition; it puts the user's scheme, font and report back. Single-Mac suite: 16 scenarios, 81 checks; 99 NibKit tests.
- **Not yet:** re-importing when the source file changes (HL9, P2), the first run's Look step (§5.9), and drawing the globals Nib doesn't draw yet (brackets, tags, guides, find highlight, invisibles, gutter).

**Untitled buffers (2026-09-28), ED3, open question 12 (local only).**

- **What they are:** File › New (⌘N), and the tab bar's +, opens "Untitled", "Untitled 2"… (the lowest number free) as a tab among the files. Each is a file of Nib's own at `~/Library/Application Support/nib/Untitled/<id>/Untitled 2`, on this Mac only, so the journal (within 300 ms), saving, History, crash recovery and the syntax menu work as for any file (`nib/Model/Untitled.swift`). They skip everything about iCloud (no hold, fetch, note or duplicate checks) and stay out of the Sync Log; the strip says `= ON THIS MAC ONLY · 17:51:06` and the title bar "Untitled 2 – On this Mac only". The M1 safety rule lets them through: they're never the user's files.
- **Hot exit:** quitting keeps them (a quit isn't a close), and the next launch brings every one back, empty ones aside; after a crash, the journal's typing comes back as for any file (`RECOVERED TYPING FROM BEFORE NIB QUIT UNEXPECTEDLY · SAVING IT NOW`).
- **Closing one** asks nothing (V8): a non-empty buffer's text goes into its History (source "closed") and onto **File › Reopen Closed File (⇧⌘T, §5.12)**, which brings it back where it was, under its name, for 30 days; after that, a sweep at launch removes it and its History. An empty one just goes. Reopen Closed File also reopens closed files (skipping any deleted since, which opening would recreate empty); it keeps the last 20. (Since 2026-10-03 an empty one's History goes with it unless it once had text, and one pushed off the 20 goes, History and all: §8, Untitled buffers' History.)
- **Save… (⌘S on an untitled buffer, W3)** asks where, starting in the test folder, writes the text in place, opens that file in its place and lets the buffer go (its History and caret going with it since 2026-10-03). Until M1 has passed, only into the test folders: elsewhere it's refused and nothing is written (`NIB OPENS ONLY TEST FILES …`); a file already open in Nib is refused too.
- **Checks:** three launches. `untitled`: ⌘N, the journal, saved on this Mac only, nothing in the Sync Log, a second one as a tab, closing it into History and the list, ⇧⌘T bringing it back, an empty one leaving nothing, Save… refused outside the test folders and then done inside, and typing only the journal has before a SIGKILL. `untitled-return`: both buffers back, the first with the journal's typing, saved; closed and forgotten; one more left open for an ordinary quit. `untitled-quit`: it's back. Single-Mac suite: 19 scenario runs, 98 checks; 99 NibKit tests.
- **Not yet:** a way to browse closed untitled buffers beyond ⇧⌘T. (`cmd | nib`, and Duplicate, Rename… and Move To…, came later the same day.)

**The Sync Log window (2026-09-28), SY13, A13, §5.2.**

- **Window › Sync Log (⌥⌘L)**, Blit's Transfer Log for syncing: every line of `sync.jsonl` on this Mac in a table, newest first (any column sorts), each with the strip's glyph and a word (V4: `=` Saved, `▲` Uploaded, `▼` Fetched or Refreshed, `✓` Merged or Resolved, `‖` Upload refused or unanswered, `✕` Conflict or anything that failed, in the one muted red, V3; `·` the rest: Opened, Held, Handed back, Closed), the file, the detail, size, time taken and the SHA-256's start. Search takes every word against the file, event, device and detail, or the start of a SHA-256. Filters: All, Saves, Arrivals, Problems (conflicts, refusals, no answers, failures). The selected line shows the whole record, with Show in Finder, Show History and Copy SHA-256; Show Log File reveals `sync.jsonl`. It follows the log as lines are added (`SyncLog.didAppend`, read again once a burst settles). The strip's right-click menu has **Show Sync Log**, which opens it on that file (not for untitled buffers, which stay out of the log). The words, glyphs, filters and search are `NibSync/SyncLogDisplay.swift`, with 4 tests; a `synclog` scenario checks the menu item and ⌥⌘L, newest first, a save appearing while the window is open, search by SHA-256 and by file, and the Problems filter. Single-Mac suite: 20 scenario runs, 105 checks; 103 NibKit tests.
- **Found with it on its first day:** 12 holds that failed today, and those files ran unheld (iCloud syncing them on its own schedule): 10 with `Couldn't communicate with a helper application` (Cocoa 4097/4099, iCloud's file provider not answering), nearly all on test files the scenarios had just created, and 1 permission error (513) on `scratch-test.md` this morning. No scenario checks that a file is held, so none failed. **Fixed the same day:** a hold that fails that way is tried again in the background (§4.3), 2, 5, 10, 20 and 40 s later, then every minute while the file is open. Meanwhile the file runs unheld, as before; once it holds, Nib takes in whatever iCloud wrote, uploads with fail-on-conflict, and checks the note. Closing waits for a hold in flight (up to its 20 s limit), so a file isn't left paused. The Sync Log shows `‖ Not held` ("not held yet: …; trying again in 2 s"), then `Held` ("held on try 3"), or `✕` "still failing after 6 tries". A `holdretry` scenario forces the first two holds of its file to fail (`-NibFailPauses 2 -NibFailPausesFor hold-`): it opens and saves unheld, is held on try 3, its interim save is uploaded (`✓ SYNCED`), and closing hands it back. The `files` scenario now checks that both of its just-created files end up held. Eight runs that evening held all 16 new files on the first try: the real failures came in a cluster (7 between 16:08 and 16:12, while that scenario ran back to back) and then singly, so the retry's real-world path waits for the next one. Single-Mac suite: 21 scenario runs, 112 checks; 103 NibKit tests.
- **Not yet:** the log is never trimmed (865 KB after a day of test runs); Export (Blit exports its log as CSV).

**Two-Mac test, eleventh run (2026-09-28, 19:11): passed on both Macs, the same SHA-256** (`60076cd4…`), with everything since the tenth: bold and italic, the syntax menu, theme import and Settings, untitled buffers, the Sync Log window and the hold retry, from one commit on both Macs. a's lead line reached B as soon as B came forward (18.8 s after a added it, the test's wait); the clash wrote one block, Keep Both resolved it and the resolution reached A; in the apart phase B merged a's change onto its own by itself, no block on either Mac; both ended `✓ SYNCED` with the file holding exactly the buffer. B's Sync Log for the run: held at once (no retry needed), two uploads refused while A's version was newer, each followed by the fetch and the merge, as designed.
- **Not yet for files:** dragging a file onto a window, the `nib` command-line tool (AP4), ⌘1-⌘9 to pick a tab. (All three came on 2026-09-29.)
- **Not yet:** a real iCloud conflict version or duplicate produced by two Macs (the M1 by-hand "both offline" test, skipped); the merge view's Keep Both Files for an unrelated duplicate (duplicates are merged into the text instead); the lineage extended attribute (§4.6 base 1), iCloud conflict versions and duplicates (§4.6 3-4), marking the merged ranges (V6, V7).

**Incremental highlighting (2026-09-28), §6.3, §6.5.**

- **Measured first** (`nib-fidelity --bench`, a release build, on generated text: scratch-shaped Markdown at 2.4 MB and 5 MB of JavaScript). Every pause in typing reparsed and restyled the whole file off the main thread: Markdown 0.5 s to parse, 1.7 s with queries, embedded languages and runs, 0.84 s more for styles, about 2.6 s a pause; JavaScript 0.9, 6.3 and 12.7 s, about 19 s. Resolving each run's style against the scheme was the worst of it: a file has a few hundred distinct scope stacks among a million runs.
- **Now:** each scope stack's style is worked out once per scheme (12.7 s → 0.15 s), and `NibSyntax.SyntaxDocument` (an actor) keeps the document's tree. The first parse is whole (0.43 s Markdown, 0.51 s JavaScript); after that the change is found by comparing the new text with the last (common start and end, in UTF-16 units), handed to tree-sitter as an edit, and parsed incrementally from units read straight out of an array (swift-tree-sitter's String reader turns each chunk's offset into a String index). Queries run on a cursor range, embedded languages are found and parsed only where they touch the window, and runs are swept only across it. The editor asks for the text in view with 12,000 characters either side (it draws 3,000 either side), and again when scrolling passes that; no parse then.

  | Release build | Markdown 2.4 MB | JavaScript 5 MB |
  |---|---|---|
  | First parse (once, at open) | 0.43 s | 0.51 s |
  | A keystroke: reparse | 50-115 ms | 35-55 ms |
  | A screenful's styles | 6-8 ms | 25-28 ms |
  | Before: every pause | about 2.6 s | about 19 s |

  An edit still reads a few MB (tree-sitter re-lexes around reused nodes: 3.3 MB for a Markdown keystroke, 9 MB for JavaScript), which is tree-sitter's own cost on these grammars; it's off the main thread.
- **Checks:** styles for over 500 windows of the six reference files (any size, starting mid-token, in a fence or a script) equal the whole text's cut to the window; 240 random edits (brackets, quotes, fences, emoji) leave a tree identical to a fresh parse, with identical runs; the fidelity bar is unchanged (99.86%). NibSyntax: 4 new tests.
- **Found by the `bigfile` scenario** (a generated 2.4 MB Markdown file in the test folder, the Debug build): each keystroke took 270-280 ms on the main thread, and scrolling to the end left it uncolored. Neither was the highlighter. **The strip's LN, COL** counted lines from the top at every caret move (255 ms at the end of the file): it now counts on from the last place asked, kept valid through edits by the text storage's own notifications (an edit on the counted line or after it keeps the count; one above it starts again from the top). **Conflict blocks** were searched for in the whole text on every keystroke (14 ms): with no blocks, the search runs only when the edited lines hold a marker. **Scrolling:** the viewport wasn't laid out yet when the scroll handler asked what's visible; it's laid out first now (TextKit 2's `layoutViewport()`). After the fixes, Debug: the slowest keystroke 10 ms; the end colored 105 ms after scrolling there; a heading typed there colored within 14 ms of typing stopping; LN, COL right after typing and after an edit above the caret. The top of the file is colored 2.2 s after asking to open it (Debug; opening and the first parse together).
- **And one race the suite caught:** `flush` (⌘S, closing, quitting) returned at once if a save was already running (an app switch's), before the file had the text. It waits for that save now, then saves what's left.
- Single-Mac suite: 22 scenario runs, 118 checks; 107 NibKit tests.
- **Not yet:** a big `<script>` or fence is parsed whole each time the window touches it; the whole first parse of a very large file keeps its first colors back about half a second (release).

**Two-Mac test, twelfth run (2026-09-28, 20:02): the same SHA-256 on both Macs** (`c9af30d8…`), **but a clash block left unresolved, and one check failed on each Mac.** This time B won the clash race (its upload accepted at 20:03:06) and A's was refused (20:03:07), correctly. A's refusal fetch then asked iCloud for anything newer, and iCloud said no, as the settled rules warn it can for a held file; that answer leaves no Sync Log line, nothing retried, and the other Mac's note, read at 20:03:10, had been marked handled though A had put it off (unsent work). A sat 48 s with B's line nowhere until the scenario brought it forward (20:03:55): then the fetch found B's version, merged it, and wrote the block at 20:03:59, after the scenario's window for Keep Both (20:03:50). So A's "block resolved" check and B's "both clash lines" check (at 20:04:05, a second before A's version reached it) failed; nothing was lost, both files hold both lines, between markers.

- **Fixed:** a refused upload whose fetch brings nothing newer now forces a refresh (the file handed back taking the server's version, evicted, downloaded; the buffer, written to the journal first, merged with it), at most once every 20 s, and otherwise tries the upload again 20 s later. Not with unsaved typing: that save's own upload is refused in turn and comes back here. An announcement put off for unsent work isn't marked handled any more, so a later check takes it up. And a version that's exactly the one this Mac last uploaded, arriving while its unsent work sits on top of it, is nothing new: the work is saved over it again and uploaded (a stale answer or a refresh can bring just that; without this, the whole text went between markers). A revert on another Mac is a different version and merges as before.
- **Checked on one Mac** with a `refusal` scenario: a debug switch refuses the next upload as if another Mac had uploaded first, another makes the next fetch answer "nothing newer". Saved → Upload refused → "nothing newer: refreshing" → Refreshed → Reloaded ("saving that work again") → Saved → Uploaded, `✓ SYNCED`, nothing between markers. Single-Mac suite: 23 scenario runs, 122 checks. The real check is the next two-Mac run.

**Two-Mac test, thirteenth run (2026-09-28, 20:25), with that fix on both Macs: passed on both, the same SHA-256** (`13bf3c7e…`). B won the clash race again; A's upload was refused (20:26:07), and this time iCloud's fetch answered with B's version at once (20:26:09), so the forced refresh wasn't needed and is still checked only on one Mac. A merged before B's note arrived (no version both had known: both whole versions between markers), upgraded to the three-way merge when the note came 2 s later (1 place between markers), Keep Both at 20:26:14, and both Macs had it resolved and synced by the check; the apart phase merged by itself.

- **A wrong name, fixed:** the Sync Log and the strip named the Mac a version came from by iCloud's own answer, or else the last note read. iCloud named the Air for the Mac mini's version (on the Air, as "Bill's Macbook Air"), and on the Mac mini the fallback named the Mac mini itself. Now the other Mac is named only when its note names that very version, never this Mac; otherwise "another Mac" (W2). iCloud's answer is kept in the fetch line, as what iCloud says.
- **Found in the suite run after it (not yet fixed):** handing a file back to iCloud at quit failed with iCloud's helper not answering (Cocoa 4099); Nib's quit takes any failure there for a refusal and hands it back taking the server's version (journaled), which here also failed, so the file stayed paused. The next launch found it paused, couldn't hand it back first (3328), and pausing an already paused file answers "not supported", which Nib took as iCloud not offering the controls: running unheld for the session, with the file in fact paused by Nib, so nothing uploaded it, and the strip still said `✓ SYNCED`. The `refusal` scenario, which needs a held file, failed that launch and passed on its own after. Read per file, the sequence was a little different from the first reading: the quit's second try did hand the file back; the next launch, a second later, saw iCloud's status still saying paused, and both handing back and pausing answered "not supported", so iCloud had probably uploaded it itself and the ✓ was likely true. The flaw was taking "not supported" as final.
- **Fixed (§4.3):** only a real conflict takes the server's version, at open, at quit and when settling paused files at launch. "Not supported" from a hand-back means the file isn't paused (it's held fresh); from a pause, either the file is paused, by this Mac (held as it is), or it's tried again, never "unheld for the session". A passing failure handing back a left-paused file at open holds it as it is and uploads, fail-on-conflict; at quit it's tried once more, then the file stays paused and listed for the next launch (logged as that, not as a refusal). Checked with debug switches for failed hand-backs (`-NibFailResumes`) and "not supported" pauses (`-NibUnsupportedPauses`): `holdretry` held on try 2 after "not supported"; `handback` failed both hand-backs at quit, and `handback-return` found the file left paused, failed once more, held it as it is, uploaded it (`✓ SYNCED`), and handed it back at its own quit (nothing left in `paused.json`). Single-Mac suite: 26 scenario runs, 133 checks.

**Two-Mac test, fourteenth run (2026-09-28, 20:55): failed, and the two Macs ended with different files** (A `41a36361…`, not synced; B `41cd4768…`), **the text doubled on both, nothing lost.** Four faults, read from A's log:

1. **Mine, from the refusal fix at 20:02:** a note put off for unsent work was no longer marked handled, and nothing asked whether its version had been merged since, so A refreshed (20:56:15) for a version it had merged six seconds before.
2. **Saves during a refresh:** Keep Both's save (20:56:16) went into the file while that refresh was evicting and downloading it; the upload answered "not supported", the file being unpaused mid-refresh. The same race came back at 20:57:24.
3. **A merge before the note:** in the apart phase A fetched B's version (20:57:17) a few seconds before B's note, merged with no proven base (both whole versions between markers), and uploaded that (20:57:22). The note came that same second, but the base was checked against `uploadedSHA`, which the upload had just changed, so it no longer counted as proof; Keep Both (20:57:23) then kept both whole versions: the text twice.
4. **A refused save left alone:** a save refused because the file had changed on disk (20:57:25) wasn't tried again, so A sat `SAVED LOCALLY` until it quit.

- **Fixed:** a version from iCloud with no proven base yet now waits up to 10 s for its note (as the note-folder watch reads it, and fresh from the server every 2 s), saving nothing meanwhile (typing goes to the journal); then the merge from the version both had, or, with no note, both whole versions as before (and at once if the file is closed during the wait: 2026-10-07, "Closing while a version waits for its note"). Another app's change on this Mac comes with no note and doesn't wait. A late note is checked against what was uploaded before the merge, so it still upgrades a merge that's gone up since. A note for a version already taken in (merged, superseded, or being waited on) is marked handled and not refreshed for. No save or upload while a refresh runs (they wait), and none while a merge waits for a note. A save refused by the file's change is saved again if anything's still unsaved. And while a document closes, no fetch, note check, refresh or refusal follow-up: the suite found the refusal follow-up's refresh running alongside the quit's own hand-back, which left the buffer only in the journal and a recovery block in the file at the next launch.
- **Checked** with the `merge` scenario in four ways (a debug switch delivers the other Mac's version by fetch, as iCloud does): the note first (a clean merge at once); the note 3 s after the version (it waits, nothing between markers, then a clean merge); no note (both whole versions after 10 s); and the note after that whole-versions merge was uploaded (merged from the version both had after all, then saved and uploaded).
- **Seen, not chased:** quitting a second after a merge, the merge's upload was refused by iCloud and the quit's fallback journaled the text, taking the server's version; the next launch put both between markers. Waiting for the upload to finish, the same merge uploads normally.

**Two-Mac test, fifteenth run (2026-09-28, 21:55), with those fixes on both Macs: passed on both, the same SHA-256** (`32a4fa08…`), nothing doubled, no markers left. The case that failed at 20:55 came up again and went right: in the apart phase A fetched B's version (21:57:17) before its note, logged "waiting up to 10 s for its note", got the note a second later, and merged cleanly (21:57:18); the clash phase merged three-way with its note already there, Keep Both, synced.

- **Found while getting the suite through, and fixed:** (1) **a launch that never finished:** the File menu's Open Recent read the system's recent-documents list at launch, on the main thread, and that waits on a system helper that had stopped answering (`-[NSDocumentController recentDocumentURLs]`, for good, twice in a row); Nib keeps its own list now (the last ten, missing files dropped) and still tells the system of each file, for the Dock's menu. (2) **a crash in iCloud's newest-version call:** `fetchLatestRemoteVersionOfItem` once answered with neither a version nor an error, and Swift's async form traps on that; the completion-handler form is used, and no version is "nothing newer". (3) **the suite script stopped without a word** when a scenario's launch died at once (`set -e` and a `pkill` that found nothing); it goes on and reports "FAIL no output" for that scenario.
- **And one more, found the same way:** after the `bigfile` scenario (a 2.4 MB file made, uploaded a few times and deleted), iCloud's daemons on this Mac stopped answering for minutes, and the next launch waited for good on questions with no time limit: first iCloud's state for the file (`resourceValues` for the ubiquitous keys), then, with that limited, whether Nib's iCloud folder is there (`url(forUbiquityContainerIdentifier:)`, a synchronous call to iCloud Drive's daemon). Both have a 10 s limit now, asked on threads of their own: with no answer, the file is opened anyway (it's on disk) and held once iCloud answers, by the hold retry; the Sync Log says so. The other status questions (after a fetch, a refresh, the unheld upload state) have limits too and no longer run on the main thread. A launch in that state still took about a minute to reach the scenario, so something else waits as well, not yet found. `bigfile` now runs last in the suite. Worth knowing for the real scratch file, which is that size: whether saving and uploading it every few seconds strains iCloud the same way is open (the M0 spikes measured single uploads).
- **The suite on all of it (22:38-22:53):** 145 checks passed and 107 NibKit tests; `bigfile`, last, passed too (slowest keystroke 12.9 ms). iCloud was still slow from the diagnosis runs before it: three launches (both `holdretry` runs, `handback`) didn't reach their scenario in 120 s, and `handback-return` after them found its file not yet held. Two fixes from reading it: the script showed those scenarios' logs from an earlier run as if they'd passed (it now deletes each log first, so a launch that never starts says "FAIL no output"); and the quit took "not supported" from a hand-back (3328) for a passing failure and left the file listed, where it means the file isn't paused, so there's nothing to hand back. Those four scenarios, run again once iCloud answered: all passed, nothing left paused.

**Two-Mac test, sixteenth run (2026-09-28, 23:10), on all of it: passed on both Macs, the same SHA-256** (`5291202f…`), nothing doubled, no markers. The first time the forced refresh and "the version this Mac last uploaded" ran against the real iCloud: A's upload of its Keep Both resolution was refused (23:11:20) though the server held A's own upload of five seconds before; the fetch said nothing newer, so A refreshed, got that version of its own back, took it for what it was ("saving that work again"), and uploaded the resolution (23:11:25). Before those fixes that was the 20:02 stall. Two small things from the logs, fixed: that refresh called A's own version "another Mac's" (the note it read was A's own); and on B a fetch (the window coming forward) ran alongside a refresh for the note (23:12:29), harmless, but a fetch now waits out a refresh.

**The clipboard jobs (2026-09-28), PT3-PT5, SC5, SC6, J1.**

- **⌘V** stays plain text only (open question 7, as recommended): the clipboard's text with "\n" line endings. A clipboard with no plain flavor (rich text or HTML only) pastes as its text, and files copied in Finder as their paths, one per line (PT5; `Clipboard.text`).
- **Paste Clean (⌥⇧⌘V)**, in place of the system's Paste and Match Style: invisible characters out (zero-width space, word joiner, BOM, soft hyphen, bidi controls, invisible operators; zero-width joiners and non-joiners too, but kept between two non-ASCII characters, where an emoji family or Persian needs them), unusual spaces made plain (non-breaking, thin, ideographic…), trailing whitespace trimmed; with Settings › General's "Paste Clean straightens quotes and dashes" (off by default), ‘’ “” – — … made plain. The strip says what it did: `PASTED CLEAN · 1 INVISIBLE REMOVED · 1 SPACE MADE PLAIN · 1 LINE TRIMMED` (`NibText.PasteCleaner.clean`, which counts each kind).
- **Clean Clipboard (⌃⌥⌘V, from any app, and in the Edit menu)** rewrites the clipboard as clean plain text in place, no window; a HUD line at the top of the screen confirms it for 2.5 s, never taking focus (`Toast`, standing in for the menu bar panel until the menu bar extra exists, §5.7), and VoiceOver hears it: `CLIPBOARD CLEANED · 1 INVISIBLE REMOVED · 1 SPACE MADE PLAIN · 1 LINE TRIMMED · FORMATTING DROPPED`.
- **Copy Block (⇧⌘C)** copies the fenced code block at the caret without its fences (the caret on a fence line counts), else the paragraph at the caret, else, with a selection, the selection; the copied text flashes once in the selection color, and the strip says `COPIED THE CODE BLOCK · 2 LINES` (`NibText.TextBlocks`).
- **Append Clipboard to Scratch** (Edit menu; the menu bar and an optional global shortcut come with the menu bar extra): the cleaned clipboard at the end of scratch.md after a blank line, one undoable edit, the caret where it was, without bringing Nib forward; `APPENDED 1 LINE TO SCRATCH` in the strip, and the HUD line when Nib isn't in front.
- The Edit menu's clipboard group is Nib's own now: Cut, Copy, Copy Block, Paste, Paste Clean, Delete, Select All, then Clean Clipboard and Append Clipboard to Scratch; each goes to whatever has focus, so fields in Settings and the Sync Log's search work as before.
- **Checks:** 5 NibText tests (what's removed and kept, the counts, quotes only when asked, fences and paragraphs), and a `cleantext` scenario with the real clipboard (put back after): Paste Clean, an HTML-only clipboard, Clean Clipboard on rich text, Copy Block in a fence and in a paragraph, Append (after a blank line, the caret unmoved, saved), and the Edit menu. Single-Mac suite: 31 scenario runs, 156 checks, all passing; 112 NibKit tests.
- **Not yet:** Append's global shortcut; images on the clipboard (PT6, open question 8). (The menu bar extra came later the same day.)

**Sublime's line commands (2026-09-29), ED6, KB4, §5.12.**

- **A Text menu**, on Sublime's keys: Indent ⌘] and Outdent ⌘[ (Tab and ⇧Tab too, over several lines), Toggle Comment ⌘/, Duplicate Line ⇧⌘D, Move Line Up ⌃⌘↑ and Down ⌃⌘↓, Select Line ⌘L (again: the next line too), Join Lines ⌘J, Go to Line… ⌃G. Each is one undoable change, named in Undo; the edits themselves are `NibText.LineEdits`, with the selection after each.
- **Toggle Comment** follows the file's syntax: `//` for JavaScript and `#` for shell, at the lines' least indentation (all commented: uncommented); `/* */` for CSS and `<!-- -->` for HTML and Markdown, around the lines; none for JSON and plain text (the strip says so). Inside HTML's `<script>` and `<style>` it's still HTML's `<!-- -->` for now.
- **Indentation per file (ED6):** found on open (tabs if most indented lines start with one, else the step of the spaces: 2, 4…), shown in the strip (`· 2 SPACES ·`, `· TABS ·`); for a file that shows none, Settings › Editor's tab width and "Indent with spaces", which Import from Sublime Text now sets and uses (its report says `TAB SIZE 4 · TABS`, no longer "not used yet"). Indent and Tab add the file's unit (spaces to the next stop), Outdent removes up to one, and tabs are drawn that many spaces wide (they were AppKit's 28 points). Return keeps the line's indentation (auto-indent).
- **Go to Line:** ⌃G, or a click on the strip's line slot, turns the slot into a field: "12", or "12:5" for a column; Return goes there, Escape closes it.
- **Checks:** 9 NibText tests (each command, the first and last line, a last line with no line break, blank lines left alone, indentation found), and a `lines` scenario on a JavaScript file indented with two spaces: the indentation found, each command and its undo, `//` then `<!-- -->` in the Markdown scratch file, Return and Tab (one "Typing" undo, as AppKit coalesces typing), Go to Line 4:3, the field in the strip, and the Text menu.
- **Not yet (ED6):** line numbers, show invisibles, trimming trailing whitespace and ensuring a final newline on save.

**Nib's pauses were crashing iCloud's file provider (found 2026-09-29).** The suite started after the line commands left three scenarios with no log (`themes`, `synclog`, `holdretry`), each hung at launch in a coordinated read of its file that iCloud never granted. `fileproviderd`, the process behind iCloud Drive, had crashed at 07:07, as the first of them started, and didn't come back: launchd showed it gone (exit −6), and a coordinated read from any process waited without an answer, so iCloud Drive was stopped for every app on the Mac mini, not only Nib. (It started again at 07:23, 16 minutes later.)

- **The crash:** 36 crash reports since 2026-09-28 16:08, every one the same: `-[FPDXPCServicer pauseSyncForItemAtURL:behavior:bundleID:completionHandler:]` building an array with a nil in it (macOS 27.0.1). Matched against the Sync Log, each came a second or two after Nib paused a file that iCloud was still uploading: a scenario's file written moments before, the test file the suite script had just rewritten, or a file the scenario before had just handed back with an upload to make. The first was the day the `files` scenario began making new files and opening them at once. On Nib's side a crash showed as Cocoa 4097 or 4099, "Couldn't communicate with a helper application": the "failed holds, mostly on just-created test files" the Sync Log showed on its first day, and the reason for the hold retry, were this crash. A macOS bug, but Nib's calls set it off.
- **What iCloud says of a new file** (a probe that only asks, pausing nothing): known to the file provider at once (an identifier, "current", by 0.2 s), not uploaded, then uploading for about 3 s, then uploaded.
- **Fixed (§4.3):** a hold waits until iCloud isn't uploading the file (every quarter second, up to 15 s; not for a file that's paused already or one iCloud doesn't sync), then pauses; closing ends the wait and there's nothing to hand back. That's the first hold at open and its retries. A re-hold of a file Nib is holding (after a fetch or a refresh put a version in place) pauses at once, as before: the first version waited there too, and unpaused for those seconds iCloud put the server's copy back over Nib's newer save, which Nib then took for another app's (the suite's `merge` scenario; nothing was lost, the journal and History had it). No crash was ever near a re-hold. The Sync Log says `pause · after its upload (3.2 s)`. In the find scenario's nine runs every new file waited 2.4-3.7 s and was held at the first try, and no crash report came; before, about half the new files' holds failed. Whether a pause with the network off (a file that can't upload) is safe isn't known; it waits the 15 s and then pauses.
- **And a race it brought out:** a disk check (the file presenter's, for a save's own write) could read the file after a save wrote it but before the save took it as the new base, and so take Nib's own save for another app's change; with the buffer edited meanwhile, that's both whole versions between markers, saved, seen again, and so on: in the `external` scenario 30 times in 2 s, the generated test file doubling each time (25 MB when found). A disk check that overlaps a save is dropped now: the save's compare-and-swap sees any other change, and a later one comes with its own notice. And a version a merge waits on a note for, seen again by a disk check, is left to the wait: it had merged at once, "changed by another app", skipping the 10 s wait (§4.6). The `merge` scenario's no-note check now asks for the wait.
- **And a recovery bug behind a day's worth of crashes:** from 08:15 every launch crashed within 0.3 s (`EXC_BAD_ACCESS` in Swift's executor check as the text view drew) or hung with nothing running. A killed scenario had left a journal whose text was the buffer after Keep Both, with the file (the same base) still holding the block. Recovery put the journal's text in but kept the blocks found in the file's longer text, so the text view drew conflict frames from ranges past the end of its text; TextKit 2 takes such an offset without a word, and what followed was memory corruption. Moving the journal aside made launches work; putting it back made the same kind of launch crash. **Fixed:** recovery finds the blocks again in the text it puts in, and every range the text view turns into TextKit locations is checked against the text first (nil if it doesn't fit). Checked with a `crash-blocks` and `recover-blocks` pair (a block saved, Keep Both typed and journaled, the app killed; the next launch has the text's own blocks, none, and saves it): it passes, and with the fix taken out, the relaunch segfaults at once.
- **The hung launches:** a coordinated read has no time limit (its wait is the system's, and a file can't be shown before it's read). With the file provider gone, Nib can't open an iCloud file at all; it doesn't crash, and it says nothing. Worth a line in the strip if it's seen outside the suite.

**Find and replace (2026-09-29), ED7, KB1.**

- **Nib's own find bar**, over the strip and dark like it (A17): the system's bar has no regular expressions. Find, and with ⌥⌘F or its disclosure button Replace; the options as Sublime shows them: `.*` regular expression (`$1` in Replace is the first group), `Aa` match case, whole word, In Selection (what was selected when it was turned on, following edits); previous, next, Done. The count in the HUD's words: `3 OF 12`, `12 MATCHES`, `NO MATCHES`, `10,000+` past the limit; a pattern that isn't a regular expression in the failure red: `NOT A REGULAR EXPRESSION · “v1\.(”`, as typed, since case matters there.
- **Typing finds as it goes**, from the caret, as Sublime does: the first match after it is selected and scrolled to while the caret stays in the field. The search runs off the main thread, 120 ms after the last change, and again as the text changes while the bar is open. Every other match in view is drawn in the scheme's `find_highlight`, with `find_highlight_foreground` if it sets one (An Old Hope · Nib: black on `#FFE792`), as rendering attributes like the syntax colors (§6.3); the selected match shows as the selection. The import report counts the two as drawn now. *Since 2026-10-06 the selected match is in the find color too, and outlined (§8, Find's matches in one color).*
- **Keys (§5.12):** Edit › Find: Find… ⌘F (the selection in the field, if it's on one line), Find and Replace… ⌥⌘F, Find Next ⌘G and Find Previous ⇧⌘G (Return and ⇧Return in the field), round the end; Use Selection for Find ⌘E, into the system's find clipboard, which ⌘G reads with the bar closed (another app's ⌘E finds here, as on every Mac); Replace ⌥⌘E (Return in the Replace field), Replace All ⌃⌥Return, Sublime's keys. Replace goes on to the next match; Replace All is one undo step, changing only the stretch from the first match to the last, and the strip says `REPLACED 3 MATCHES · ⌘Z UNDOES IT`. A plain replacement is taken as typed (a `$` is a dollar sign).
- **Escape** closes the conflict banner, then the find bar, and does nothing else: the text view's own Escape opened the word completion list, which Nib has no use for (⌥Esc still asks for it). Opened from a scenario, the list was followed by a crash in SwiftUI's next update three times out of three (`EXC_BAD_ACCESS` in Swift's executor check, drawing the strip or the window); not since the change. Why isn't known: the same signature later turned out to be memory corruption from stale conflict frames (below), and whether the list met one of those isn't known either.
- **The engine** is `NibText.TextSearch`: matches (plain text escaped, whole word as `\b(?:…)\b`, `^` and `$` at every line, empty matches left out, stopping at 10,000), one replacement, Replace All with the count, and the next match round the end. 6 tests. The `find` scenario, on a generated Markdown file: the Find menu and its keys, ⌘F from the menu with the selection in the field and the caret there, the other matches in `#FFE792`, ⌘G round the end and ⇧⌘G back, Match Case and Whole Word, a regular expression found as it's typed, a bad pattern, Replace with `$1` then Replace All (one undo each), Replace All on whole words, In Selection, ⌘E into the find clipboard, Escape closing the bar and then doing nothing, ⌘G with the bar closed, and the file as it began. The Find menu acts on the document of the window in front, or, with none, sends its command to the text view with focus. A scenario launched in the background has neither, so it brings its window forward first; when macOS won't let it (another app in use, as in the suite), it opens the bar with the text view's own action and says so.
- **Single-Mac suite, on all of it (2026-09-29, 08:43): 37 scenario runs, 196 checks, all passing; 127 NibKit tests.** No file provider crash during it (still 36 reports, the last at 07:07), and every new file's hold waited for its upload. The suite script now samples a scenario that hangs before killing it (`build/scenarios/<name>-sample.txt`).
- **Not yet:** find in the merge view (⌘F there opens the document's bar, behind the sheet); find in files; a history of queries; Sublime's "highlight matches" option (they're always drawn); multi-line queries typed in the field (⌥Return).

**Two-Mac test, seventeenth run (2026-09-29, 09:15), with the upload wait and all of the above on both Macs: passed on both, the same SHA-256** (`b1d9baf1…`), nothing doubled, no markers. On the Mac mini the test file was held at once (it was uploaded already), and no file provider crash came on it during the run (still 36 reports). The apart phase took the path a stray disk check had cut short that morning: the Air's version came before its note, the merge waited, and it was clean (0 blocks written). a's first line again came only once b came forward (3.9 s after), as a held file doesn't download by itself; two forced refreshes on b brought the Air's versions.

- **Seen, and read wrong at first:** the Air's run printed `[09:14:57]`, 57 s after it should have opened Nib, where the Mac mini's printed `[09:14:00]`. That line comes from the script just before it launches Nib, after its wait, so it wasn't Nib: the script's `sleep` ran late, most likely the Air sleeping for part of it (a sleeping Mac doesn't count time in `sleep`). The eighteenth run found the same, six minutes' worth.
- **Fixed from the Air's build:** a Swift 6 warning in the HUD line's fade-out (`Toast`), a main-actor call in AppKit's completion handler, which AppKit calls on the main thread; it says so now (`MainActor.assumeIsolated`).

**Go to File and the command palette (2026-09-29), KB2, KB3, A6, §5.6.**

- **Blit's palette**, copied to `nib/Vendor/FromBlit/CommandPalette.swift` from Blit at `6d417b0` and generalized: an entry carries its own group label and, for a command, its keys, and what choosing it does is the caller's. The same look (a 520-pt sheet on the window, SF Symbols, the group on the right), the same keys (↑ ↓, Return, Escape, a double click) and Blit's scoring (a prefix, then a substring, then letters in order; a name match above a folder match), ties in the list's own order.
- **Go to File (⌘P, and ⌘K for the family):** "Go to a file, a recent file or the scratch". The scratch file, the open files (untitled ones "On this Mac only"), the recent ones newest first, then the text files in Nib's folder (four folders deep, not hidden, not pictures, sound, video, PDFs or archives; names only, nothing read), each file once, with Finder's name for its folder (`iCloud Drive › Nib › Test`). The folder is listed off the main thread, as iCloud's file provider answers for it and has stalled for minutes: its group comes when the list does. `:12` (or `12:5`) goes to that line of the file in front, as in Sublime. Choosing a file opens it or brings its window forward; the M1 safety rule still refuses files outside the test folders. ⌘K is a key monitor, a menu item taking only one key.
- **The command palette (⇧⌘P):** "Run a command". Every menu command that can be chosen with this window in front, read from the menus as the palette opens (so View › Syntax's languages are there, HL1), named as in the menu with its path (`Edit › Find`) and its keys (`⌥⇧⌘V`); not the palette's own two, the window list, Open Recent (files are Go to File's) or Services; the app menu (About, Hide, Quit) last. What's chosen runs once the sheet has gone, so a text command reaches the text: the item is found again in the menus as they are then, and not run if it's disabled by then.
- **Menus:** File › Go to File… ⌘P; View › Command Palette… ⇧⌘P. Print and Page Setup are gone (Nib doesn't print; they held ⌘P and ⇧⌘P). From a window with no file (Settings, the Sync Log) the palette opens over the scratch window.
- **Found on the way:** SwiftUI doesn't call a sheet's `onDismiss` when the sheet is closed while still coming in, so a choice made that fast didn't run; closing the palette now runs it in a moment anyway, once, whichever comes first.
- **Checks:** a `palette` scenario on a generated JavaScript file: the menus and keys and no Print; Go to File's groups in order, each file once, this file under Open, the Nib folder's group arriving; the scoring; `:3` going to line 3 once the palette has gone; choosing the scratch file; the command palette's commands with their menus and keys (Toggle Comment `⌘/`, Duplicate Line `⇧⌘D`, Find… `Edit › Find ⌘F`, Paste Clean `⌥⇧⌘V`, Sync Log `⌥⌘L`, Move Line Up `⌃⌘↑`), not its own or the window list; a chosen command running once it's gone (Duplicate Line when Nib is in front; Sync Log when macOS won't let it come forward, as in the suite); ⌘K. Each palette is saved as a picture beside the log.
- **Not yet:** recently used commands first (Sublime's order); Goto Anything's `@` (symbols) and `#` (words); files in other folders once the safety rule is lifted (a folder picker, or the folders of recent files).

**Line numbers and invisibles (2026-09-29), ED6.**

- **Line numbers**, on unless turned off: a gutter down the left in the scheme's `gutter` and `gutter_foreground` (or, when it sets none, the text color dimmed toward the gutter only as far as it stays at 4.5:1), the caret's line in `gutter_foreground_highlight` or the text color. One number a line of the file, level with its first row when it wraps; the empty line after a final newline numbered too; three digits wide at least, wider past 999 lines. It's an `NSRulerView` with no client view, and the text view's own ruler support is off; the scenario checks the text view is still on TextKit 2.
- **A line index** (`NibText.LineIndex`): where each line starts, kept current from the text storage's own edit notices (every change, typed or not), looking only at the changed characters of the storage's own string, not a copy; a line by offset is a binary search. The gutter's numbers and the strip's LN, COL both come from it, in place of the strip's count-on-from-the-last-place cache. 2 tests, one of them 2,000 random edits checked against an index made afresh. In the 2.4 MB `bigfile` scenario, the slowest keystroke is 10.3 ms (Debug), as before, and LN, COL are right at line 35,313.
- **Show Invisibles**, off unless turned on: spaces (and no-break spaces) as a dot at the middle of the space, tabs as an arrow across their width, in the scheme's `invisibles` (An Old Hope's `#3B3A32`, faint on purpose), from each row's own character positions (TextKit 2's line fragments), so wrapped lines and wide tabs come out right. Under the text; not saved, copied or journaled.
- **Where:** View › Show/Hide Line Numbers and Show/Hide Invisibles, and Settings › Editor. Import from Sublime Text brings `line_numbers` and `draw_white_space` ("all" is on; Sublime's default, "selection", shows them only in the selection, which Nib doesn't do: off) and says so in its report (`LINE NUMBERS ON · INVISIBLES OFF`). The import report counts the gutter colors and `invisibles` as drawn now.
- **Found on the way:** (1) the gutter painted its background over the text: the scroll view now lays a ruler over the full-width clip view and moves the text in by an inset, and views no longer clip their drawing to their bounds (macOS 14), so the gutter clips itself. A picture made by caching a view's drawing doesn't show TextKit 2's text, so the fault didn't show there; a capture of the window itself (`screencapture -l`, the snapshot scenario now logs the window's number) did. (2) Widening the gutter while the text storage was mid-edit (the thousandth line) re-tiled the scroll view, which laid the text out, and TextKit 2 threw ("enumerate while editing"); the gutter widens a moment after the edit now. (3) SwiftUI renames menu items ("Hide Line Numbers") only when AppKit says the menu is about to open: the command palette asks for that before reading the menus, so it lists what the menu would show.
- **Checks:** a `gutter` scenario on a generated file with a tab, trailing spaces, a wrapped line and a final newline: on by default and still TextKit 2; the numbers drawn (1-6, the wrapped line once, the empty last line) and the bright one; LN, COL against a count from the top after an edit; the gutter widening past 999 lines and undo putting it back; View › Hide and Show Line Numbers; Show Invisibles (a dot for each of the 100 spaces, an arrow for the tab) and Hide; the file unchanged.
- **Then (the user's call, 2026-09-29): trimming trailing whitespace and ensuring a final newline, on ⌘S only.** Nib saves by itself a second after typing stops, so doing either at every save would take away a space just typed at the end of a line, or add a line under the caret. Settings › Editor: "⌘S trims trailing whitespace" and "⌘S ends the file with a newline", both off (Sublime's defaults); Import from Sublime Text brings `trim_trailing_white_space_on_save` (true, "all" or "not_on_caret" is on) and `ensure_newline_at_eof_on_save`. Pressing ⌘S (File › Save) in a file typed in this session trims spaces and tabs off every line's end and adds the newline, as one change ⌘Z undoes ("Trim Whitespace and Add Newline"), the caret carried to where its text went, then saves and uploads as ⌘S always has; the strip says `TRIMMED TRAILING WHITESPACE ON 3 LINES · ADDED A FINAL NEWLINE · ⌘Z UNDOES`. A file only opened, or changed only by a merge or a reload, is left as it is (SY4). Save… for an untitled buffer tidies it too. The edit itself is `NibText.SaveTidy` (3 tests: each part only when asked, the text around untouched, the caret, a stray `\r` kept); a `tidy` scenario checks it's off by default, does nothing to a file not edited here, that Nib's own save keeps a space just typed, then ⌘S, the notice, the file on disk, and one ⌘Z putting it all back. Single-Mac suite (11:03): 41 scenario runs, 225 checks, all passing; 133 NibKit tests.

**Two-Mac test, eighteenth run (2026-09-29, 10:40): failed on both, and didn't test anything.** The Air's script woke from its wait at 10:45:18, six minutes after it should have opened Nib (10:39:00), most likely because the Air was asleep; by then the Mac mini had run its side alone and quit (a's lines 0×, its own synced), and the Air ran every phase within a second (its checks meaningless, and it quit mid-edit). Nothing in Nib; the Mac mini's own side was clean (held at once, no file provider crash).

- **Fixed, in `run-m1-two-mac.sh`:** it keeps the Mac awake while it runs (`caffeinate -dis`; a closed lid on battery still sleeps), waits by the clock, and if it gets to the start more than 30 s late, says so ("was this Mac asleep?") and doesn't run.

**Two-Mac test, nineteenth run (2026-09-29, 10:55), with everything since 09:15 on both Macs (line numbers, the palette, the lost-hold fixes): passed on both, the same SHA-256** (`56d37918…`), nothing doubled, no markers; both Macs opened Nib on the minute. The Mac mini's Sync Log shows the new `unsent` rule's path against the real iCloud: after Keep Both, its upload was refused, iCloud offered a version it had moved past and then said nothing was newer, the forced refresh brought back its own last upload, and that was taken as "the version this Mac last uploaded, which its unsent work starts from; saving that work again", saved and uploaded. The clash merged three-way (one block, Keep Both); on the Air the apart phase waited for its note and merged cleanly. No file provider crash on the Mac mini.

**The menu bar extra (2026-09-29), §5.7, AP3, V11, A10.**

- **The item:** §5.7's pixel nib over its line, 9 cells at 1.5 pt, a template image of a fixed width, still when idle. Once an upload has been pending (saved, not yet taken) for more than 3 s, the line's dashes march left to right, a step every 0.16 s (V7), until it's taken; then it's still again. While any open file holds a conflict block or a problem, the muted-red ✕ sits beside the nib. VoiceOver hears "Nib, synced at 11:53:21", and the tooltip says the same in the HUD's words.
- **The panel:** Blit's 320 pt column on the menu's dark material, under the item: a HUD card for the scratch file (its name, the strip's state line, `SAVED 11:53:14 · UPLOADED 11:53:21`, a conflict's places, and the other Mac's last upload from its note, or `NO OTHER MAC YET`); then native rows with their keys: Show Scratch ⌥Space, Clean Clipboard ⌃⌥⌘V and Append Clipboard to Scratch (their result on a line in the panel: PT4's "the menu bar panel confirms it"), up to five recent files (not the scratch file, not files gone since), Settings… ⌘, and Quit Nib ⌘Q. It takes the keys without making Nib the active app, and closes on Escape, a click elsewhere, or an action that opens a window. Receipts from the other Macs aren't there yet: Nib doesn't have them.
- **Settings › General** (a new first tab): "Show Nib in the menu bar", on by default, as in Blit.
- **AppKit's own status item, not SwiftUI's `MenuBarExtra`:** on macOS 27, (1) `MenuBarExtra(isInserted:)` going false did nothing: the item stayed (and with the setting read only through its binding, the app's scenes weren't even made again when it changed); (2) of a label with two images, only the first showed: the ✕ beside the nib never appeared. So Nib makes the `NSStatusItem` itself, removes it when turned off, and draws the nib and the ✕ as one image (then not a template, the ✕ keeping its red; the nib in the label color, which takes the menu bar's own appearance when drawn). Blit builds its item the same SwiftUI way, so it likely has both faults on macOS 27; worth a look there.
- **Checks:** a `menubar` scenario: the item there by default; the art a fixed width with its dashes stepping right; still while synced; marching once an upload has waited more than 3 s (uploads held back by a debug switch), still again once synced; the scratch card's lines; the ✕ with a conflict block open (the image wider and no longer a template); a click opening the 320 pt panel under the item, Escape closing it; Settings › General taking the item away and putting it back. The item (plain and with the ✕) and the panel are saved as pictures beside the log.

**A lost hold that took the typing out of the buffer (found by the suite, 2026-09-29, 10:08).** In `refusal`, the save of a typed line was refused (as it's meant to be), the forced refresh failed with "not supported" (3328: the file wasn't paused after all; the scenario before had handed it back a moment before this launch paused it), iCloud put the server's older copy in the file, and Nib took that copy into the buffer as "nothing unsent here". The line survived only in the journal, and the next launch put it between markers. Two faults: a disk check begun before the refresh read the file during it and acted on it with the hold down; and `unsent` counted a saved version iCloud hadn't taken only while the file was held.

- **Fixed:** a disk check that overlaps a refresh is dropped (as one overlapping a save already is), and a failed refresh ends by looking at the file itself once the hold is back; and a version saved while the file was held stays unsent until it's uploaded, even if the hold is lost meanwhile (a file iCloud syncs by itself, never held, is as before). An older copy arriving then is "the version this Mac last uploaded, which its unsent work starts from", and the work is saved over it again and uploaded.
- **Checked** with `refusal-lost-hold`: `refusal` again, with a debug switch that hands the file back for real, taking the server's version, just before the refresh, so the refresh fails with 3328 and iCloud puts the older copy in the file: the line stays, is saved again and uploaded, `✓ SYNCED`, nothing between markers. Single-Mac suite on all of it (10:15): 40 scenario runs, 218 checks, all passing; 130 NibKit tests. The suite's own timing (the stale disk check) didn't come back on demand: the switch passed with the old rule too, so the check of the fix for that exact sequence is the reading of the log, and the next suite runs.
- **It came back for real in the suite at 11:59**, in `refusal-lost-hold`, before the switch was even reached: the launch paused the file a second after the one before handed it back, the pause didn't last, the refused upload was followed by iCloud's older copy on disk, and the new rule did its job ("the version this Mac last uploaded, which its unsent work starts from; saving that work again", nothing between markers). What followed didn't: every upload after answered "not supported" (3328, an upload for a paused file, and the file wasn't), and Nib retried until quit, the strip saying `ICLOUD ISN'T TAKING UPLOADS`. **Fixed:** an upload answered "not supported" holds the file again (if iCloud says it isn't paused), looks at the file, and uploads again a second later; and 3 s after the hold at open, Nib asks whether the file is still paused, and holds it again if a hand-back just before undid it ("the hold didn't last"), before iCloud can put anything in the file. Both variants of `refusal` pass alone; the suite on everything (12:12): 42 scenario runs, 235 checks, all passing; 130 NibKit tests. No hold was lost in that run, so neither new path has been seen working yet: the Sync Log will say "the hold didn't last" or "the file isn't held any more" when one does.

**Launch at login and the first run (2026-09-29), AP2, AP5, §5.8, §5.9; and the global shortcuts made changeable (SC2, PT4, SC6).**

- **The first run:** at the first launch on a Mac, a Welcome to Nib window in three steps, native controls (V1), each step changeable later in Settings. (1) **Scratch File:** "Where's your scratch file?"
