Nib first asks iCloud for its folder (which sets it up on a Mac new to it, up to 10 s), then says whether the file is there: "Found scratch.md in iCloud Drive › Nib", with Show in Finder and Choose Another…, or "There's no scratch.md… yet" with Create and Choose…. **With no scratch file, none is made until the user says so** (the scratch window's strip says `‖ NO SCRATCH FILE YET · HELP › WELCOME TO NIB SETS ONE UP`, the text not editable): two Macs each making one on their first run would leave a `scratch 2.md` (§4.6 C, table row 20), and the note says to make it only on the first Mac. A file chosen in place of the one open is used once Nib reopens, at the end. Then **what macOS may ask**, before it asks: nothing for Nib's own iCloud folder or its shortcuts; once, perhaps, for a file chosen in Documents, the Desktop or elsewhere in iCloud Drive; and a notification when Nib is added to Login Items. (2) **Look:** the scheme and font in use, and what Import from Sublime Text would bring, read from Sublime's settings without importing (`MENLO 12 · TAB SIZE 4 · TABS · AN-OLD-HOPE (NIB KEEPS ITS OWN EDITION)` on the Mac mini), with Import from Sublime Text and then its report; or "Sublime Text isn't on this Mac". (3) **Keys:** the two global shortcuts' fields and Open Nib when you log in. Closing it early counts as done if the scratch file is settled; otherwise it comes back at the next launch. **Help › Welcome to Nib** shows it again (in place of the Help Book Nib doesn't have).
- **Under the safety rule** (§8) the scratch file it finds is the test file, and it says so ("Until M1 has passed, Nib opens only test files… so for now this is its test file"); Choose… refuses a file outside the test folder in a sentence. Once `allowLiveScratch` is set, the standard scratch file is `iCloud Drive/Nib/scratch.md` (before, setting it left Nib on the test file). The scenarios always use the test file, whatever this Mac has chosen. *Since 2026-10-06 only a test run keeps the rule: the standard scratch file is `iCloud Drive/Nib/scratch.md` with no setting, and the sentences say "In a test run, …".*
- **Launch at login (AP2):** `SMAppService.mainApp`, a switch in the first run and Settings › General that reads the system's state each time it's shown (it can be turned off in System Settings), with "Open Login Items" when macOS waits for approval. For a build that has never been registered, macOS reports the login item as "not found", which reads as off.
- **The global shortcuts, changeable:** Settings › General has Show Scratch (⌥Space), Clean Clipboard (⌃⌥⌘V) and Append Clipboard to Scratch (none until chosen, SC6's "optional global shortcut"). A field listens on a click; the new keys need ⌘, ⌃ or ⌥ (⇧ alone would take a capital letter from every app) or are a function key; Escape keeps the old ones, Delete clears them, and while it listens Nib's own shortcuts are off, so pressing ⌥Space reaches the field (and they come back if the window loses focus). The Edit menu and the menu bar panel show the keys as set. Keys another of Nib's actions has are refused.
- **What stands in the way, said under the field (SC2):** Nib now registers its shortcuts exclusively. **Found:** Carbon reports another app only if that app registered the keys exclusively too (`eventHotKeyExistsErr`, checked with a second process); a plain registration by another app, as launchers may make, succeeds for both and nothing says so. So Nib says: "Another app holds ⌃⌥⇧J, so Nib can't use it" (an exclusive holder); "macOS uses ⌘Space itself… so it won't reach Nib" (the system's own shortcuts that are on, from `CopySymbolicHotKeys`); and for ⌥Space, Raycast's and Alfred's usual key, when one of them is running, "If Raycast still uses it, the two clash".
- **Checks:** a `firstrun` scenario (22 checks): the window at the first launch; step 1 finding the scratch file, refusing a path outside the test folder (a path only, never read), taking another test file for when Nib reopens, and Keep; step 2's summary of Sublime's settings; Nib's own keys, registered; Show Scratch's field listening with the shortcuts off, ⇧K refused, ⌃⌥⇧K taken and registered, Escape keeping, Delete clearing; keys another action has refused; keys another app holds (a helper, `scripts/hotkey-holder.swift`, holding ⌃⌥⇧J exclusively); macOS's ⌘Space; the Edit menu showing ⌃⌥⌘V, then ⌃⌥⌘B; open at login turned on and off (a stand-in, `-NibFakeLoginItem`: the real one would add a Debug build to the Mac's Login Items); Done; Help › Welcome to Nib; Settings › General's three fields; and a scratch file not made while the welcome window waits, then made when it says so. The three steps and Settings › General are saved as pictures beside the log. This Mac's own settings (done, shortcuts, the scratch file) are put back after. The suite on everything (14:13): 40 scenario runs and 256 checks, all passing, and 133 NibKit tests (counted by scenario and by check line this time; the earlier "42 runs, 235 checks" counted the runner's other headings and one line twice). No new file provider crash.

**The `nib` command-line tool (2026-09-29), AP4, §6.3.**

- **What it does,** as `subl` does: `nib file…` opens files (a relative path from the shell's folder; `file:12` and `file:12:5` go to a line and column, unless a file has that whole name); `nib -w file…` waits until they're closed, their text in the file by then (for `$EDITOR="nib -w"` and git commit messages); `cmd | nib` (or `nib -`) opens standard input as an untitled buffer (ED3), and `-w` waits for it to close; `cmd | nib -a` appends standard input to the scratch file, cleaned as Append Clipboard to Scratch cleans (SC6), without bringing Nib forward; `nib --scratch`, and `nib` alone, show the scratch window. Exit 0 when done; 1 when Nib says no, says nothing in 30 s, or quits (crashes) before a waited-on file is closed; 2 for a mistake in the arguments. Nothing is printed on success; problems go to standard error as `nib: …`, in sentences.
- **How it asks Nib:** a NibKit executable (`NibCLI`, product `nib-cli`, installed as `nib`) that never touches a synced file (the sync controls need an app, §8 M0). It writes the request whole (a rename) to `~/Library/Application Support/nib/Requests/<token>.json`, starts Nib if it isn't running (`open -b com.banastas.nib`, `-g` for `-a`), and posts a distributed notification with the one-time token each second until Nib answers. Nib takes the request by renaming it (once, whichever Nib gets there first), acts, and answers with the token (`ok`, a message, how many files it waits on); a Nib just started takes waiting requests from the folder at launch. **A waited-on file's `closed` notification comes after its window has closed and `close()` has saved and uploaded it**, or when Nib quits, after every file is saved. A request is a file, not a `nib://` URL, so that only a process on this Mac that can write to the user's Library can make one: a web page can open a URL. (§6.3 had standard input going to a temp file; it goes in the request, and Nib makes the untitled buffer from it.)
- **Under the safety rule** (§8; since 2026-10-06 only in a test run, "… in a test run, Nib opens only files in …"), a file outside the test folders isn't opened at all: the tool says "… isn't a test file: until M1 has passed, Nib opens only files in iCloud Drive › Nib › Test" and exits 1, so a `git commit` with `EDITOR="nib -w"` stops at once with git's own "problem with the editor", rather than waiting on a window that can't be edited. A folder is refused ("… is a folder: Nib opens files"); `-w` on the scratch file doesn't wait (it never closes).
- **Installing:** `scripts/install-nib-cli.sh [folder]` builds it (release) and copies it to `~/.local/bin/nib` (first on this Mac's PATH; `/usr/local/bin` isn't writable without sudo). It works with whichever Nib.app macOS knows, the Debug build until there's a release.
- **Checks:** a `cli` scenario (13 checks) running the real binary against the scenario's Nib (told never to start or bring forward Nib): `file:4` with the caret on line 4; `-w` waiting, then returning when the window closes with the typing in the file; standard input as an untitled buffer; `-a` at the end of the scratch file with the caret where it was, then undone; `-a` with nothing piped in; `--scratch`; a path outside the test folder refused with exit 1 (a path only, never read); a folder refused; `-x`, exit 2; a waiting `-w` let go when Nib quits; no request left in the folder. NibKit's `BridgeTests`: paths and lines from arguments, and a request read back as written. The suite at 14:46 (41 scenario runs, 269 checks, all passing, and 135 NibKit tests) turned out to have run an app built at 14:33, before the scenario's requests stopped bringing Nib forward (below). Built as the sources are, and with the fixes below, the suite at 15:36: 41 scenario runs and 269 checks, all passing, and 135 NibKit tests; the focus stayed with the app in front for all 41 runs; no new crash of Nib or the file provider.
- **Found while checking:** typing on the Mac during a suite run lands in the scenarios' windows. Each scenario's Nib comes forward when it starts, and two `cli` runs had two stray characters in a test file (`…second line\nea`) and a caret moved a line: keystrokes typed on the Mac mini meanwhile. Not a Nib fault, and harmless to the test files, but whatever was typed didn't reach the app it was meant for (the user confirmed typing then). **Fixed:** a scenario's Nib gives the focus back to the app that had it, at launch and whenever it comes forward (`ScenarioFocus`); only `find`, `palette` and `snapshot` take it, for the few seconds they need a key window, and each log's first line names the app keeping the focus. Sampling the front app every 0.25-0.5 s through a scenario, Nib was never in front. The tool's own requests in the scenario don't bring Nib forward either. **And** the runner's final tally didn't count `firstrun` (its results at 14:13 were printed and passed, but a failure wouldn't have failed the run); it counts `firstrun` and `cli` now, and a scenario that wrote no log at all counts as a failure. **And the app stopped being built:** from 14:33 on, `xcodebuild -scheme nib` said "BUILD SUCCEEDED" and compiled nothing of the app, now and then, so a suite ran an app older than its code. **The cause, found at 16:45:** NibKit's new executable product was called `nib`, Xcode makes a scheme for each package product, and `-scheme nib` then built that one (the command-line tool) instead of the app (the dependency graph showed three NibKit targets, not the app's 28). **Fixed:** the product is `nib-cli` (the installed command is still `nib`). And `scripts/build-nib.sh`, which both scenario scripts use, builds and then checks that the app is newer than every Swift source; if not, it builds again from a fresh build database, and if the app is still older it stops. (Before the cause was found this was put down to Xcode missing file changes on the external volume, and touching the files or removing the build database seemed to help: they only happened to leave the app's scheme picked.)
- **And ⌘F now and then left the caret in the text:** with Nib no longer in front at a scenario's start, `find` failed once in the suite and once in three runs alone (the bar open, the caret not in its field). The field asked for the caret on the next turn of the run loop, and a bar just opened may not be in its window by then. Now the field keeps the request until it's in a window. Four runs of `find` passed after; the runs that failed had Finder as the app in front, which none of the four had, so that exact case isn't shown fixed.

**Files and tabs, the rest (2026-09-29), ED2, ED4, §5.12.**

- **⌘1…⌘9** pick the window's tab by its number, as in Sublime; with fewer tabs, a beep. With the caret in a conflict block, ⌘1, ⌘2 and ⌘3 stay the Conflicts menu's (§5.4, and §5.12's "except with the caret inside a conflict block"); in a sheet (the merge view, a palette) and in a window with no tabs (the scratch window) the keys are left to the menus. Not in a menu: the Conflicts items already show ⌘1-⌘3.
- **Files dragged onto a window open**, each in its tab (a folder dragged with them is left out), as in Sublime; text dragged in, or moved inside the view, goes in as ever. To put a file's path in the text, copy it in Finder and paste (PT5).
- **File › Duplicate (⇧⌘S):** an untitled buffer (ED3) with the same text, named as Apple's apps name a copy (`notes copy.md`, then `notes copy 2.md`); Save… suggests that name, keeping its extension.
- **File › Rename… and Move To…:** a sheet with the name (its base selected, as in Finder), or a folder panel. The file is saved and uploaded first; if iCloud hasn't taken the last changes within 10 s, nothing moves ("TRY AGAIN IN A MOMENT"). Then it's closed as its window closing would close it (handed back to iCloud, so a held file is never moved, §4.3), moved with a coordinated move (off the main thread, within 20 s), and opened again under the new name. Its History, caret, syntax and known duplicates go with it, and Open Recent lists the new name, not the old. Refused, and said in the strip: another file's name ("… IS ALREADY THERE"), a file open in Nib, a name with "/" (a leading "." is allowed since 2026-10-07: `.env`, F70), an empty one ("A NAME IS NEEDED"), and anywhere outside the test folders under the safety rule. A new name by case alone (`c-` to `C-`, the same file to the Mac's disk) goes through a name in between. An untitled buffer is renamed as a new buffer of the same text and caret, the old one let go with nothing left to reopen; Move To… on one is Save…. The scratch file isn't renamed or moved (Settings › Scratch & Sync picks another).
- **Checks:** a `fileops` scenario (14 checks): four files as four tabs in one window; ⌘2, ⌘9 with four tabs; ⌘1 left to the Conflicts menu with the caret in a block, ⌘4 still a tab; the scratch window's ⌘1 left alone; Duplicate; Rename… with the caret, History and Recent entry carried over; refused onto another file's name; by case alone; Move To… refused outside the test folder (a path only), then into a folder; an untitled buffer renamed; the scratch file not moved; a file and a folder dragged in (the file opens); the File menu's items and keys.
- **Found by the suite on the way, in the merge view (§5.5):** after ⌘1, ⌘2 or ⌘3 it moves the caret to the next block by its list of blocks, and that list was brought up to date only when the editor's change notice came, which can be later: with the old ranges the caret could land between blocks, and a quick second ⌘1 only beeped (`mergeview`, three blocks resolved in a row, failed once in the suite with two left; it passed alone). Now the list is brought up to date before the caret moves.
- **The build check caught the scheme clash twice more** (in the command-line tool's entry above: the `nib` product building instead of the app), before its cause was found; the suite results here are all of an app newer than every source file.
- **The suite on everything (16:19):** 42 scenario runs and 283 checks, all passing (`mergeview` too, with the fix), and 135 NibKit tests; the focus stayed with the app in front for every run; no new crash of Nib or the file provider.

**VoiceOver and Reduce Motion (2026-09-29), AX1-AX3, A16, V4.**

- **The strip reads as one summary (AX1),** as Blit's does: its label is "scratch.md, synced at 10:42:21, line 14, column 3, Markdown" (the notice in place of the state while one shows), with the line slot and the syntax menu still reachable inside it; the indentation, encoding and line endings read "2 spaces, UTF-8, LF line endings", not with dots.

The HUD line's fade stays (A16 makes motion a fade); nothing else in Nib moves yet (the sync HUD and its CRT switch-on aren't built).

SwiftUI makes its accessibility tree only when an assistive app asks, and in a scenario the window's hosting view has no accessibility children at all, so that part needs VoiceOver itself.

The background retries' failures weren't in the Sync Log (only the first try's and, after six, "still failing"); **now each failed try is** ("not held on try 3: …"). Nothing Nib changed today touches holding.

**Delivery receipts (2026-09-29), SY14, §4.5, §4.6 (place 6).**

- **What each Mac publishes:** `<folder>/.nib/devices/<Mac>.json`, format 1: the Mac's name and, for each synced file in that folder, the SHA-256 and time (by its own clock) of the version its buffer comes from. That's set whenever it changes (a file opened, saved, fetched or merged); the file is written at most every 5 s and only when something changed, merged with what's already there (files gone from the folder, and entries over 30 days old, dropped), as the notes are written (a coordinated replace; never paused, so it travels in seconds while the documents are held), and whatever is waiting is written on quit. Files in iCloud Drive only; untitled buffers never. (§4.5 left iCloud key-value storage to S1; the file goes the way of the notes, which the M1 tests showed arrive in 3-6 s.)
- **Reading the other Macs':** a watch on the folder (as for the notes), and fresh from the server (evicted and read back) when Nib comes forward and every 30 s while it's in front. A receipt of another format is ignored, not misread (row 21).
- **What it says,** while the file is synced: **"✓ SYNCED · ON STUDIO 10:42:31"** when another Mac's receipt has this very version, and **"▼ NEWER ON AIR (10:42) · NOT HERE YET"** when one has a version this Mac hasn't seen (hasn't had, saved, moved past, merged in or kept in History). An earlier version says nothing on the strip. VoiceOver hears "Synced at 10:42:07, and on Studio since 10:42:31"; the strip's tooltip names each Mac and what it has, and that receipts come through iCloud too, so they're as late as iCloud is; the menu bar card has a line for each other Mac: `✓ ON STUDIO · SINCE 10:42:31`, `▼ NEWER ON AIR · 10:42 · NOT HERE YET`, or `AIR HASN'T GOT THIS VERSION YET · HAS 10:40:02'S`, beside its last upload from its note.
- **The Sync Log** gets `receipt` lines: "Studio has this version, 7 s after this Mac uploaded it", for a version made here (how long versions take to arrive, measured, T3, the input to V2), and "Air has a version this Mac hasn't seen: asking for the note" (§4.6's place 6, informational), which reads the note fresh in case the version is already on the server.
- **Checks:** a `receipts` scenario (6 checks), a hand-written receipt standing in for another Mac: this Mac's receipt written with its buffer's version, in 2 writes for two saves 1.5 s apart; another Mac with this version (the strip, the card, VoiceOver's words and the Sync Log's time); with a version this Mac hasn't seen; with an earlier one (the strip unchanged, a line on the card); a receipt of a later format ignored. NibKit's `ReceiptsTests` (4). **The two-Mac test** now waits at its end for each Mac to see the other's receipt with the version both converged on (its first run, at 17:50, failed that check on both Macs: below). The suite on everything (17:29): 44 scenario runs and 297 checks, all passing, and 140 NibKit tests; no new crash of Nib or the file provider.

**Two-Mac test, twentieth run (2026-09-29, 17:50), with everything since 10:55 on both Macs (the first run, the `nib` tool, files and tabs, VoiceOver, receipts): the sync passed on both, the same SHA-256** (`a948662e…`); the new receipt check failed on both. b had a's lead line the moment it came forward (20.0 s after a added it); the clash was written between markers and resolved with Keep Both on both; the apart lines merged cleanly by themselves on b ("1 change from Bill's MacBook Air onto this Mac's edits"), no block written; both files exactly the buffer, synced.

- **The receipts:** each Mac wrote its receipt for the converged version (the Mac mini at 17:52:36, the Air at 17:52:48) and each reached the other through iCloud (the Air's arrived on the Mac mini at 17:52:52, and the folder's watch saw the change), but neither Mac read the other's: **iCloud marks the files in `.nib` hidden** (the `hidden` flag, not a dot; this Mac's own file had it too), and the listing skipped hidden files. The single-Mac scenario's stand-in receipt was written on the Mac and wasn't hidden yet when read. **Fixed:** the listing skips only names starting with a dot; `ReceiptsTests` sets the hidden flag (it fails with the old listing, passes with the new), and the `receipts` scenario hides its stand-in too. Run then, the Mac mini read the Air's real receipt from the test: "Bill’s MacBook Air (earlier, a948662e)" (earlier by then: the scenario had typed since). The receipt check needs the next two-Mac run. The suite with the fix (18:05): 44 scenario runs and 297 checks, all passing, and 141 NibKit tests. (Also from the Air's build output: two compiler warnings, a capture in the receipts' folder watch and an unused variable in the `access` scenario, fixed.)

**Two-Mac test, twenty-first run (2026-09-29, 18:11), with the receipts fix on both Macs (committed at `fb87aa5`): the sync passed on both, the same SHA-256** (`3049f562…`); the receipt check passed on a and failed on b. b had a's lead line the moment it came forward (20.2 s after a added it); the clash resolved with Keep Both on both; on b the apart lines waited for the Air's note, then merged cleanly by themselves.

- **a (the Air):** "✓ SYNCED · ON M2PR0M4CM1N1 18:13:27": the Mac mini's receipt for the converged version, read.
- **b (the Mac mini):** "receipts: Bill’s MacBook Air earlier", though the Air's receipt for `3049f562…` (written 18:13:40) was on the Mac mini by 18:14:02. **The second gap:** that file was there already, and iCloud changed it in place, which leaves the folder as it was (its last change was the Mac mini's own write at 18:13:27), so the watch on the folder saw nothing. (a passed because its own receipt write changed the folder after the Mac mini's had come in.) The notes don't have this problem: a presenter hears of them ("note changed" in the Sync Log). **Fixed:** a presenter on each receipts folder too, told of a file that appears or changes, in place included. `receipts` now changes its stand-in in place with a coordinated write, checks that the folder didn't change, and passes (the strip "▼ NEWER ON TEST MAC" within a second). b's side of the receipt check needs the next two-Mac run. The suite with the presenter (18:23): 44 scenario runs and 297 checks, all passing, and 141 NibKit tests; no new crash of Nib or the file provider.

**Two-Mac test, twenty-second run (2026-09-29, 18:30), with the receipts' presenter (committed at `f1f6784`):** on b (the Mac mini) everything passed, the receipt check too: "✓ SYNCED · ON BILL’S MACBOOK AIR 18:33:07", the file `676b4b55…`. On a (the Air) everything passed too, the receipt check with it: "✓ SYNCED · ON M2PR0M4CM1N1 18:32:52"; the same SHA-256 on both. The first run with the receipts' check passing on both Macs.

- **Receipts, measured for the first time (T3):** "Bill’s MacBook Air has this version, 15 s after this Mac uploaded it", and 18 s for the last version: the whole way round (the note to the Air, its refresh, its receipt within 5 s, and the receipt back through iCloud). Three times the Air's receipt came before its note or with it, and once (01:30:17 UTC) it had Nib read the note a second before the note's own change arrived.
- **Found from it: saves waiting seconds behind note reads.** Saves in this evening's three runs took 1-11 s (at 10:55, under 0.5 s; single-Mac suites unchanged, median 5-7 ms), and then `type` failed alone: saved 4.8 s after the last keystroke (2.5 s allowed). The Sync Log now splits a slow save, and it said "34 ms writing, after 3803 ms waiting behind other file work": the document's one serial queue also read the other Macs' notes fresh (evict, then download from the server: seconds when iCloud is slow), when Nib comes forward, while it waits for a merge's note, and now on a receipt too, and a save waited behind that. (Not only today's: the run at 19:11 on 2026-09-28 had saves of 1.4-5 s.) **Fixed:** notes are read on a queue of their own; the document's reads and writes don't wait for them. `type` three times after: saved 1.0 s after the last keystroke (the debounce itself). The suite after (18:46): 44 scenario runs and 297 checks, all passing, and 141 NibKit tests; its 55 saves a median of 4 ms, at most 324 ms, none waiting behind other file work.

**§6.5, measured (2026-09-30).** M2 asks for each target "met, or its gap written down": the table in §6.5 now has both. **Met:** Show Scratch's side (p95 25 ms), keystrokes (Nib's share), keystroke to journal and to file, a new version on disk to the screen, idle CPU once launch's own work has tailed off (about 20 s; the first measure caught the tail: 0.7-0.9%), and cold launch on a quiet Mac (the suite's 34 launches: p95 274 ms; 312-393 ms while the Mac was busy, AppKit and SwiftUI taking about 240 ms before Nib's open can start). **Not met:** idle memory (45-55 MB, against 40 and 25). (A 5 MB JavaScript file and the scratch file at 2.4 MB, not met at first, were made fast the same day: below.) Not measured: glass-to-glass typing (Typometer), and the one-way save-to-load time (two round trips so far, 15 and 18 s).

- **How:** `scripts/run-perf.sh`, an optimized build with the scenarios in it, `perf` (a new scenario: each measure a MEASURED line with its target, none failing the run for a missed one) and ten `launch` runs; `bigfile` adds the reload; every scenario's first line now gives its launch time ("editable 327 ms after launch, held 1503 ms", with the phases), and the Sync Log's open line the open's steps ("iCloud's folder 0 ms · iCloud's status 5 ms · reading 4 ms · showing 7 ms").
- **Found on the way, and fixed:** saves waiting seconds behind note reads (above, the twenty-second run). And a crash, twice: Swift's check that code is on the main actor crashed (reading a bad executor) in AppKit's calls into Nib while a window was torn down, the same signature as a crash on 2026-09-29 in the text view's drawing. **The cause was an Objective-C exception unwinding through Swift code:** `perf` inserted text at a "not found" location (an `NSString` range at `NSNotFound`, `-[__NSCFString replaceCharactersInRange:withString:]: Range or index out of bounds`, logged by AppKit a few seconds before each crash), inside a Swift task; AppKit caught it, and Swift's record of the running executor was left pointing at the unwound frames, so the next check crashed. The exception was the scenario's own (fixed); the lesson is for all of Nib: an `NSString` call with a bad range, anywhere in Swift, can crash Nib later and somewhere else. (The 2026-09-29 crash in drawing likely had an exception before it too; its log is gone.)
- **Pitfalls in measuring, for next time:** ED9 highlights files up to 5,000,000 characters and none larger (the first "5 MB" file was 5,000,014 bytes: never colored); an untitled buffer is Nib's own file and nothing watches it, so another app's change to one isn't reloaded; a file just opened, or just merged, has a version iCloud hasn't confirmed, and another app's change then goes between markers rather than replacing it (by design; a test has to wait until it's synced); big files in iCloud Drive slow iCloud's daemons for minutes; a run killed while holding files leaves them for the next launch to hand back (the 6.5 s launch); and zsh's own `log` isn't `/usr/bin/log`.
- **Idle CPU and launch, looked into (16:30-17:15):** idle CPU tails off (launch's own work) over about 20 s to 0.00-0.05%, and in 20 s of `sample` nothing of Nib's ran (AppKit saving window state, a spell-checker setup); the `perf` scenario now waits for it to settle. Launch, broken down by marks in every scenario's first line (the `launch` scenario does nothing else): the window is up at about 225 ms, the text about 100 ms later; Nib's own share is about 20 ms of setup (shortcuts, the menu bar item) and about 20 ms of opening. Two changes tried and taken out again: handing back paused files beside the open (nothing was paused: no gain) and the setup queued after the open's start (it landed just as iCloud answered and held the text back). The suite's 34 launches: median 267 ms, p95 274 ms, so cold launch meets its target on a quiet Mac. And one race in a check fixed: the `merge` variant whose note comes late read the Sync Log just before its conflict line was written (it now waits for the blocks and the line together). The suite after (17:13): 44 scenario runs and 297 checks, all passing.
- **The suite with the timings (15:05):** 44 scenario runs and 297 checks, all passing, and 142 NibKit tests (a merge at 2.4 MB among them); no new crash.

**Big files, made fast (2026-09-30), §6.5.** Opening, reloading and merging big files met none of their targets; now all are met (optimized build). Each step of an open is timed (the `bigopen` scenario logs them), and the time was in Nib's own passes over the whole text, not in TextKit:

- **Detecting the indentation: 1,009 ms of a 4.95 MB file's 1,340 ms open (Debug).** It meant to look at the first 5,000 lines, but `split` made every line (about 200,000) before `prefix` took 5,000. It splits no further than it needs now, and reads the text as read, not the view's copy: 4 ms.
- **Looking for conflict blocks: 120 ms.** An edit as big as the file (an open, a paste, a reload) looked for all four kinds of marker line before the search for blocks, which looks for one: a big edit goes straight to that search, in the text storage's own string, not the view's copy: 29 ms (Debug).
- **Decoding: 84 ms,** most of it checking each byte for a carriage return in Swift: `memchr` now, under a millisecond.
- **A reload replaced the whole text** (TextKit's layout, the line index and the undo step for all of it: 303 ms at 2.4 MB, Debug). Now only what changed, the part between what the old and new texts share at the start and at the end (NibText's `TextDelta`, compared 64K UTF-16 units at a time with `memcmp`: 2.4 MB in under a millisecond; whole characters only); the caret moves along when the change is before it, and a read-only view still takes the whole text. 89 ms at 2.4 MB in iCloud Drive, optimized.
- **A merge split its three texts into lines by character** (walking graphemes: 99 of a 2.4 MB merge's 108 ms); by the line-feed byte now, the same lines (a line feed never joins a grapheme, and Nib's texts have "\n" endings): 25 ms.
- **Checks:** `TextDeltaTests` (2,000 random edits with emoji; whole surrogate pairs; nothing for the same text; 2.4 MB), `MergeSizeTests` (2.4 MB); the scenarios that replace a buffer (`undo`, `external`, `merge`, `history`) pass. The suite after (17:41): 44 scenario runs and 297 checks, all passing, and 146 NibKit tests; `run-perf.sh` then: everything met but idle memory (48.6 MB with the window, 48.7 without), and cold launch while the Mac was in use (305-392 ms; on a quiet Mac its p95 is 274 ms, above).
- **The reload, timed exactly (later the same day).** `bigfile` looked for the reloaded text every 50 ms, so the 89 ms above was a lucky look. Timed to the millisecond it was 154-167 ms, and 78 ms of it, on the main thread, was Swift's `==` comparing the file's text with the buffer's: one side is bridged from the text view's string, and `==` compares as Unicode, a character at a time. `TextDelta.same` compares UTF-16 units with `memcmp`, in under a millisecond, and exactly as a file does (a precomposed é isn't a decomposed one, which `==` calls equal); the reload and four merge paths use it. A reload is 71-89 ms now: about 40 of it the change notice coming, 35 the coordinated read and its SHA-256, off the main thread.

**Idle memory with a big file (2026-09-30), §6.5, §6.3.** With the scratch file at 2.4 MB, Nib sat at 182-201 MB idle, about 130 MB of it the syntax tree; as plain text, 64-70 MB.

- **The targets, revised (the user's call).** A bare SwiftUI app with one text view is 24.5 MB, and the scratch file stays open with its window closed (SC1, so Show Scratch is instant), so "≤ 25 MB with no windows" couldn't be met: one target now, ≤ 45 MB with a small scratch file, window open or not (43 MB measured). And a new one for the case that matters: ≤ 100 MB with the scratch file at 2.4 MB, the size scratch files reach before archiving (§2).
- **Measured first:** a whole tree costs 4-58 bytes a character. Markdown with blank lines between paragraphs 11; the harness's text, runs of lines with none, 58 (each line a lazy continuation of a list item); JavaScript 13, JSON 14, CSS 4. A real 2.4 MB scratch file's tree would be 26-130 MB, and resetting or releasing the parser frees none of it.
- **Now:** a big Markdown, JavaScript or CSS file (over 256K characters) keeps a tree of only the stretch around the view (`SyntaxDocument`): from 32K before the text it styles (the view and 12K either side) to 64K after it, about 130K, 1.5-8 MB of tree. A stretch is parsed from a place where a parse of the whole text is in its starting state too, so it reads on exactly as that parse does: in Markdown, a block in the document or a section, at the start of a line, or an item of such a list (a whole scratch file can be one list); a statement at the top of a script; a rule at the top of a style sheet. Those places are found by parsing the text 128K at a time, each tree let go once its places are taken, only as far as the view needs; they're kept and move with edits. A stretch ends on one too (looking up to 128K further), so nothing in it is cut short.
- **Edits.** Typing in the stretch reparses its tree incrementally. Past the edit, the places to start hold only if the parse falls back in step with the one they came from (the same three places at the stretch's end): typing a word does, typing ``` turns the rest of the file into code. If it doesn't, they're unsure from there and looked at again (a chunk at a time, until back in step) when the view goes there. An edit before the stretch marks it unchecked; if its start is still a place to start once looked at, it stays, two characters along for two typed.
- **Where a stretch cuts something off** (a block longer than its look ahead): tree-sitter reads a /* with no */ as code, a template string's ` with no end as one stray character, and Markdown's front matter with no closing --- as text, and found places to start inside them. A stretch that stops short is read with what closes them after it (`closer`: a line `*/` then a backtick in JavaScript, a line `*/` in CSS, a `---` and a `+++` line in Markdown), and an embedded language cut off with its own. The root scope spans the whole text, as in a parse of the whole (a fence cut off ran on past the stretch's end and sorted outside it), and a capture's text comes from the stretch's own string (a comment that ran into the closer lost `comment.block.js`: its `#match?` read past the end of the text).
- **Not for JSON, HTML or shell scripts:** a tree of the whole at any size (to ED9's 5 MB). Inside an object a JSON key reads as a string; inside an element, HTML's end tag reads as a stray one; and tree-sitter-bash carries state from one command to the next (a parse started at one took the line after a pipeline for more of it). Files that big in those languages are rare, and the memory is as before.
- **Where it can still differ from a tree of the whole:** while JavaScript has a syntax error near the view, tree-sitter's repair can depend on how much text follows (a stray ` flips every template string after it in the stretch, where with the whole file after it tree-sitter skips the one character instead; Sublime shows the rest as a string too, until it's closed); a heading's # typed alone at the very end of a file with no heading before it in the stretch reads as plain until its first letter (tree-sitter-markdown, as a tree of the whole does in a file with no headings); and a fence longer than the look ahead is read to the stretch's end.
- **Checks:** `WindowedTests`: stretches against a tree of the whole for all six languages, scrolling down, jumping and back up (small limits, so 40K is many stretches); editing as people do (an edit or two that can break the syntax, undone, a construct added or taken out, the view moving), exactly as the whole whenever the whole text parses cleanly, Markdown always; the same at real sizes (400K, the editor's 30K window); a 2.4 MB scratch file (typing at the end grows the stretch with the text; an edit at the top leaves it as it was). Once, 18,000 such edits (60 seeds, three sets of limits) all passed. The fidelity check is unchanged (99.86%).
- **Measured (optimized):** idle with the 2.4 MB file 67.8 MB once settled (77.6 in the first 40 s), flat over three minutes (the slow growth seen before is gone), 64.5 MB as plain text; opening, the top colored at 171-183 ms (2.4 MB of Markdown) and 242-247 ms (4.95 MB of JavaScript), from 545-549 and 658-743; the end of 2.4 MB, jumped to, 481-529 ms; a heading typed at the end colored 18 ms after typing stops.

**Receipts a day old (2026-09-30), SY14.** The suite after the windowed trees failed two `receipts` checks: the strip said "▼ NEWER ON BILL'S MACBOOK AIR (18:33) · NOT HERE YET", from the Air's receipt of the evening before for the test scratch file, version `676b4b55…`, which the Mac mini had had too (the twenty-second run converged on it).

- **Why:** "a version this Mac hasn't seen" meant one History doesn't keep, and History thins what's more than a day old (the newest of each hour, to a week). `676b4b55…`'s hour had later versions, so it went, and a Mac that last loaded the file a day before read as having a newer one. In use: the Air left on yesterday's version, and the Mac mini's strip saying the Air has a newer one, until the Air opened the file again.
- **Now:** History remembers the SHA-256 of each version it lets go, for its 30 days (`thinned.txt` beside its index), so whether this Mac had a version holds as long as History covers; `contains` still means the text can come back. And a reader ignores another Mac's entries older than 30 days, as their writer already drops them.
- **The scenario** uses a file of its own now: the real Macs' receipts name the scratch file, and the Air's had taken the strip from the stand-in. On this Mac, the Air's receipt for `676b4b55…` still reads as newer until the Air writes another: that version was let go before History remembered what it thins.
- **Checks:** `thinsByAge` (a version let go is still one this Mac had, and not after 30 days); `receipts` passes (its 6 checks). The suite after (20:28): 45 scenario runs and 297 checks, all passing, and 152 NibKit tests.

**Two-Mac test, twenty-third run (2026-09-30, 21:28), with the windowed trees, `TextDelta.same` and the receipts fix (committed at `379f8fd`): everything passed on both Macs, the same SHA-256** (`ef5aa436…`), receipts included: a's strip "✓ SYNCED · ON M2PR0M4CM1N1 21:30:18", b's "✓ SYNCED · ON BILL’S MACBOOK AIR 21:30:30". b had a's line 19 s after a added it, once it came forward; the clash was kept both and resolved on both; the apart phase merged by itself on b (1 change from the Air onto its edits), with no block. The Air's receipt for the test file is current again, so the Mac mini's stale "newer" (above) is gone.

**The journal's edit log (2026-09-30), SY1, §4.3, §4.9 row 24.** Each pause in typing journaled the whole text: in a 2.4 MB scratch file, 2.5 MB written and fsynced every pause (about 10 MB/s while typing), its SHA-256 worked out on the main thread each time (about 8 ms). And a save that left nothing unsaved deleted the journal, so the next pause wrote a whole snapshot again.

- **Now,** over a million characters (`Journal.logAbove`): a whole snapshot at most once a minute (or once its log reaches 1 MB), and at each pause only what changed since the last write (`TextDelta`), a line appended to the log beside the snapshot and fsynced. The log's first line names the snapshot it follows (one left by a crash between the two names the one before, and is ignored); a torn last line (a crash mid-append) is ignored, so the text is as of the pause before. A save that leaves nothing unsaved adds a line saying so, in place of deleting the journal; a document closing with nothing unsaved deletes it, and one opening deletes what has nothing to recover. Small texts are as before, a snapshot each pause.
- **Off the main thread:** the text is taken there, and its SHA-256 (a snapshot's) is worked out on the journal's queue.
- **A save while typing goes on now tells the journal its new version** at once (a base line, or a snapshot of a small text). Until the next pause, the journal said the edits were from the version before, and a crash then came back as both versions between markers, nearly the same text twice. A save on losing focus in `crash-big` showed it: the buffer came back as recovered typing, no conflict.
- **Checks:** six new `JournalTests` (30 random edits with emoji in a 1.2 MB text, read back as written, the snapshot written once, the log under 8 KB; a torn last line; a clean save leaving nothing to recover, and the next typing journaled from the new version; a snapshot after a minute; the version moving with a save; a text growing past the size and back); two new scenarios, `crash-big` (a 2.4 MB untitled buffer, typing over six pauses, then SIGKILL) and `recover-big` (it comes back whole and is saved).
- **Measured:** six pauses' typing in the 2.4 MB buffer, 1,136-1,243 bytes appended to the log (whole snapshots would have been about 15 MB); a keystroke to the journal 259-262 ms, most of it the 250 ms wait for a pause.

**Failure modes, SY12 (2026-09-30), §4.8 E, §4.9 (rows 8-13).** What another app, a full iCloud or a full disk does to an open file.

- **Moved or renamed by another app (rows 9, 10).** It was "MOVED · QUIT AND REOPEN NIB", every save failing meanwhile. Now the buffer follows it: its text is journaled under the new path, its caret, syntax and History are carried (as Move To… carries them), the document lets the file go without saving, uploading or handing it back at the old path, where nothing is, and the file opens again where it is, held as one left paused (the pause goes with the file). Typing not in the file yet comes back from the journal, the caret where it was (recovery used to put it at the end of the text: fixed for every recovery). Its folder renamed: the same, the file's presenter told of it. The scratch file, or a move out of the test folders (§8), only says where it went.
- **Deleted, or moved to the Trash, by another app (row 8, E).** Finder's Move to Trash is a move, into the Trash, and counts as a deletion. A banner, "✕ IN THE TRASH" or "✕ DELETED", with **Restore** and **Close**, and the strip in red; saves and uploads stop, nothing makes the file again, and typing goes on into the journal. Restore writes the text back where the file was (if a file is there again by then, that one's taken in, not written over), holds it once iCloud has it (§4.3) and uploads it. Close, or closing the window, keeps the text in History, on File › Reopen Closed File, as an untitled buffer's is. Reopened (⇧⌘T), it's written back where the file was; with a file there again by then, that one opens as it is, and the text comes back beside it as an untitled buffer, "notes (closed).md", unless it's the same text (2026-10-03). The scratch file has Restore only. A file there again (an app that saves by deleting and making it anew) is taken as that app's version. A save that finds the file gone counts as a deletion too: after a restore, the file's presenter was still on the copy in the Trash, and missed the next one (it's put back on the file's path now).
- **iCloud storage full (row 12):** its own red line, "ICLOUD STORAGE IS FULL · SAVED LOCALLY ONLY · RETRYING" (Foundation's `NSUbiquitousFileNotUploadedDueToQuotaError`, or the file provider's insufficient quota), where it was "ICLOUD ISN'T TAKING UPLOADS"; the upload is tried again on the backoff, to 5 minutes.
- **This Mac's disk full (row 13):** "DISK FULL · YOUR LATEST CHANGES ARE ONLY IN MEMORY · FREE SPACE OR COPY THEM" when the journal or the save can't write for want of space. The save was tried again every 15 s already; the journal is now too, typing or not.
- **iCloud Drive turned off, or signed out (row 11), built against a simulation** (turning it off for real is the user's to do, and how macOS takes the files away then isn't measured yet). The file's removal comes as a deletion with no iCloud account on the Mac (`ubiquityIdentityToken` nil): the banner says "✕ ICLOUD OFF", the strip "ICLOUD DRIVE IS OFF ON THIS MAC · SAVING TO NIB'S RECOVERY FOLDER", and saves go to `Application Support/nib/Recovery/<the file's key>/<its name>`, never to the file's place, and never counted as saved (the file hasn't the text). The banner's Show in Finder shows that copy. When the account is back (`NSUbiquityIdentityDidChange`, then every 5 s for 5 minutes while iCloud brings its files down, and whenever Nib comes forward), the file is taken in as one there again after a deletion is: the same version as before, and the typing is saved into it; another, and it's merged. Once the file has the text, the Recovery copy goes. A quit meanwhile leaves the text in the Recovery folder and in History, on Reopen Closed File, not brought back by itself.
- **Not yet:** row 13's notification and Empty Trash; row 7's ◐ while an evicted file downloads, and F (offline, the shadow copy). And a deletion or a move made on another Mac: iCloud doesn't change a held file, and how it brings either to a paused one isn't known yet (a two-Mac probe), nor how macOS removes the files when iCloud Drive is turned off for real.
- **Checks:** three new scenarios. `moved`: a rename, and then its folder's, typing not saved yet: followed both times, caret and History carried, saved where it is now, nothing at the old path, synced. `deleted`: moved to the Trash: the banner, nothing made at the path, Restore, held and synced again; then deleted outright and closed: the text on Reopen Closed File, no journal left. `full`: storage full: saved here, red until there's room, then synced; disk full: red, and saved within 15 s of room again, nothing typed. `icloudoff`: the file gone with no iCloud account: the banner and the strip, saved to the Recovery folder and nowhere else; the account back and the file with it: the typing in the file, held and synced, the Recovery copy gone.
- **In the suite, near the end.** Renaming folders, and moving files to the Trash or deleting them, in iCloud Drive left its daemons busy for a minute or more: every pause in the `merge` after them answered "Resource busy", and right after the folder rename in `moved`, iCloud said the file was paused when it wasn't, refused uploads (no permission, then "not supported") and its helper stopped answering; Nib's retries got it synced in 9 s on a quiet Mac, not within 30 s on that busy one. So `moved`, `deleted` and `icloudoff` run just before `bigfile`, which is last for the same reason. The suite after (22:48): 51 scenario runs and 323 checks, all passing, and 158 NibKit tests.

**Two-Mac test, twenty-fourth run (2026-10-01, 11:01), with the journal's edit log and the failure modes (committed at `94f0536`): everything passed on both Macs, the same SHA-256** (`f9a412c9…`), receipts included: a's strip "✓ SYNCED · ON M2PR0M4CM1N1 11:03:23", b's "✓ SYNCED · ON BILL’S MACBOOK AIR 11:03:30". b had a's line 18 s after a added it, once it came forward; the clash was kept both and resolved on both; in the apart phase b waited for the Air's note, then merged by itself (1 change from the Air onto its edits), with no block. (The run set for 10:50 didn't count: b ran alone, and was stopped.)

**Multiple carets, ED8 (2026-10-01).** Sublime's set, on Sublime's keys, in a Selection menu: ⌘-click adds a caret (on one, takes it away; held, it selects), ⌥-drag makes a column (a selection a line, a caret at the end of a line too short), ⌘D selects the caret's word and then each next occurrence (whole words, case and all, when it began from a caret; any occurrence of a selection the user made), ⌃⌘G every occurrence, ⇧⌘L splits a selection into its lines, ⌃⇧↑ and ⌃⇧↓ add a caret on the line above or below, and Escape (or Selection › One Caret) goes back to one: the primary, the one the strip's line and column follow (the last added, where a drag ended, or Find All's first match after the caret). Find All (⌥Return with the find bar open, or its button) selects every match, closes the bar and says "12 SELECTED".

- **At every caret:** typing (a run of it one undo step), Return with each line's indentation, Tab, deleting (a character, a word, to the line's start or end), the arrows with ⌥, ⌘ and ⇧ (↑ and ↓ keep each caret's column past shorter lines), copy, cut and paste (as many lines as carets, a line each, as Sublime does; else all of it at each), and the line commands: Indent, Outdent, Toggle Comment, Duplicate Line, Select Line, Join Lines, Move Line Up and Down. A line is changed once however many carets are on it, and each caret keeps its place in its text; Join Lines from a column of carets makes all their lines one, and carets on touching lines move as a block. Tab with a selection over several lines at any caret indents at all of them, ⇧Tab outdents. Two carets that meet (deleting back from side-by-side carets, moving to a line's start) become one; selections that only touch stay two (⌘D on "aa" in "aaaa").
- **How:** `NSTextView` has one insertion point: given several empty selected ranges it keeps one, and TextKit 2's text selections don't drive its editing (probed). So Nib keeps its own: NibText's `Carets` does the arithmetic (each edit at every caret, made in the text as it is, and where every caret goes; `LineEdit.shifts` says where Indent and Toggle Comment changed each line). The text view keeps its own selection on the primary caret, so the strip's line and column, scrolling and Find follow it, draws the others (the system's insertion indicator at each, their selections under the text, each caret's line highlighted and its number bright in the gutter; only those in view, so drawing Find All's 10,000 costs a screenful's), and makes each edit as one change (`shouldChangeText(inRanges:)`), with undo steps of its own that put every caret back. An undo whose text changed under it since (a version from another Mac taken in without an undo step) beeps and is dropped rather than garble the text. Anything not done at several carets (⌘A, a plain click, Page Down, an input method composing) goes back to one caret, then works as ever. The strip adds "· 3 CARETS".
- **Measured:** Find All on 2,000 matches in 84 KB of highlighted JavaScript, 40 ms; then a keystroke at all 2,000 carets, 67 ms on the main thread, and a delete, 69 ms (Debug). The keystroke was 2.9 s: the editor moves the syntax colors with the text until the next highlight, and did it once for each caret's edit, over every color; now in one pass for all the edits. That code also stretched a color ending before an edit up to the edit, until the next highlight (seen only when something drew in between, the find bar open, say): fixed.
- **Not yet:** ⌘K ⌘D (skip this occurrence; the ⌘K chords are open question 10) and soft undo (⌘U, the last caret change taken back); VoiceOver hears the primary caret only (the strip's summary says how many); the merge view's text and Go to Line have one caret.
- **Checks:** `CaretsTests` (11: Sublime's merging, typing, deleting with emoji and combining accents, moving, adding and removing, splitting into lines, occurrences, the line commands at several carets, the shifts, and 500 random edits checked against making each alone); the `carets` scenario, 32 checks: the menu's keys, typing at three carets and one undo for it all, ⌘D past "rename", ⇧⌘L with ⌘→, ⌃⇧↓, the arrows with ⇧, copy and paste a line each, side-by-side carets deleting into one, each line command, Tab and ⇧Tab, Find All, Escape, a click, ⌘-click on and off, ⌥-drag, and the 2,000 carets, timed, with their colors drawn where the text went and the tree right to the end.
- **Found by the suite meanwhile:** the wait for the other Mac's note (§4.6) read the note fresh, which waits for iCloud to bring it down, with no time limit; with iCloud's daemons slow, one read held the 10 s wait past 15 s, and `merge-no-note` saw no merge. Each read now gets what's left of the 10 s (`CloudControl.within`); a note that comes later still narrows the blocks. An untitled buffer a scenario left open (its close not finished when Nib quit) came back in the next scenarios' windows (`fileops` counted 6 tabs): `carets` waits for its buffers to close. And Nib's list of the files it holds paused, which the next launch hands back (§4.9, 14a), kept files that were gone (deleted, or taken away with iCloud Drive: `moved` and `icloudoff` left six), trying them again at every launch; a file that's gone is dropped from it now.
- **The suite after (12:36):** 51 scenario runs and 356 checks, all passing, and 169 NibKit tests (the paused list's fix came after it, and was checked alone: the list empty after the next launch).

**Two-Mac test, twenty-fifth run (2026-10-01, 12:50), with multiple carets, the note wait's time limit and the paused list's fix (committed at `7bafa90`): everything passed on both Macs, the same SHA-256** (`89a03959…`), receipts included: a's strip "✓ SYNCED · ON M2PR0M4CM1N1 12:52:21", b's "✓ SYNCED · ON BILL’S MACBOOK AIR 12:52:28". b had a's line 20 s after a added it, once it came forward: the Air's receipt named a version b hadn't seen, b asked for the note, and a forced refresh brought the version in 3.0 s. The clash merged from the version both had, one place between markers, kept both and resolved on both. Just after, an upload of b's resolution failed with Cocoa error 512 ("couldn't be saved"), and the next was refused with iCloud saying nothing was newer, so Nib refreshed, got its own version back, saved the work again and uploaded it, 17 s after the failure (§4.3). In the apart phase each note came before its merge, so the note wait, and its new time limit, wasn't needed: b merged by itself, 1 change from the Air onto its edits, with no block. The Air had each of b's versions 9-20 s after b uploaded it.

**The test harness apart from the Nib in daily use (2026-10-01).** Before the user's own scratch file comes into Nib (CLAUDE.md's item 2), the scenarios had to stop sharing everything with the Nib in daily use. Both scripts deleted `Application Support/nib/Journal/*.json` as they started, which would be the user's crash-recovery journal. The scenarios wrote to the same History, Sync Log, untitled buffers, closed files and paused list, and a scenario's Nib, at launch, would have handed back to iCloud every paused file that wasn't its own (the user's held scratch file among them) and reopened the user's untitled buffers. Some scenarios turn settings off and back on (uploads held, the menu bar extra), and one killed half way leaves them off. Test files went into Open Recent and the Dock's list, and a scenario's Nib registered the same global shortcuts and the `nib` tool's bridge as the user's.

Now a launch with `-NibScenario` (every script's) is the test harness's Nib (`NibPaths.isScenario`), and keeps apart:
- **Its folder:** `~/Library/Application Support/nib-scenarios/`, for the journal, History, Sync Log, untitled buffers, closed files, the paused list, imported themes and the bridge's requests. The scripts clean that journal, not the user's.
- **Its settings:** the defaults domain `com.banastas.nib.scenarios` (`NibDefaults.store`, which every read and write of Nib's settings now goes through, `@AppStorage` too). Launch arguments and registered defaults are seen through it as before (checked).
- **The Mac's:** no global shortcuts (but for `firstrun`'s own, let go at its end), no menu bar extra (but for `menubar`), the login item always its stand-in, nothing in the system's recent files, and the bridge on names of its own (`com.banastas.nib.scenarios.*`), which the `cli` scenario's `nib` tool uses (NIB_SCENARIOS). Its windows don't save their places: SwiftUI keeps them in the app's own defaults, under a name it gives each window as it makes it, before Nib sees the window, so a scenario's Nib has AppKit's frame saving do nothing (`ScenarioWindows`, Debug only). The names stay, so its windows open at the sizes the user's have.
- **The Nib in daily use:** `scripts/install-nib.sh` builds a Release, team-signed as the Debug build is, stamps its build number with the time (higher than the Debug build's 3, so macOS opens files with it), and installs it as `/Applications/Nib.app`, refusing while it runs. The `nib` tool starts that copy when Nib isn't running. Not Developer ID signed or notarized (DS1).
- **Still shared:** the scenarios' test file is the scratch file of any Nib that hasn't the live one yet (the day test's), so the suite can't run beside such a Nib. Once the user's scratch is `Nib/scratch.md`, the two hold different files, with their notes and receipts in different folders. While both run, Finder's opens and the Dock reach whichever Nib macOS picks.
- **Checks:** the suite after the day test (15:00), with the user's Nib's folder and settings taken before and after. The folder was untouched (3,650 files, the same sizes and times); the settings had changed in one place, a file window's saved frame: a scenario's cascaded window had saved its place under the name SwiftUI gave it. A first fix took the names away. That run failed 11 checks in five scenarios, which I put down to the fix, and keeping the names (stopping only the saving) then passed; the real cause was a cascade under load (below, 18:56), and the A/B behind the blame was confounded by the blocks it left. Stopping only the saving stays, the smaller change, and the frame was put back as it was. The third run (15:31): 51 scenario runs and 370 checks, all passing, 173 NibKit tests, and the user's folder and settings as they were. (The system's recent files can't be compared from a shell, whose access to that folder macOS refuses; Nib's own Open Recent is in its settings.)
- **The leftovers cleared (16:18, the user's call):** on the Mac mini, what the test runs had left in the user's folder and settings before the separation went to the Trash, in one folder with a copy of the settings as they were (`scripts/clean-test-leftovers.swift`, a dry run without --remove; nothing erased): 1,251 History folders (530 MB), the 7 MB Sync Log, 4 journal files, 2 empty untitled-buffer folders, 1,846 per-file settings, Open Recent's 7 entries and Reopen Closed File's 19, and 64 old entries in iCloud Drive/Nib/Test. Kept: the window places, the first run done, and a caret place for one of the user's own files. The Air's are the user's to clear, with the same script.

**Evicted and offline, §4.9 row 7 and §4.8 F (2026-10-01).** A file Optimize Mac Storage has taken off this Mac.
- **Online:** Nib asks iCloud for it and opens it once it's here, ◐ NOT ON THIS MAC · DOWNLOADING meanwhile. It's never a coordinated read that waits on the download: offline that waited for good, and at open it had no time limit, against §4.3's rule. The status is asked instead, each time within its limit, for up to 20 s.
- **Offline, or not here in 20 s:** the copy Nib saw last, History's newest version of the file (§6.4's shadow copy, so no store of its own), slightly dimmed, the strip saying `◐ OFFLINE · SHOWING THE COPY FROM 16:38 · EDITS MERGE WHEN ICLOUD RETURNS`. Typing is allowed and journaled, and nothing reads, writes or holds the file until it's here (saves, disk checks and the hold wait). Nib asks for it again whenever this Mac is online, and once it's here takes it in as any version from iCloud (§4.4): the same as the copy, and the typing is saved over it; another, merged with the typing from the copy's version (the copy's version counts as uploaded, so another Mac's note naming it as the parent proves the base, §4.6), or between markers without that proof. Then it's held, as any open file.
- **A file Nib never saw on this Mac, offline:** no copy, so the window waits, `‖ NOT ON THIS MAC · OFFLINE · IT OPENS WHEN ICLOUD RETURNS`, and opens when it comes.
- **Also:** offline, Nib no longer reads another Mac's note fresh (a fresh read evicts the note and waits for it to download again), and that read now has a time limit (10 s), as §4.3 has for every iCloud call. The welcome window's step 1 suggests Finder's Keep Downloaded for iCloud Drive › Nib: the SDK can't keep one file downloaded (M0).
- **Checks:** `evicted` (10): taken off this Mac by FileManager's eviction (as `brctl evict` does); online, it opened in 0.9-3.2 s, the main thread's longest wait 25-28 ms; offline, the copy, dimmed and editable, the typing journaled and the file left alone, and a version arriving meanwhile not taken in; iCloud back, merged ("✓ MERGED 1 CHANGE FROM TEST MAC"), saved, held and synced; and a file never opened here waiting, then opening. "Offline" is the scenario's say-so (`TestHarness.offline`), not the network turned off.
- **Found by the suite:** the file's arrival cleared "awaiting the download" before asking iCloud its status and reading the note, and a disk check took the version in first, with no note: both whole versions went between markers, narrowed a moment later when the note came. It's cleared only once the version is taken in now. And `palette` had leaned on earlier runs' files in the test folder for its Nib Folder group: once those were cleared away the group came up empty, so it makes a file of its own. `evicted`'s check that Nib leaves the file alone reads Nib's own Sync Log, not iCloud's download state: just after a hand-back, iCloud brought an evicted file back by itself.
- **The suite after (17:08):** 52 scenario runs and 380 checks, all passing, and 173 NibKit tests. The last scenario's quit (`bigfile`, iCloud's daemons slow after its 2.4 MB) didn't finish handing the test file back, so it's left on the scenarios' paused list for the next launch to hand back, as designed.
- **Not yet:** a file evicted while it's open (not seen, not tested); the real network off, which is the user's to try on a laptop.

**Folders that came back (2026-10-01).** Clearing the test folder showed scenarios' folders coming back with only `.nib` in them. This Mac's receipt (written every few seconds for a folder that had a file open) and the notes made `.nib` with every folder above it, so a folder removed, whether by a scenario, by the user, or moved away, came back as soon as Nib next wrote a receipt or a note for it. Now they make `.nib` only in a folder that's still there, and a receipt waiting for a folder that's gone is dropped, not retried every 5 s. Found with it: `untitled` left its saved file each run (it ends in SIGKILL, so its `defer` never ran), and `evicted` removed its folder while its windows were still closing; both clear up before they end now.

**Two-Mac remote probe (2026-10-01, 18:18), SY12's last question: all four checks passed** (`scripts/run-m1-two-mac.sh a|b HH:MM remote`, scenario `two-mac-remote`). On the Air (a), four test files open and held; on the Mac mini (b), as Finder would, at 18:19:00 one moved to the Trash, one deleted, one renamed, and the folder of the fourth renamed.
- **iCloud brings all of them to a held file**, with nothing coming forward: on a, the trashed file was gone from its place 3.3 s after, the deleted one 10.5 s after, and the renamed one and the renamed folder's file were at their new places 4.4 s after. Holding a file doesn't keep another Mac's deletes or renames from it.
- **Nib's handling of another app's changes on this Mac serves as is:** the deleted banner for both deletions, and the buffer following both moves, open, synced and held again at the new place 5.4 s after (the hold goes with the file: "left paused by an earlier session").
- **A move to the Trash on another Mac arrives here as a deletion** (that Trash is the other Mac's), and nothing says which Mac did it, so the banner and the notification, which said "by another app", say "deleted, by another app or on another Mac" now. §4.8 E's banner naming the Mac ("MOVED TO THE TRASH ON STUDIO") would need the other Mac to say so, and no note is written for a deletion; the other Mac's Nib may not even be running.

**A cascade under load (2026-10-01, 18:56).** Two full suites failed the same 11 checks in five scenarios (`crash-blocks`, `recover-blocks`, `merge`, `mergeview`, `refusal`), each time with the user working on the Mac, and every one passed alone. The first link: `crash-blocks` resolves its block, waits a fixed 600 ms for the journal, checks, and kills itself (SIGKILL) to crash. On a busy Mac the journal came later, the check failed, and the block stayed in the shared test file; every block scenario after it then met a block that wasn't its own (`mergeview` resolved the stranger first, as the first block in the file). Now `crash-blocks` waits for the journal (up to 5 s), its check names each of its four conditions, and `mergeview` starts from its own first block. The suite after (18:56): 52 scenario runs and 380 checks, all passing, 173 NibKit tests, the paused list empty and the test folder clean.

**Large files, ED9 (2026-10-01).** Measured by the new `large` scenario (Debug).
- **Up to 5 MB (5,000,000 characters), every feature.** A 4.9 MB JavaScript file opened in 119-125 ms, highlighted (its colors at 5 MB are §6.5's); the feature most at risk at that size, Find All, took 10,000 matches (the most at once) in 29-30 ms, then a keystroke at all of them 219-225 ms and a delete 217-221 ms.
- **5 to 100 MB: no highlighting**, as before but now said on the strip, in the syntax's place: `HIGHLIGHTING OFF · 42 MB` (and to VoiceOver). Still editable: 42 MB opened in 604-608 ms, a keystroke cost 19-20 ms on the main thread, and a save 151-152 ms, which holds the main thread up to 159-166 ms (the text is copied and encoded there; the write and the SHA-256 aren't). Nib peaked at 491 MB of memory with it open.
- **Above 5 MB, lighter on disk and memory:** History keeps the version opened, merges, other Macs' versions and restores, and the version closed with, not every save (at 42 MB a save a pause would be gigabytes a day); and the versions kept in memory for merges are two, not eight (eight copies of a 42 MB text were 670 MB). A merge with no version both had kept falls back to both whole versions between markers, as anywhere.
- **Over 100 MB, asked first:** "“big.log” is 240 MB. Open it?", Open or Cancel, from every way a file opens (⌘O, Open Recent, Finder, the Dock, Go to File, the `nib` tool, which on Cancel says why and doesn't wait). Nib's own untitled buffers aren't asked about.
- **Not yet:** question 17's real sizes may move the limits; a save above 5 MB still holds the main thread for its copy and encode (150 ms at 42 MB), which could move off it.
- **The suite after (22:58):** 53 scenario runs and 386 checks, all passing, 173 NibKit tests.

**History keeps the newest save of each 5 minutes, open question 22 (2026-10-01, the user's call: b).** A save comes 1 s after typing stops or every 5 s of steady typing, and History kept a whole copy of each for a day: up to 720 copies an hour, 1.7 GB an hour of steady typing at the scratch file's 2.4 MB.
- **Now:** of the saves, the newest of each 5 minutes goes in (a span starts at its first save), at the span's end, with the time it was saved. Every version opened, merged (blocks written, the merge view and Revert To too), from another Mac or another app, restored or closed with goes in at once, as before. That's at most 12 saves an hour, 29 MB an hour at 2.4 MB. Above 5 MB, none of the saves between (ED9).
- **The save waiting for its span's end** goes in first whenever anything else does, so History stays in order. It also goes in at a close or a quit, which now waits for it: it was a task a quitting Nib could cut off, and ED9's closing version had the same gap. A file another app moves takes it along with its History.
- **Every save still counts as one this Mac had** (`History.noteSeen`: a line in `thinned.txt` for History's 30 days, as a version thinning lets go gets). So a receipt naming a save that wasn't kept isn't news (SY14), and an iCloud conflict version or a duplicate file identical to one isn't a conflict: that check looked at History's copies only (the text goes into History then, as before). `thinned.txt` is searched as bytes now, not split into lines; it holds about as many lines as before, a line for each save, written at once rather than after a day.
- **Merges don't need those copies:** a merge's base is this Mac's last upload (or, for blocks written two-way, the one before them), and the versions kept in memory for merges never let either go now (History's copy was the fallback when they did).
- **Checks:** `history`, with a span of seconds: two saves in one span, of which only the newer goes in, at the span's end, with its save time; the other still counts as had; Revert To goes in at once. A new `history-closed`: the save Nib quit with, waiting for its span, is in History at the next launch. And a NibKit test: a save not kept counts as had for 30 days, through thinning. Both scenarios passed alone (23:32), then in the suite after (23:34): 54 scenario runs and 391 checks, all passing, 174 NibKit tests; a Release build compiles.

**Undo Selection, Skip to Next Occurrence, and saves encoded off the main thread (2026-10-02, overnight).**
- **Undo Selection (⌘U) and Redo Selection (⇧⌘U)**, in the Selection menu: the last change to the carets or the selection taken back, and put back, as VS Code's cursor undo does. Sublime's soft undo also steps back through edits; ⌘Z does those here. Every change counts: a key, a click (a drag as one, at its end), ⌘D, ⇧⌘L, ⌘-click, Find All. The steps are places in the text as it was, so an edit starts them over, from the carets it leaves once its event is done: an edit sets the selection more than once on its way (⌘Z puts the text back, then the carets), and none of those is a step. At most 50 steps, and 20,000 carets in them all (NibText's `CaretHistory`, 4 tests).
- **Skip to Next Occurrence** (Sublime's ⌘K ⌘D): the selection ⌘D added last goes, and the next occurrence comes in its place; from a caret, its word, as ⌘D. It's in the Selection menu and the palette with no key: ⌘K is Go to File's second key (§5.12), and whether Nib takes Sublime's ⌘K chords is open question 10. Until then it has no key, as other commands with none in Apple's or Sublime's set (KB1; "the one menu command without a key" wasn't so: the QA review's F49).
- **Saves encode the text on the file queue,** not the main thread (ED9's last "not yet"). During a 42 MB save the main thread's longest wait was 163-172 ms; it's 28-39 ms now, the keystroke before it included (18-19 ms), and what's left is the text's copy. At the scratch file's 2.4 MB the encoding held the main thread about 9 ms a save.
- **The version closed with went into History last, and a slow quit lost it.** The suite at 00:12 failed `history-closed`: iCloud was slow (holds of 37 s, after `bigfile` runs just before it), the quit's upload and hand-back took all of its 10 s (the watchdog, §4.3), and History's write, at the end of closing, never came. It comes first now, before iCloud's steps, and a save the closing itself makes goes in at once. `-NibSlowQuit` makes the suite's `history` quit that way every run (cut off after its save): the old order fails `history-closed` ("saved, saved, opened"), the new one passes.
- **`files`, in the same suite,** didn't see its two new files held within its 60 s, for the same slowness (it passed alone at 00:30, held in 6 s); its message said "held at once" for files not held, and says "not held in 60 s" now.
- **`bigfile` left its file paused** (the suite's "Paused files left behind", at 22:58 and 23:34): it waited for the file to leave Nib's list, which happens as its closing begins, then removed it while the closing was still saving, uploading and handing it back. It waits for the hand-back now. The next launch had dropped the entry, as one for a file that's gone.
- **Checks:** `carets`: Skip to Next Occurrence; ⌘U back through each change to the carets the last edit left, and no further; ⇧⌘U forward again; typing starts them over; a single caret's arrow moves; the menu's keys. `large`: the main thread's wait during the 42 MB save. `bigfile`: the paused list empty after it, in two runs that held the file and handed it back.
- **The suite after the fixes (00:31):** 54 scenario runs and 397 checks, all passing, nothing left paused; 178 NibKit tests; a Release build compiles. During the 42 MB save, the main thread's longest wait was 21 ms.
