**The minimap, ED11 (2026-10-02, the user's call).** Asked for on the first day of daily use, after Sublime's; it had been among §1's non-goals.
- **What it is:** a 100-point strip between the text and the scroller (`nib/Views/Minimap.swift`, tiled in by the editor's scroll view, `EditorScrollView`). The text is at a seventh of the editor's size, each word a bar in its syntax color, with conflict blocks in Blit's muted red and the lines in view under a box, brighter under the mouse and while held. A click brings the line drawn there to the middle of the view; the box, held, follows the mouse, and the text with it. A text taller than the minimap moves in it as the text scrolls, the box going from its top to its bottom, as VS Code's does. View › Hide Minimap and Settings › Editor turn it off; the merge view's panes have none; a window with less than 200 points of text beside it doesn't show one.
- **How:** its rows are its own, one per the editor's wrap width in characters, worked out from the line index's starts after an edit or a resize, so no text out of view has to be laid out. It draws only the rows it shows, from the text storage's own string, never a copy. Its colors are the editor's styles, whose window now takes in what the minimap shows (up to 200,000 characters round the view). A click or a drag lays out only the line it goes to. Nothing touches `layoutManager`.
- **Measured (Debug):** a draw 3-4 ms (about 500 rows). In 1.9 MB of JavaScript: a draw 3 ms, a jump to its middle 3-4 ms, the slowest of ten keystrokes 6-7 ms (the minimap only notes a keystroke, and draws a tenth of a second later). `bigfile` at 2.4 MB is as it was: the heading at the top colored in 367 ms, the last one after a jump in 1.8 s, a typed heading 13 ms after typing stopped, the slowest keystroke 7.9 ms. The first build had 5.1 s and 3.3 s there: the colors' window came from the minimap's last draw, which a jump leaves at the old place, so it spanned the file between them. It's worked out from where the text is now.
- **Checks:** `minimap` (8): on by default, beside the scroller, the text narrower by it; bars in the syntax colors; the box at the top, then in the middle with the text; a click 90% down bringing the line drawn there to the middle of the view; the box dragged up taking the text with it; View › Hide Minimap giving the text its width back, and Show bringing it back; the 1.9 MB timings. It leaves a picture of a feed-like HTML file, `build/scenarios/minimap.png`. The first runs also left their 2 MB buffers to come back at the next launch: the scenario waited for a buffer to leave Nib's list, which happens as its closing begins; it waits for its file to be gone now.
- **The text shook at the foot (2026-10-06, the user's report).** With the box dragged to the minimap's foot, every small move of the mouse moved the text 14 points and back. The drag worked out the row to go to from the rows in view at each move, and at the very end a wrapped line in view or not changed that count with each scroll, so the row went back and forth. Now the mouse's place becomes a row by the minimap as it stood when the button went down, the text scrolls only for another row, and the foot means the end of the text. `minimap` has a ninth check: dragged past the foot, the mouse moving a point or so there, the text at its end and still. Before the fix it moved 14 points at each move (51,194 and 51,180 by turns); after, 0, and the user found it still in use.

**Colors left behind on the screen, and a TextKit 2 crash (2026-10-02, the user's report).** A numbered list made in the scratch file, "1. ", "2. "… typed in front of title lines, came out mixed: the first item all white, some markers white with orange text, the rest in the list's orange (An Old Hope · Nib's `markup.list`).
- **The colors were right; the screen wasn't.** A parse of the whole scopes every item alike, and so does the tree kept across edits, key by key or a word at a time, at a text's end or in its middle, small or 2.4 MB (NibSyntax's `ListTypingTests`). TextKit held every item's color right too (`mdlist`). But TextKit 2 draws each stretch of text in a view of its own, into a layer, and draws it again only when its text changes. Neither the text view's `needsDisplay` nor a change of rendering attributes reaches it, and the view ignores its own `needsDisplay`. So when a highlight landed, every line not just typed on stayed as it was last drawn: the first item white from when it was a plain line, each "N." in the colors of the moment it was typed. `Editor.redrawText` now has the layers of the views in view drawn again whenever the colors change (a highlight, Find's matches, a copied block's flash), 25-40 of them at a time. Typing at 2.4 MB is as it was (the slowest keystroke 6.4 ms).
- **A crash found on the way.** A scroll to a stretch taller than the view, in a 2.4 MB file, then typing, crashed Nib in AppKit, three times out of three (`__NSTextViewScrollContainerViewToTextRange`, from `-[NSTextView textViewportLayoutControllerDidLayout:]`). TextKit 2 keeps a stretch it can't show whole to scroll to again after later layouts, and an edit meanwhile leaves it pointing at text that's gone. A 3,000-line file didn't crash. Nib scrolled to stretches in six places: the next conflict block, the selection restored at open, the line commands' and ⌘L's selections, Find's match, and the primary caret's selection. It scrolls to a place now (`NibTextView.reveal`): the stretch's start, or the caret. Scrolls AppKit makes on its own, as a selection made with the keys grows, are as they were.
- **Checks:** `mdlist`: 17 items typed key by key in front of title lines in 2.4 MB of Markdown, near line 4,383, every marker and text in the list's color, and new colors having each line's view drawn again (which fails without the fix). `widescroll`: typing after a scroll to 300 lines of 2.4 MB, which crashed with AppKit's scroll and survives Nib's. `ListTypingTests` (3).
- **The suite after the minimap and these (10:50):** 57 scenario runs and 407 checks, all passing but two of `menubar`'s, whose upload iCloud took more than its 20 s to finish (both passed in two runs alone, 11:02, synced in 7-11 s); 181 NibKit tests; a Release build compiles.

**One window of tabs, ⌘T and ⌘W (2026-10-02, the user's report).** In daily use, ⌘T did nothing, ⌘N's untitled buffer opened in a window of its own beside the scratch window, and ⌘W closed nothing at all.
- **⌘W:** File › Close wasn't in the menu. SwiftUI puts Close in the same menu group as Save, and Nib's File menu replaced that group with its own (Save, Duplicate, Rename…, Move To…, Revert To) from its first build, 2026-09-27, so ⌘W never worked; the scenarios close windows directly, so none noticed. Close (⌘W) is back: the window in front, which is its tab in a window of several (saved, uploaded and handed back, as ever) or the window when it's the last.
- **⌘T, and the scratch file a tab (A5, the user's call):** New (⌘N) and New Tab (⌘T) open an empty file as a tab in the window in front, in Markdown until another syntax is picked, and Save… then offers "Untitled.md". The scratch window takes tabs now, the files' among them, as in the user's Sublime; Show Scratch brings that window forward with its tab, and after ⌘W on it, the Dock or Show Scratch brings it back as a tab.
- **Found on the way:** a file window's document was got in its view's `body`, and a redraw as the window closed made a new document for the file just let go, and opened it again, with no window: an untitled tab's file stayed behind (and a real file could have stayed held in iCloud). The window gets its document once now, as it first appears (`FileWindow.document`). And each window watched for its own close from the window its view first landed in, which a tab made from the scratch window wasn't: one watch for every window now, by the text it holds (`Documents.watchClosing`).
- **Checks:** `files` (10): New ⌘N, New Tab ⌘T and Close ⌘W in the File menu; a second file a tab beside the first, and the scratch file a tab in the same window; New Tab from the scratch file making an empty Markdown tab beside it; File › Close closing the tab in front, the others staying, its file let go and no document made again for it. `fileops` now expects the scratch file among the tabs (five in one window, and ⌘1 from it picking the first).
- **The suite after (11:36):** 57 scenario runs and 410 checks, all passing once `fileops`' two expectations were brought to one window (they still had the scratch window apart; 14 of 14 alone, 11:48); 181 NibKit tests; a Release build compiles.

**Go to Heading, SC7 (2026-10-02, the user's call: pulled forward from M3).** Text › Go to Heading… (⌘R), Sublime's Goto Symbol for a Markdown file: its headings in order, each with its level (`#`, `##`…) and its line, filtered as typed with Go to File's scoring, the list starting on the section the caret is in. Return puts the caret at the heading and the heading near the top of the view, two lines above it in sight; Undo Selection (⌘U) goes back. A file in another syntax gets an empty list that says why.
- **What's a heading** (NibText's `Headings`, as CommonMark reads them, a line at a time: the syntax tree of a big file covers only the stretch round the view): ATX headings (up to three spaces in, a closing run of #s dropped, "#hashtag" not one) and setext headings (a whole paragraph, underlined with === or ---), not inside fenced code (``` and ~~~, closed by as many or more), HTML comments or YAML front matter, nor indented four spaces; a --- after a blank line, a list item, a quote or a table row is a rule or text, not a heading.
- **Speed:** found off the main thread in a copy of the text, the sheet coming up once the list is made: 35,000 lines in about a quarter of a second (Debug), 2.4 MB's 351 headings listed in 0.9 s. A sheet that came up first and had its list filled a moment later held the main thread a third of a second while SwiftUI made it again. What's left is the palette's own: its sheet holds the main thread about 0.3 s coming up, Go to File's as much (Debug). The scenarios' stall watch measured that as nothing before: its timer ran in the run loop's default mode only, which a sheet coming up leaves; it runs in every mode now.
- **Checks:** `HeadingsTests` (7) and a `headings` scenario (8): the menu item and ⌘R; the headings in order at their levels, not a comment in code; the list starting on the caret's section; filtering; choosing one moving the caret and putting it near the top; coming up as quickly as Go to File; a JavaScript file's empty list saying why; 2.4 MB found off the main thread.

**Indent guides, matching brackets and tags, closing pairs: ED10 (2026-10-02, the user's call).** As the user's Sublime has them: its settings for these are all Sublime's defaults (`draw_indent_guides`, `match_brackets`, `match_brackets_content`, `match_tags`, `auto_match_enabled`, none set). Each is a switch in Settings › Editor, on by default, and Import from Sublime Text sets them from Sublime's settings.
- **Indent guides:** a guide at every step of a line's indentation, on its first row (a wrapped line's other rows start at the left), dotted as Sublime draws them without its "solid" option, in the scheme's `guide` (An Old Hope's #2D2D2D). A blank line takes the guides of the lines round it, the fewer.
- **Matching brackets** (NibText's `Brackets`): with the caret beside a bracket, it and its partner are underlined in the scheme's `brackets_foreground`; with none beside it, the pair round the caret in `bracket_contents_foreground`, as Sublime's match_brackets_content does. (), [] and {}, not angle brackets (Sublime's match_brackets_angle is off by default). A scan of the text, each type counted on its own, 50,000 characters either way at most; a bracket in a string or a comment counts as any other.
- **Matching tags:** in HTML, the tag names of the element round the caret, from the syntax tree (`SyntaxDocument.enclosingTagNames`), dotted in `tags_foreground` or the tag's own color, as `tags_options` says (An Old Hope: stippled_underline). A void element, with no end tag, gives the element round it.
- **Closing pairs** (NibText's `AutoPairs`, by Sublime's default keymap): (, [, {, ", ' and ` get their closer when what follows is a space, a tab, the line's end or a closer; a quote not after a letter, a digit, an underscore or the same quote (an apostrophe stays one); a closer typed where the same is next steps over it; a selection is wrapped and stays selected; Backspace between an empty pair takes both; Return between brackets opens a line a step further in, the closer under its line. At every caret too (`Carets.typing`). Paste and Nib's own inserts never pair. The scenarios type with pairs off unless one turns them on: they type text as it's to end up.
- **The import report** counts these as drawn now. An Old Hope · Nib's reads "15 GLOBALS USED · 5 NOT DRAWN YET (GUIDES, SELECTION BORDER, SHADOW)": the active and stack guides, the selection's border, the shadow.
- **Checks:** NibKit's `BracketsTests` (4), `AutoPairsTests` (6), `CaretPairsTests` (1) and `TagMatchTests` (1); a `pairs` scenario (9): each rule of the pairs as typed, the brackets beside the caret and round it, both underlined and the guides drawn, an HTML element's tags, and each switch turning its part off. Found on the way: the `large` scenario's 42 MB buffer, still closing at its quit, came back at every scenario launch after; it waits for its file to be gone now.
- **The suite after Go to Heading and these (13:12):** 59 scenario runs and 427 checks, all passing but two of `icloudoff`'s: once the file was back with iCloud Drive, holding it again ran out of time twice (iCloud's helper not answering), so it was neither held nor uploaded. Both passed in a run alone (13:29, synced at once). 200 NibKit tests, once the import report's test expected the globals drawn now; a Release build compiles.

**XML, HL8 (2026-10-02, the user's call).** A podcast feed opened as plain text on the first day of daily use, and HL8 adds a language once a real file of it shows up.
- **What's XML:** what Sublime's XML syntax takes: by name (`.xml`, `.rss`, `.svg`, `.opml`, `.xaml`, `.rng`, `.tld`, `.dtml`, `.slnx`, and the names its menus leave out: `.tmTheme`, `.tmLanguage`, `.tmPreferences`, `.sublime-snippet`, `.csproj` and the other project files), or by the first line: a declaration (`<?xml`), a SOAP envelope, or a DOCTYPE that isn't HTML's (so a launchd plist is XML). XML is in View › Syntax and the strip's menu, and in Markdown a fence named xml, atom, plist, svg, xjb, xsd or xsl is XML, as in Sublime's Markdown.
- **How:** tree-sitter-grammars' XML grammar (0.7.0, MIT), copied into `NibKit/Vendor/` (its manifest depends on SwiftTreeSitter at its old address, which SwiftPM would take for a second package with the same module). Nib's query gives Sublime's scopes: tag names (a name's namespace and colon split off, as in `itunes:image`), attributes, strings in either quote, the declaration and processing instructions (with `<?xml-stylesheet ?>`'s pseudo-attributes), comments, CDATA as a string, entities (`&#` and `&#x` as their marks), and a DOCTYPE with its internal subset (ELEMENT, ATTLIST, ENTITY, NOTATION). Like HTML, XML keeps a tree of the whole file at any size, up to ED9's 5 MB.
- **Half-typed XML:** the grammar wants a single root element, closed, so while a tag is being typed the parse falls back on tree-sitter's repair. The text stays colored, but an end tag the repair can't place loses its name's color until the element is closed (`</a>` after an unclosed `<b>`). Where the repair keeps the end tag, a query pattern keeps its name red (`</rss>` while `<enclo` is typed above it). A stray `&` is plain, where Sublime marks it invalid in red.
- **ED10 and Toggle Comment:** matching tags work in XML as in HTML (a self-closing element gives the element round it), and Toggle Comment uses `<!-- -->`.
- **Fidelity (HL7):** three generated reference files, all at 100%: a podcast feed (2,145 characters: RSS with the iTunes, Atom and content namespaces, CDATA, entities, a stylesheet instruction), a launchd plist found by its first line (715), and a catalog with an internal DTD (943). An XML fence added to the Markdown file matches too. Overall 99.92% (9,541 of 9,549), up from 99.86%; the other files are unchanged. The scope names are Sublime's, except Sublime's `meta.string.xml` around strings and the `.xml` ending of punctuation scopes; no scheme seen so far colors by either.
- **Speed and memory (Release):** on a 2.4 MB podcast feed (3,926 episodes, 39,267 lines), all off the main thread: the first parse of the whole file 233 ms, a screenful's colors 11.5 ms, a keystroke's reparse 6-8 ms. Its tree takes 82 MB, where Markdown's windowed tree for 2.4 MB takes 13 MB (`nib-fidelity --memory`): a parse can't start partway into an element, as it can at a Markdown block. Over ED9's 5 MB, a file has no highlighting and no tree. The grammar adds 72 KB to the app (8.7 MB, Release).
- **Found on the way:** the HTML DOCTYPE's split into a keyword and a name was keyed on a scope prefix that XML's DOCTYPE shares. It now applies to HTML only. A capture warning in Go to Heading is fixed: its listing task holds the document weakly now.
- **Tools:** `nib-fidelity --nodes <file>` prints every node with its text, unnamed ones too (what a repair keeps), for writing queries; `--memory <file>` prints what a file's highlighting adds to memory.
- **Checks:** `HighlightingTests` (finding XML by name and by first line, the colors, an XML fence, the end tag while a tag is typed), `TagMatchTests.xmlElementsTags`, and the incremental and windowed tests on the three new reference files (nine now); the `syntax` scenario (a plist found by its first line, six colors drawn, XML in View › Syntax, Toggle Comment; `snapshot-syntax-xml.png`) and `pairs` (an XML element's namespaced tags underlined).
- **The suite after (13:58-14:10):** 59 scenario runs and 430 checks, all passing except `icloudoff`'s two, as at 13:12. Once the file was back, holding it again timed out on every try for over a minute, while other files' holds in the same run answered at once. It failed alone too at 14:12, after passing alone at 13:29. A try waits up to 15 s for iCloud's upload of the file and then pauses it, and pausing a file mid-upload is what crashed `fileproviderd` before (§4.3), so it's being looked into on its own. 201 NibKit tests; a Release build compiles.

**`icloudoff`'s second hold (2026-10-02), §4.3, §4.9 row 11.** The suites at 13:12 and 14:09 and two runs alone (13:27, 14:12) failed `icloudoff`'s last two checks; it passed alone at 13:29, and in the suites at 10:50 and 11:36. Each failure reads the same in the Sync Log: the file back with iCloud Drive and saved, then 37 s later "not held on try 2" with Nib's own 20 s limit on the pause (`ControlError 0` is `noAnswer`: Swift numbers an enum's cases with values first), and no upload after. Not iCloud's helper, as the note at 13:12 had it, nor a slow upload: iCloud never took the returned file in.
- **What iCloud did** (fileproviderd's log, which names a file by its first letter, length and last letter: `a{20}7.md`). The scenario deletes the file while Nib holds it, types for 1.4 s (47 characters, 30 ms apart) and writes it back, a new document at the same path, 1.6 s after the deletion. iCloud's file provider settles a deleted file by looking for its document: three looks 0.2 and 0.4 s apart, then a fourth about 1.5 s after the first. In 7 of the day's 8 runs the file was back 50-250 ms before that fourth look, which found the new document in its place ("ESTALE: opened wrong doc_id in _validate_file_openbyid [21, 136295, 136301]", three times). Three of those settled it 1-2 s later all the same (11:00, 11:46, 13:05: the old item deleted, the new one made and uploaded 6-10 s after it came back). The four that failed never did: no delete for the old item and no create for the new one, so nothing uploaded it, while iCloud said it was uploading (the strip a minute on: `▲ UPLOADING · SAVED 14:11:45`), and the scenario's cleanup removed it 60 s later, "deleted not reconciled". At 13:29 the file came back just after the fourth look, and everything went as designed. So it came and went by tens of milliseconds. A real iCloud Drive isn't off and back in 1.6 s; an app that saves by deleting a file Nib holds and making it anew could meet the same (not seen).
- **fileproviderd's restart at 13:25:29 was a crash,** the one above ("Nib's pauses were crashing iCloud's file provider"): `-[FPDXPCServicer pauseSyncForItemAtURL:behavior:bundleID:completionHandler:]` putting a nil in an array, an uncaught exception, SIGABRT (the unified log has it; no new crash report was written). It was `bigfile`'s pause, not `icloudoff`'s: iCloud had just finished uploading the 2.4 MB file (13:25:27.285), the scenario wrote another app's version 30 ms later as Nib's pause went in, iCloud began uploading again, and the scenario's end removed the file (13:25:28.8) with the pause still in iCloud's hands; the crash came 0.26 s after. None of the four unanswered `icloudoff` pauses crashed it. The cpu_resource report is fileproviderd at 75% of a core from 13:20 to 13:22, working through file-system events in the suite; the JetsamEvent at 13:23:06 ended only `stickersd`.
- **Fixed (§4.3):** a hold that waits for iCloud's upload (the first at open, after a Restore, and every retry) no longer pauses a file iCloud still says it's uploading when the 15 s are up: the Sync Log says "not held yet: iCloud is still uploading it after 15 s", and the retry tries again (2, 5, 10, 20 and 40 s later, then every minute), the file unheld meanwhile. A retry loses nothing by waiting, and a pause mid-upload is the crash; at a launch it can cost more (below). A file that isn't uploaded but isn't uploading either is still paused once the 15 s are up, as before, so that Nib's own upload can say what's wrong (iCloud storage full, §4.9 row 12). `icloudoff` keeps iCloud Drive off for 10 s, not 1.6: the file provider's first look at a deletion came within 0.02 s for half of the day's 252 deletions and within 1.1 s for nine in ten, 25 s at worst. Its check now quotes the file's last hold from the Sync Log. `bigfile` writes its other app's version once Nib holds the file, as `moved`, `deleted` and `icloudoff` already did. The check's 60 s stays: no wait is long enough when iCloud never takes the file in, and once it does, the hold comes at the first try after the upload (6-15 s after the file was back, in the runs that passed).
- **Checks:** `icloudoff` alone three times (16:22-16:23), all six checks each: the file provider settled the deleted file 2.5-3 s after it went (longer than the scenario's whole 1.6 s outage had been), and the file, back 8 s later, was taken in as a new item, uploaded in 3.5-7 s and held on try 2; no ESTALE. The suite after (16:24-16:37): 59 scenario runs, 427 of 431 checks, `icloudoff` and `bigfile` among the passes (`bigfile` held its file before another app's version came). `history`'s three failures came from another scenario run at the same time: a second Nib opened the test file mid-run, its "opened" version, in the History they share, was the one Revert To took, and its quit handed the file back under the run's hold; `history` and `history-closed` passed alone after (17:27). `syntax`'s one counted 20 languages, where new grammars in progress made more.
- **What it cost, found by the suites after (16:38 and 18:04, in the entries after this one):** `holdretry` expected its file held on the try after its made-up failures, within 20 s; iCloud was still uploading the typing just saved, and the hold came on try 4, 38 s after opening. And a launch 4 s after the run before had handed the test file back with an upload to make opened it unheld: iCloud said "uploading" for 100 s, `merge` with no note never started within the runner's 115 s, and the hold came on try 3, 86 s after the first. Before, both would have paused mid-upload at 15 s; the second is one of the crash's three patterns (a file just handed back with an upload to make).
- **So the suite waits for iCloud too (the user's call):** `holdretry` takes the hold on a later try within a minute, the tries between saying "still uploading"; and before each launch the runner waits, up to 2 minutes, for iCloud to finish uploading the test file (`scripts/wait-uploaded.swift`; a line under the scenario's heading when it waited), as no real launch comes seconds after a hand-back mid-upload. The suite after (21:40-21:58): 60 scenario runs, 435 of 436 checks, both `holdretry` runs, the four `merge` runs, `icloudoff`, `bigfile` and `history` among the passes; the runner waited before two launches, 1 and 2 s. The one failure, `files`' File › Close, had the screen locked (`loginwindow` had the focus), as at 16:57 and 18:04. 208 NibKit tests.
- **fileproviderd crashed once more, at 17:12:04,** the same way, in a suite whose `bigfile` still wrote its other app's version before Nib's hold (its Sync Log: "the buffer is now iCloud's version", then the pause failing with 4099 as the file was removed). None since: its process has run from 17:12:04.

**HL8's first tier (2026-10-02, the user's call).** JSX, TypeScript and TSX; diffs; Git's commit messages and its ignore, attributes and config files; Makefiles; YAML; TOML; Python; INI and `.env`, which Sublime doesn't color; and the other file names Sublime reads as JSON.
- **Which files:** as Sublime's syntaxes find them: by extension and file name (`.mts`, `COMMIT_EDITMSG`, `GNUmakefile`, `Cargo.lock`, `SConstruct`…), then by the first line (a `python`, `uv`, `node`, `ts-node` or `make` shebang, `%YAML`, a repository's `[core]`, a diff's header). Nib's own additions: INI (`.ini`, `.cfg`, `.editorconfig`) and `.env` files (`.env`, `.env.local`, `production.env`), read as shell. All are in View › Syntax and the strip's menu, which are in alphabetical order now that there are 20 languages. Markdown's fences take them by the names Sublime's Markdown knows (`py`, `ts`, `yml`, `patch`, `make`…), and ⌘/ uses each one's comment mark (`;` for INI).
- **Grammars:** eleven, vendored in `NibKit/Vendor/` with their versions and licences (`Vendor/README.md`), since most of their manifests name SwiftTreeSitter at its old address or look for the scanner with FileManager, and three have none: TypeScript's two, Python, YAML, TOML, Make, Diff, and Git's commit (WTFPL), ignore, attributes and config; INI's is Apache 2.0. JSX is JavaScript's own grammar.
- **Queries, in Sublime's scopes:**
  - `typescript.scm` is JavaScript's query adapted to TypeScript's grammar, which names some nodes differently (`public_field_definition`, `type_identifier`), plus TypeScript's types and keywords.
  - `jsx.scm` follows JavaScript's or TypeScript's query, for .jsx and .tsx files and for JSX in a .js file, which Sublime's JavaScript doesn't read.
  - Sublime's own readings are kept: a declaration's name has only its own scope (`only.`) where a type reference's would color it; an arrow function with a return type is a plain value; Python's raw strings are regular expressions and its docstrings comments; YAML's keys are strings, as JSON's are; a pattern's dots in `.gitignore` are separators, but not in a directory's name.
- **Makefiles:** a recipe line is the shell's (an injection), with make's references blanked to the same length for the shell's parser, so a reference first on a line is the command, as Sublime reads it. Make's query draws the references over the shell's colors.
- **Nib's own colors**, where Sublime has none: INI as Sublime colors Git's config files, which are the same format (sections a heading, keys and values strings, booleans constants), and `.env` as shell.
- **Fidelity (HL7):** twelve more generated reference files, 9,372 characters, every one at 100%, and a Python and a YAML fence in the Markdown file. Overall 99.96% (19,055 of 19,063); the rest are zsh's and a fenced tilde, as before. The fidelity test now holds each file to 98%, not only the whole.
- **Tools:** `nib-fidelity --queries` compiles every language's query and says why one doesn't (a node type the grammar lacks, a shape it can't have). A query that doesn't compile is no query at all: JavaScript lost its colors until `jsx.scm` existed.
- **Found on the way:** with twelve more files, the incremental test's shared random edits changed, and `<b>` typed into a JavaScript function's parameters left a kept tree different from a fresh parse. That's tree-sitter repairing broken code, and it promises the same tree only for code that parses cleanly. So a differing tree now fails only where the code is clean; where it's broken, the difference is counted and must stay rare (at least 800 of the 840 edits compared). Each file's edits now come from a generator of its own.
- **Checks:** detection of every new extension, name and first line; INI's and `.env`'s colors; the incremental and windowed tests on all 21 reference files; the `syntax` scenario (View › Syntax's 20 languages by name, and a `.py` file drawn in Sublime's colors).
- **App size:** the Release binary is 13.9 MB, up from 8.7 (Nib.app 16 MB), and the grammars are 9.6 MB of it. The largest of the new ones: Git's commit messages (2.3 MB, its lexer knows 480 tokens, mostly git's own phrases), TSX (1.5 MB), TypeScript (1.4 MB) and Python (0.5 MB); the rest are under 0.2 MB each. As before, a grammar's tables are read in only when a file in it opens.
- **The suite after (14:59-15:11):** 59 scenario runs and 431 checks, all passing, `icloudoff` among them; 202 NibKit tests; a Release build compiles. A Python string's closing quotes are marked only when it has them (an unterminated one, while being typed, had its last character marked); checked with the NibKit tests after.

**HL8's second tier (2026-10-02, the user's call).** Go, Rust, Ruby, Java, C, Lua, PHP, SQL and reStructuredText; LaTeX is deferred (the user's call, above, HL8).
- **Which files:** Sublime's names: `.go`; `.rs`; `.rb` and Ruby's own files (`Gemfile`, `Rakefile`, `Podfile`, `Vagrantfile`, `.gemspec`, `.irbrc`…); `.java` and `.bsh`; `.c` and `.h`; `.lua`; `.php` to `.php8`, `.phtml`, `.phpt`; `.sql`, `.ddl`, `.dml`; `.rst`, `.rest`. And their first lines: `ruby`, `cargo`/`rust`, `java`, `lua` and `php` shebangs, and a first line starting `<?php`. Markdown's fences take Sublime's names for them (`go`/`golang`, `rs`, `rb`, `h`, …). ⌘/ uses `//`, `#`, `--` or `..` as each has it.
- **Grammars:** nine, vendored (`NibKit/Vendor/README.md`), all MIT. SQL's comes from its release tarball, since the repository holds no generated parser; PHP's is the grammar that reads the HTML round `<?php ?>`.
- **What Sublime's own syntaxes do, kept:**
  - In C and Ruby a local name or a call of one's own is plain, where Go, Rust, Java and Lua make names variables.
  - Each has its own builtins: Go's `len`/`make`, Rust's `Some`/`None` and std types, C99's library, Lua's `io`/`math`/`string`, Ruby's methods (Sublime's list of 170, by name, a variable named `index` included), PHP's functions.
  - A declared name is plain where Sublime declares it (Go's types, Rust's structs and impls, Java's enums and constants, C's typedefs).
  - Rust's lifetimes and `Self { … }` are red; its format macros' `{}` are placeholders.
  - Go's struct tags are annotations; a composite literal's type is a variable (`&client{…}`).
  - SQL's logical keywords, `HAVING` and `AS` are plain; what it doesn't know (`PARTITION BY`, `INTERVAL`) reads as a column name.
  - reStructuredText's heading is its adornment, not its title.
- **SQL's grammar makes each of its 356 keywords a node of its own;** the query's keyword lists are generated from the grammar, so every name in it exists (a name that doesn't stops the query compiling). Every keyword is Sublime's red, then the kinds Sublime colors otherwise.
- **PHP:** one injection of HTML over the whole file, PHP's stretches its holes (blanked for the HTML parser, which then reads `<li data-index="____________">` whole), the holes marked embedded PHP and clearing the HTML string round them, as in Sublime. PHP alone (no HTML) has no injection.
- **Fidelity (HL7):** nine more generated reference files, 9,546 characters, every one at 100%, and a Go, a Rust and an SQL fence in the Markdown file. Overall 99.97% (28,758 of 28,766); the rest are zsh's and a fenced tilde, as before.
- **Checks:** detection of the new extensions, names and first lines; the incremental and windowed tests on all 30 reference files (at least 1,140 of the 1,200 edits compared); the `syntax` scenario's View › Syntax, all 29 languages by name.
- **App size:** the Release binary is 21.7 MB, up from 13.9 after the first tier (Nib.app 24 MB), and the grammars are 18.1 MB of it. The largest of the nine: SQL (2.5 MB, its 356 keywords), Ruby (2.1 MB), Rust and PHP (1.1 MB each), C (0.7 MB); the others are under 0.3 MB.
- **The suite after (16:38-16:57):** 59 scenario runs and 426 checks, all passing but three, neither from this work. `holdretry`'s two: the hold after its simulated failures came 22 s after opening, iCloud still uploading the new file at the third try, which is now left unpaused (`StillUploading`, the `icloudoff` work); it passed alone at 16:58. `files`' File › Close: its tab couldn't be brought in front because the Mac's screen was locked (every scenario's focus line said `loginwindow`), and it failed alone for the same reason. 202 NibKit tests; a Release build compiles.

**Swift, HL8 (2026-10-02, the user's call).** The first language Nib colors that Sublime doesn't: Sublime ships no Swift, so its colors are Nib's own.
- **Which files:** `.swift` and `.swiftinterface`, a `swift` shebang (`#!/usr/bin/env swift`, `#!/usr/bin/xcrun swift`), and Markdown's `swift` fences. ⌘/ uses `//`.
- **Grammar:** alex-pinkus/tree-sitter-swift 0.7.3 (MIT), vendored: only its `-with-generated-files` tags hold the generated parser (`NibKit/Vendor/README.md`).
- **Colors:** the scopes Sublime gives the same things in its other languages, so An Old Hope · Nib, or any imported scheme, colors Swift as it colors them:
  - keywords and modifiers (`private(set)`, `static`, `some`) red;
  - types cyan italic;
  - a function's name yellow, a call green, a capitalized call (an initializer, `Text(…)`) cyan italic as a class, and the standard library's functions (`print`, `min`, `fatalError`…) as Python's builtins;
  - parameters, call labels and `$0` orange italic, as Python's keyword arguments;
  - properties, other names and an imported module green, as Python's;
  - `@MainActor` as Java's annotations;
  - a class's or an actor's name yellow underlined, as Python's; a struct's, an enum's or a protocol's plain, as Rust's and TypeScript's;
  - strings cyan, an interpolation's code in its own colors, `#if` and macros red;
  - `as` plain, as TypeScript's.
- **The grammar reads `defer { … }` as a call with a closure,** so the name is colored as the keyword it is.
- **Fidelity:** with no Sublime to compare against, `fidelity/own/` holds generated samples in Nib's own colors (a Swift file, and the INI and `.env` samples), which `nib-fidelity --colors <file>` prints run by run. Swift's colors were checked there against the same things in the reference files (Python's `print` and `json`, TypeScript's `as`, JavaScript's `this`).
- **Time and memory, at 2.4 MB of Swift (`nib-fidelity --bench`, Release):** the first parse 426 ms, a keystroke's reparse 8-14 ms, a screenful's styles 16-18 ms, all off the main thread. Swift keeps a tree of the whole at any size, as XML does: 108 MB at 2.4 MB, 8.5 MB for the 2 KB sample. A Swift file that big is rare. Parsing a big one a stretch at a time, as Markdown, JavaScript and CSS are, would need a place a parse can safely start, and an unfinished `/* /* */` (Swift's comments nest) or `"""` to be closed for it.
- **Checks:** detection of the names and shebangs, Swift's colors (`swiftsOwnColors`), and the incremental and windowed tests on all 33 files, Sublime's and Nib's own (every one of the 1,320 edits compared, none repaired differently; the bound is now 1,250).
- **App size:** the Release binary is 25.2 MB, up from 21.7 (Nib.app 27 MB). Swift's grammar is 3.5 MB of it.
- **In the app:** the `syntax` scenario draws a `.swift` file in these colors, and View › Syntax lists Swift. The suite after is CSV's and logs' (below).

**CSV and logs, HL8 (2026-10-02, the user's call).** Nib's own colors again, since Sublime colors neither, and the first languages Nib reads without a tree-sitter grammar.
- **Which files:** `.csv`; `.tsv` and `.tab`; `.log`, a rotated log (`app.log.1`) and `syslog`. View › Syntax lists CSV, TSV and Log. Neither has comments, so ⌘/ says so, as for JSON.
- **No tree (NibSyntax's `LineSyntax`):** both are read a record or a line at a time, from the start of the record or line a stretch starts in, so a stretch's colors are the whole text's, cut, and a file costs no memory past its text. A grammar's tree would hold a node for every field and comma, and big CSV exports are common.
- **CSV:** each field takes its column's color, round six: plain, red, yellow, green, cyan, orange. Those are the scopes An Old Hope · Nib colors that way (a keyword, a function's name, a variable, a string, a number), so an imported scheme colors the columns apart too. Rainbow CSV's own ten scopes would color An Old Hope's columns white, red, white, grey, cyan, red, cyan, white, orange and green. Separators are plain.
  - A field that starts with a quote runs to its closing quote, commas, `""` and line breaks included (RFC 4180); a quote inside a field is text.
  - The separator: a .tsv file's is a tab, and its quotes are text. A .csv file's is whichever of comma, semicolon, tab and bar its first line has most of outside quotes (a comma if none), or the one Excel's `sep=;` first line names.
  - Where a record starts depends on every quote before it. So a document keeps a record's start every 16K as far as it has read, drops those past an edit, and reads a stretch on from the last one before it.
- **Logs:** in each line:
  - timestamps, dimmed as comments are: ISO 8601 with or without a time zone, syslog's `Oct  2 17:24:01`, Apache's `[02/Oct/2026:17:24:01 +0000]`, `10/02/2026`, or a time alone;
  - levels by severity: errors red, warnings yellow, information green, debugging dimmed. A level counts as a word in capitals or with a capital (`ERROR`, `Warning`), or in lower case where a level goes (`[error]`, `level=warn`, `error:`);
  - quoted strings, and URLs (a sentence's full stop left out);
  - UUIDs, numbers with their units (`112ms`, `2.5s`, `512KB`) and constants (`true`, `null`, `0x1f3a`), all orange.

  The first to match at a place takes it, so a number inside a string or a timestamp is theirs. A line over 64K is left plain.
- **Found on the way:** one pattern for all of it took 45 ms a screenful and 9 s for 2.4 MB: ICU tried every alternative at every place. Three small patterns (timestamps, strings, numbers) and a scan for words and URLs take 1.5-1.7 ms and 0.3 s.
- **Time, at 2.4 MB (`nib-fidelity --bench`, Release):** for a CSV export, a screenful's styles take 1-2 ms and a keystroke's update 1-2 ms; for a log, 1.5-1.7 ms and 1.3-1.5 ms.
- **Checks:**
  - `LineSyntaxTests`: CSV's columns in turn, quotes over line breaks, the four separators and `sep=`, a TSV's quotes; a log's colors; a long line left plain; a document's stretches of a 120K CSV through 60 random edits, the same as the whole text's, cut.
  - Detection of the names.
  - The incremental and windowed tests on the generated CSV and log samples in `fidelity/own/` (35 files; all 1,400 edits compared).
  - The `syntax` scenario: a .csv and a .log file drawn in their colors, with a snapshot of each, and View › Syntax's 33 languages by name.
- **App size:** the Release binary is 25.3 MB, 0.1 MB more.
- **The suite after (17:49-18:04), Swift's too:** 59 scenario runs and 430 checks, all passing but three, none in highlighting:
  - `cli`'s `nib -w` exited 1 once, which it does when it finds Nib gone before the file is closed; it passed twice alone (18:06, 18:07).
  - `merge` with no note logged nothing until it was killed at 115 s. The test file, uploaded a moment before by the run ahead of it, wasn't held at launch (iCloud was still uploading it after 15 s), and it was held on the third try, 86 s after the first; only then did the scenario's typing reach a save. Its sample is gone, since the next merge run has the same name and deleted it. It passed alone (18:06).
  - `files`' File › Close: the Mac's screen was locked (`loginwindow` kept the focus), as at 16:57. With it unlocked, `files` passed alone, all 10 checks (20:24).

  208 NibKit tests; a Release build compiles.

**The merge stall (2026-10-02, the user's call).** In the suite at 17:55 (above), `merge` with no note logged its first line and nothing more, and was killed at 115 s.
- **What its logs show:** the test file was saved and uploaded by the run just before it. At launch iCloud was still uploading it, so the hold waited its 15 s and left it unheld, as it should (§4.3: no pause mid-upload). The retry failed the same way 18 s later and held it on the third try, at 86 s. Every wait before the scenario's first check is bounded, about a minute in all, yet it logged no check in the 100 s it had, and its typing reached a save only when the hold came, 86 s in. Most likely Nib's main thread was held up for about a minute; by what isn't known. The runner's sample of it was deleted by the next merge run, which had the same name, and macOS kept no hang report.
- **Found on the way: App Nap.** The suite runs out of sight, with the screen often locked, and a scenario's Nib was napped like any app no one can see. From about 30 s out of sight on, its 50 ms waits took 250-260 ms, and 40 keystrokes 30 ms apart took 6.3 s, not 1.7. That's a real slowdown, but not a minute.
- **What changed:**
  - A scenario's Nib keeps App Nap off (`ScenarioFocus.start`: a user-initiated, latency-critical activity, the Mac still free to sleep). `-NibAllowNap YES` leaves it on, for comparison. The `nap` scenario hides Nib for 40 s, then types a line, and checks the waits and the typing kept their time; it runs in the suite.
  - A scenario's Nib watches its own main thread from another thread (`MainThreadWatch`). If it hasn't answered for 10 s, `sample` records where it waits, in `<log>-stall-<n>.txt` beside the log, and a STALL line says so once it answers. The runner prints STALL lines.
  - The runner gives a scenario's variants their own log and sample (`AS=merge-no-note run merge -NibNoteTiming never`): `merge`'s four, `holdretry`'s two and `refusal`'s two.
  - `merge` waits for the hold first, up to 60 s, and says when it came late. Unheld, it had gone on and then had no version uploaded while holding to merge from. `-NibStillUploading n` (new) makes the next n waits for an upload find iCloud still uploading. With 2, the file was held 21 s into the scenario and every check passed; before this change the same run failed ("nothing uploaded to merge from").
- **The Nib in daily use keeps App Nap:** it's napped only while no one can see it, and its timers matter less then (A11).
- **The suite after (20:49-21:01, the screen locked):** 60 scenario runs and 436 checks, all passing but three. No STALL lines, and `nap` passed (the longest 50 ms wait 53 ms, the typing on time).
  - `files`' File › Close: the screen was locked, as before. With it unlocked, `files` passed alone, all 10 checks (21:26).
  - `refusal`'s two failed on conflict markers in the test file, but not their own. `mergeview`'s upload at its quit was refused (iCloud had a newer version: the hand-back race of §4.3, between runs a few seconds apart), so Nib took the server's version and kept its text in the journal; the next launch found both changed and wrote both between markers, as designed, and nothing was lost. Its check now counts only blocks it makes (it said "nothing between markers" of the whole file). Both passed alone after (21:03), saying "3 left by earlier scenarios".

  208 NibKit tests.

**History thinned while a file stays open, SY13 (2026-10-02).** History was thinned only as a file opened, and Nib runs for days or weeks with the scratch file open (SC1, its window closed or not). Every version History kept for it (the newest save of each 5 minutes, and every version opened, merged, from another Mac, restored or closed with; open question 22) stayed at full density until the next launch: up to 29 MB an hour of typing at the scratch file's 2.4 MB, where §6.4 keeps all of the last day, then the newest of each hour to a week and of each day to 30 days.
- **Now:** a file's History is thinned as it opens and once a day after that, for as long as it stays open (`Document.thinHistory`), on the document's journal queue: off the main thread, and in order with History's writes. The History window follows when versions go. That's one wakeup a day per open file, not a poll (A11); a Mac asleep when the day is up thins as it wakes. `-NibThinEvery <seconds>` (Debug) shortens the day, for a scenario.
- **Thinning took seconds with a month of saves, at every open as well.** Every save has been a line of `thinned.txt` since 2026-10-01, kept 30 days, and each thin read every line's time with `ISO8601DateFormatter`, sorted the lines and formatted them again (writing the file only when that changed it): 6.4-7.8 s with 96,000 lines (about 3,200 saves a day, 8.3 MB) and 13-15 s with 192,000 (Release, `History`'s code on generated Histories), whether or not anything was due. History was locked meanwhile and the journal's queue waited, a keystroke's journal write (SY1's 300 ms) among what waited. Now the file's order does the work. It's oldest first (`noteSeen` adds the time it's called), so with nothing let go its first line says whether anything is 30 days old. When something is due, times are compared as written ("2026-10-02T12:34:56Z": always that long, so text order is time order), the file's order is kept, and only the versions let go are put in place.
- **What it costs now** (Release; Debug in brackets). Nothing due: 1-5 ms, reading the index and one line, and nothing written (0 bytes as the kernel counts, `ri_logical_writes`). Something due: 30 ms at 30,000 lines, 73-78 ms at 96,000 and 135-228 ms at 192,000 (201-203 at 96,000, 393 at 192,000), writing the index again (10-30 KB) and `thinned.txt` (2.5 MB at 1,000 saves a day, 8.1 MB at 3,200, 16 MB at 6,400), once a day after a day of typing. A file open a week, thinned for the first time: 141 ms, 8.4 MB.
- **Checks:** two NibKit tests. `HistoryTests.anOpenFileThinnedOnceADay`: two hours of saves every 5 minutes, thinned daily for a month: each hour's newest after a day, the day's after a week, none after 30 days, and the rest still counted as had until then; a thin with nothing due writes neither file. `.aMonthOfSavesThinnedQuickly`: 30,000 lines, 0 ms with nothing due and 60 ms a day on (Debug); the old thin took 2 s each and fails it. 210 NibKit tests; Debug and Release compile.

**Untitled buffers' History (2026-10-03).** History keeps versions by path and thins a path only when it opens again. An untitled buffer's path (`Untitled/<id>/<name>`) never opens again once the buffer is gone, so its History stayed for good wherever a buffer ended without it being carried or removed (§8, Disk writes, found it in the scenarios' Nib).
- **Where:** Save… and Rename… (the buffer's folder went, its History didn't); Reopen Closed File's list passing 20 (`ClosedItems.add` dropped the oldest without `forget`); ⇧⌘T bringing a buffer back under a new name, its own being taken; and every buffer that went empty, closed or empty at a launch (each ⌘N or ⌘T closed untouched left its empty opened version, and ⌘N's syntax setting). A file renamed or moved onto a path with History of its own left its History behind too. In the scenarios' Nib every buffer closed with `Untitled.letGo` left its versions: `nib-scenarios/History` was 1.8 GB on 2026-10-02, and 2.55 GB in 1,274 folders on 2026-10-03 (1.64 GB in the 21 over 10 MB, `large`'s; 820 folders written in a day). The Nib in daily use's: 928 KB in 9 folders.
- **Now:** Save… and Rename… hand the buffer over (`Untitled.handOver`): it closes with its last version in History, its History and caret go to the new path as a file's go with Rename… and Move To… (`FileCommands.carrySettings`; its syntax comes from the new name, as before), and the new path opens in its place. ⇧⌘T under a new name carries it the same way. History carried to a path that has some is merged into it, for files too (NibSync's `History.move(to:)`: the versions linked, the entries in time order, the old folder gone once the merged index is written), never into an open file's. An untitled buffer pushed off the 20 goes, History and all (the user's call): its text can now go before its 30 days, the list having been its only way back; a deleted file's item leaves its History with its path. An empty buffer's History goes with it, unless it keeps text the buffer once had: then it's on Reopen Closed File as a closed buffer is, back empty with that text in its History (the user's call). A scenario's buffer let go keeps nothing.
- **Not changed:** History already left behind stays (a folder's name is a hash of its path, so nothing tells an untitled buffer's from a file's), and a path is still thinned only as it opens, so files never opened again keep theirs, the scenarios' test files among them. Found on the way: ⇧⌘T on a deleted file's item writes its text back without checking whether the file is there again (a task of its own; fixed the same day, below).
- **Checks:** `HistoryTests` +2 (moved; merged), 210 NibKit tests; a merge with a month of saves seen (200,000 lines) takes 0.36 s (Release), the times compared as text, not parsed. `untitled`, four new and one extended: an empty buffer leaves no History or syntax; one emptied after typing is on the list with its text in History; a buffer let go leaves nothing; Save… carries History and caret; an untitled buffer pushed off the 20 goes, History and all. `fileops`: an untitled buffer renamed takes its History. `fileops`, `untitled`, `untitled-return` and `untitled-quit` passed, all 35 checks, no paused file left (2026-10-03, 06:59).

**Reopen Closed File and a file there again (2026-10-03, the user's call).** A file deleted while open (§4.8 E) and then closed keeps its text in History, on File › Reopen Closed File, under the file's own path. ⇧⌘T wrote that text to the path without looking, so a file there again by then lost its contents: one restored from the Trash, made anew by another app, or back from another Mac. Found by the untitled buffers' History work (above).
- **Now:** the text goes back to the path only if nothing is there, and the write itself refuses to replace a file (`.withoutOverwriting`), so even one that turns up in between is left alone. A file there instead opens as it is. Once it has (an evicted one gets 10 s), the text it was closed with comes back beside it as an untitled buffer, "notes (closed).md", with the strip saying why: “NOTES.MD” IS THERE AGAIN, OPENED AS IT IS · THIS IS THE TEXT IT WAS CLOSED WITH. Unless the text is the same, in which case nothing comes beside it. An untitled buffer's item works as before, and a deleted file's is no longer held back by an untitled buffer with the same name.
- **Checks:** `deleted` has three more: a file made anew at the path stays as it was, and the text comes back beside it; with the same text there, nothing beside it; with nothing there, the text is written back and opened. The first failed on the old code, as it should (09:13), and all 13 passed with the fix, twice. `fileops` (14), `untitled` (15), `untitled-return` (5) and `untitled-quit` (1) passed.

**Find's crash, and scrolling by geometry (2026-10-06).** ⌘F crashed the Nib in daily use twice on 2026-10-04 (build 202610030918, the 223 KB scratch file), six seconds apart: in AppKit's `__NSTextViewScrollContainerViewToTextRange`, from `-[NSTextView textViewportLayoutControllerDidLayout:]`, once in a highlighting pass's `Editor.visibleRange()` and once in an ordinary layout. The same AppKit crash as `widescroll`'s (2026-10-02).
- **Why:** when Nib asked AppKit to scroll to a place it couldn't finish scrolling to yet (`scrollRangeToVisible`, from Find's jump to a match, the caret restored at open, Go to Line, a resolved block; and AppKit's find indicator, which scrolls the same way), AppKit kept the range to finish after a later layout. In between, the colors' pass for the stretch just scrolled to set bold and italic on the text storage (`Editor.applyFaces`), which TextKit 2 takes as an edit and rebuilds those paragraphs with. The later layout then used a range into text that was gone. The second crash, 3 s after the relaunch, fits the caret restored at the match the first one left it on.
- **Now:** Nib scrolls by geometry (`nib/Views/Reveal.swift`): the line laid out first, then the view scrolled to its rectangle, as the minimap already did. It never hands AppKit a range to scroll to, or shows the find indicator. Far from what's laid out, the line's place is an estimate until what's then in view is laid out, so that's laid out and the place looked at again, up to three times. The caret restored at open waits until the view has its size. Closing the find bar, which gives the text its height back, brings the caret into view again.
- **Not reproduced as it happened:** `findjump` (new) jumps between matches far apart in 223 KB of generated Markdown, by the bar and by real keystrokes (⌘F, the query a letter at a time, Return, Escape, ⌘G), with a keystroke after each jump. The old code didn't crash there either. It checks every jump comes into view, which the first try of the new code missed at 82% of the file (the estimate), and closing the bar (the text's new height).

**Uploads while typing (2026-10-06, the user's call).** An upload every 3-5 s while typing went on set iCloud's daemons writing 122 MB in 70 s (Disk writes, above). Now (§4.3 step 7, NibSync's `UploadSchedule`) a save while typing goes up 3 s after typing pauses, or 20 s after the last upload while it goes on, never within 3 s of the last; on a trigger (another app in front, ⌘S, closing, quitting, sleep) at once, as before. During a long stretch of typing the other Mac is up to 20 s behind (plus iCloud's 15-18 s), for about a fifth of the uploads and their notes. `-NibUploadPause` and `-NibUploadEvery` set them for a scenario.
- **Checks:** `ScheduleAndRecordTests.uploadsWaitForAPauseOrTwentySeconds`. The `uploads` scenario (new): 30 s of typing at a keystroke a quarter second: the saves, at most three uploads while it goes on, one after it stops, synced.
- **The suite after (15:34-15:48), with both:** 62 scenario runs and 446 checks, 441 passing; 215 NibKit tests. It caught two bugs and a check too strict, each fixed and passing alone after (16:02-16:09):
  - `uploads`: an upload put off till typing paused went anyway when its wait was up, typing having gone on: still one every 5-6 s (4 while typing, 2 after). Now the schedule is looked at again then. Alone: 8 saves, 2 uploads while typing went on, 1 at 4.4 s after the last keystroke, synced.
  - `carets`: closing the find bar brought the caret into view 0.1 s later, and Find All closes it: that took the view from the end of the file back to the first of 2,000 carets, so the last line never came into view to be colored. Now it does so only if nothing has moved since (the selection, the carets, a scroll).
  - `nap`: one wait of 345 ms as the app hid itself, not App Nap, which slows nearly every wait from about 30 s on. It now counts the waits over 200 ms after the first 2 s, and allows two (0 of 725 alone).
  - `fileops`: 16 tabs in the window, not 5. Not the user's keystrokes, as first thought: the buffers of `findjump`'s runs that day, each left behind as its Nib quit before the buffer's close was done, and restored as tabs at every launch after. `findjump` now waits for its buffer to be gone (as `carets` does), and the five left were removed.

  `findjump` passed in the suite and three times alone after, ⌘G taking the focus first as ⌘F does (posted keys reach the menu only while Nib is active). The scenarios' own scrolls go through `reveal` too now, but `widescroll`'s AppKit one, which it keeps on purpose.

**Find's matches in one color (2026-10-06, the user's report).** Jumping from match to match, the highlight changed between yellow and a light grey, which was hard to read. Every match but the selected one was in the scheme's find color (An Old Hope · Nib: black on `#FFE792`), and the selected one showed only as the selection, which AppKit draws in its inactive grey while the find bar has the focus, under the scheme's light text. So the grey moved at every jump, and the current match was the hardest to read.
- **Now:** every match is in the find color, the current one too, and the current one has an outline over it, 1.5 points in the find color's foreground (black). It's a view above the text (`MatchOutline`), placed again as the text scrolls or changes, and clicks go through it to the text.
- **Checks:** `find` checks every match's colors and the outline round the current one, takes a picture (`build/scenarios/find-current.png`), and reads it at the current match's middle: yellow (`#FCEBAD`), not the selection's grey. `find` and `findjump` passed (16:29).

**The safety rule lifted (2026-10-06, the user's call).** Since 2026-09-27 Nib had opened only test files, in `iCloud Drive/Nib/Test/` (§8's rule, in code), until M1 passed; M1's by-hand checks were skipped, not passed. A Mac with `allowLiveScratch` set opened any file, and its scratch file was `iCloud Drive/Nib/scratch.md`; on one without it, ⌘O, Open With, `nib file` and a file dragged in refused everything else. The user lifted it after five days of daily use with no text lost and 27 two-Mac runs passed, since the success test (§9) needs any file.
- **Now:** the Nib in daily use opens any file, every way a file opens (⌘O, Open Recent, Open With, a file dragged in, `nib file`, Save…, Rename… and Move To…), and its standard scratch file is `iCloud Drive/Nib/scratch.md` on every Mac, with no setting (`allowLiveScratch` isn't read any more). A Mac without it set and with no scratch file chosen goes from the test file to that one at its next launch. Go to File still lists only Nib's folder and the recent files (its "not yet", above).
- **The test harness's Nib keeps the rule** (`-NibScenario`: the suite, the two-Mac test, `run-perf.sh`, `run-writes.sh`), so a scenario never opens, moves or saves a file outside the test folders. Its refusals say so now: "NIB OPENS ONLY TEST FILES IN A TEST RUN (ICLOUD DRIVE › NIB › TEST)".
- **`scripts/clean-test-leftovers.swift`** took History that no record named, and the whole Sync Log, for test runs', which was true while the rule held. It keeps both now.

**Receipts through a Mac's rename (2026-10-06).** A Mac's receipts file goes by its name (`.nib/devices/<Mac>.json`), so renaming the Air (`m4ck800k412`, 2026-10-02) left `Bill's MacBook Air.json` in each folder it had written receipts in. Every Mac read it as another Mac's for its 30 days, the Air's own Nib too: at the twenty-seventh two-Mac run the Mac mini's strip read it as newer. Those files went to the Trash by hand (2026-10-03).
- **Now:** Nib remembers the name it writes receipts under, and after a rename the old one for the receipts' 30 days (`receiptsName` and `receiptsFormerNames` in its settings). The first write in a folder after the rename carries the old file's entries over to the new name, the later of two for a file winning, and removes the old file; until then it isn't read as another Mac's. A file under the old name counts as this Mac's only if nothing in it is later than the rename (when Nib first went by the new name): one written to since is another Mac's that has taken the name, and is left alone.
- **Checks:** `ReceiptsTests.renamed` and `.removed`. `receipts` has two more: a receipt under a former name isn't read as another Mac's while one under a name taken since is, and the next write carries the first over (a sibling file's entry with it) and removes it, the second staying.

**Nib as the default app (2026-10-06, open question 15, the user's call).** With the safety rule gone, Nib can be the app a double-click opens. Settings › Files (§5.8) has a switch for each kind: Markdown (`net.daringfireball.markdown`), plain text (`public.plain-text`), JSON, HTML, CSS, JavaScript (`com.netscape.javascript-source`) and shell scripts (`public.shell-script`, `public.zsh-script`, `public.bash-script`; Terminal's `.command` stays Terminal's, which runs them). On this Mac they opened in Xcode (Markdown, JSON, shell), TextEdit, Safari (HTML, CSS) and Sublime Text (JavaScript).
- **On:** `NSWorkspace.setDefaultApplication(at:toOpen:)` for each of the kind's types, as Finder's Get Info › Open With › Change All does, after Nib notes the app each had (`openedBefore:<type>` in its settings). macOS asks the user first for the types it can run, and says so through the call's error if they decline: in use, shell scripts asked three times, once for each type (the user, 2026-10-06), and nothing else asked; the note says so now. A kind is Nib's only when all its types are.
- **Off:** the app it had gets it back, or, if that's gone, the one macOS ranks first that isn't Nib. Any copy of Nib counts as Nib (by bundle ID): macOS opens the newest copy it knows, the installed one (`install-nib.sh` numbers its builds by the time; a Debug build is 3).
- **A test run's Nib** changes only a stand-in, never the Mac's (`DefaultApps.fake`). `defaultapps` checks it: seven kinds and nine types, on and off for Markdown with TextEdit remembered, all three of a shell script's types, an app gone (the next one macOS ranks, never Nib), the Files tab (`build/scenarios/settings-files.png`), and the Mac's own defaults the same after.

**The sync HUD (2026-10-06, SY16, §5.3: pulled forward from M3, the user's call).** A click on the strip's sync state, or View › Show Sync Details (⇧⌘Y), opens it over the foot of the text, above the strip; Escape (before the conflict banner and the find bar), its header, another click or ⇧⌘Y closes it. It never opens by itself (A2), and each window's is its own.
- **What's in it** (`nib/Views/SyncHUD.swift`), always the same rows, "—" where one doesn't apply, so its height never changes: the header, "▼ SYNC · SCRATCH.MD … NIB://SYNC". FILE: the name, its place, size and lines, and the first 12 hex digits of the SHA-256 the text comes from (the whole one in its tooltip). ICLOUD: STATE (the strip's glyph and words without the time), SAVED, UPLOADED, ON DISK (CURRENT, or SAVING while typing waits for its save); an untitled buffer's panel is THIS MAC, UPLOADED NEVER. MACS: this Mac, then each other Mac's receipt (SY14): "✓ 21:06:27" with this version, "▼ NEWER · 21:06" with one this Mac hasn't got, "OLDER · 21:06" without this one yet; past three, "+N MORE". LOG: the file's last six Sync Log lines, newest last, the Sync Log window's glyph and word with a save's or opening's size, an upload's time, or any other line's detail ("= SAVED · 2.4 KB", "▲ UPLOADED · 2.7 S", "✓ MERGED · CLEAN: 1 CHANGE FROM STUDIO ONTO THIS MAC'S EDITS"), problems in the red's text tint; read as it opens and again after each new line (`SyncLog.recent`, NibSync: the log's end a chunk at a time, as it's never trimmed). WARNING, while conflict blocks are in the text: the muted red at the log's height (it never makes the HUD taller), "✕ WARNING · 1 CONFLICT · LINES 3-7 · BOTH SIDES KEPT IN THE FILE", and RESOLVE ⌥⌘R, which opens the merge view.
- **Blit's pieces** (`nib/Vendor/FromBlit/HUDPanels.swift`, from `TransferHUD.swift` at `ea8b893`): the boxed panel, the row (its value through the pixel glyphs now), the CRT switching on and off, 0.36 s on Blit's curve, and a fade instead under Reduce Motion (A16), which Blit's app doesn't check. Blit's own HUD has since gone to a two-line log and a WARNING popover, about 285 pt; Nib's keeps §5.3's six lines, about 300 pt.
- **Found on the way:** the Sync Log called a hold made after waiting for the file's upload "Not held" ("after its upload (2.6 s)"), and so a hold of this Mac's own pause; now only a hold not made ("not held yet…", "not offered…", "failed…", "still failing…") says so, and "the hold didn't last…" is "Hold lost".
- **Checks:** `ScheduleAndRecordTests.syncLogRecentForOneFile` (chunks smaller than a line, a line, the whole log), five more in `SyncLogDisplayTests.wordsAndGlyphs`, 218 NibKit tests. `synchud` (4): its log, the file's last six lines in its words; View › Show Sync Details on ⇧⌘Y opening it (97% of the band above the strip HUD black, 0% before; by the item's own action when macOS keeps the test's Nib from the front, as `find` does ⌘F's); Escape and the menu closing it; and a conflict block's WARNING panel (21% of the band its red), Escape taking the HUD before the banner. Pictures: `build/scenarios/synchud.png`, `synchud-warning.png`. Not checked by a test: the strip's click (a button), and the fade under Reduce Motion.

**About, the Dock, Services and Shortcuts (2026-10-06, M3's pieces that don't wait on the name, the user's call).**
- **About Nib (§5.10):** Blit's About window (`nib/Views/AboutView.swift`, from Blit at `de24207`) in place of the standard panel: the pixel icon at 96 pt, NIB in Silkscreen 34, "WRITE IT DOWN. LOSE NOTHING_" with the `_` blinking every 0.55 s (lit and still under Reduce Motion), a dashed divider, the version and build, and ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS ›.
- **Acknowledgements (DS3):** Blit's window (`AcknowledgementsView.swift`), the list on the left and each notice with its license on the right, from `nib/Resources/Acknowledgements.txt`, which `scripts/acknowledgements.py` (Blit's, adapted) writes from what Nib is built with: the packages the app resolves, each from its checkout at the pinned revision; the 24 grammars in `NibKit/Vendor`, each folder's LICENSE (a folder the script doesn't list stops it); An Old Hope's MIT license (`themes/An-Old-Hope-LICENSE.md`, from github.com/jesseleite/an-old-hope-syntax-atom: "Copyright (c) 2015 Jesse Leite"); and the fonts' OFL texts. 33 notices: SwiftTreeSitter (BSD 3-Clause), tree-sitter (MIT, with its Unicode data's license), 28 grammars (MIT, but gitcommit's WTFPL and INI's Apache 2.0), An Old Hope, Silkscreen and VT323. swift-tree-sitter's own Swift grammar is only its tests', so it isn't listed. `install-nib.sh` says when the file is behind what it built. Sparkle comes with DS2.
- **The Dock ✕ (AP8, A9):** while any open file has conflict blocks, Blit's muted-red pixel ✕ on Nib's icon (`nib/Views/Dock.swift`, Blit's badge from `DockProgress.swift` at `2dfcd8d`), gone as the last is resolved or its file closes. No progress cells and no ✓: Nib has no long jobs.
- **Without the Dock icon (AP6):** Settings › General, Show Nib in the Dock. Off, Nib is an accessory app: not in the Dock or ⌘Tab, and no menu bar of its own; the nib in the menu bar, Show Scratch and its windows bring it forward. So it's hidden only while the nib in the menu bar is on, and that switch can't be turned off meanwhile. Not checked yet: that Nib's keys all work in its windows with no menu bar of its own (the user's to try).
- **Services (SC8, PT7):** Services › Append to Nib Scratch puts the selection at the end of the scratch file, after a blank line and cleaned as Paste Clean cleans it, as one edit ⌘Z takes back, without bringing Nib forward (Append Clipboard to Scratch's way); Services › Clean Text puts the selection back as its cleaned plain text. In `Support/Info.plist`'s NSServices, answered by `ServiceProvider` (`nib/Model/Services.swift`). A test run's Nib offers none, or it would answer the user's.
- **Shortcuts (SC8):** three App Intents: Append to Scratch, Get Scratch (the text as it is in Nib, typing not yet saved included) and Clean Text. Launched for one, Nib waits up to 15 s for the scratch file to open, as `nib -a` does.
- **Checks:** `about` (2): the window from Nib › About Nib, 360 points, the version; 33 notices, each with its license text, gitcommit's WTFPL. `dock` (2): a conflict block's ✕ on the icon and gone once it's resolved; Show Nib in the Dock off kept while the nib in the menu bar is off, hidden with it on, back when on again. `services` (4): both Services as the system sends them (a pasteboard of their own), and the three Shortcuts as Shortcuts runs them. Pictures: `about.png`, `acknowledgements.png`, `dock-badge.png` in `build/scenarios/`.
- **The suite with all of the evening's work (22:20-22:33):** 67 scenario runs and 468 checks, 467 passing; 218 NibKit tests; nothing left paused. The one was `receipts`' receipt changed in place: the strip right, but the receipts folder changed under it, something else writing there at that moment (iCloud settling the stand-in receipt just written, or this Mac's own), which leaves the step unproven. It now says which part missed, and tries once more with another version when the folder changed; all 8 passed alone (22:34). The run before it (22:06) stopped after 52 runs: its script was edited while it ran, and bash reads a script as it goes.

**The QA and UX review, and its first round of fixes (2026-10-07, M3; the user's go-ahead).** Six read-only reviewers, one per area (the strip and what shows over the text; menus and keys; Settings, the first run and About; the other windows; accessibility and motion; alerts, errors and text outside the windows), read the code and the suite's pictures against §0 and §5: about 140 findings, 85 once merged, in `qa/nib-qa-ux-review.md` (the plan, to run it again: `qa/nib-qa-ux-review-plan.md`). The first round, the user's choice of the ones that do damage or say the wrong thing:
- **F1:** Replace and Replace All work only with the Replace row shown; from ⌘F's bar, ⌃⌥Return replaced every match with what nobody could see, "" most often.
- **F2:** a closed document neither saves nor restores (`Document.closed`), and its History window closes with it; Restore there saved into the closed file.
- **F3:** a path with no file is still made empty to open (`nib new.md`), but removed again, with what Nib kept for it, if it's closed still empty (`removeIfLeftEmpty`), so a typo leaves nothing; `nib` refuses a file whose folder isn't there (exit 1; it said 0); Open Recent and Go to File say a gone file is gone instead of making it again (`AppDelegate.openListed`). Rename and Move To keep such a file (`Documents.close(keepEmpty:)`).
- **F4:** iCloud's two errors of Nib's own are said in words (`ControlError` is a `LocalizedError`: "iCloud didn't answer in 20 s"), and a Move iCloud didn't answer reopens wherever the file is, saying it may still move.
- **F5:** typing keeps a problem on the strip (and the menu bar's ✕): the first keystroke turned a deleted file's red line, iCloud Drive off's or a full disk's into "· EDITING" for good.
- **F6:** a file outside iCloud Drive, saved, is "✓ SAVED · 10:42:05" (`SyncState.savedHere`, §4.5's local file), its HUD panel THIS MAC with UPLOADED "NOT IN ICLOUD", and not pending for the menu bar, whose line marched while one was open. A test run gets a folder outside iCloud for it (`ScratchLocation.localTestFolder`, in the Mac's temporary folder).
- **F7:** a check that finds nothing newer leaves "✓ SYNCED" and its time alone, the upload's; it moved on every 30 s and at every focus.
- **F8:** the conflict notification says ⌘1 or ⌘2 keeps one version and ⌘3 keeps both (it said ⌘3 "keeps one").
- **F9:** a conflict's other side is "iCloud" as Apple writes it in the markers (`Document.label`), and "iCloud" or "another Mac" mid-sentence; it came out "ICloud".
- **F16:** a moved scratch file's line names where it went ("… MOVED TO ICLOUD DRIVE › ARCHIVE › SCRATCH.MD"); it could say it moved to itself.
- **F18:** the large-file question no longer says every save writes the whole file.
- **F23, F24:** a global shortcut needs ⌃, or ⌘ with ⌥, or a function key (⌥Space apart), saying why otherwise ("⌘C is a key apps use for their own commands, so Nib would take it from every one of them. Add ⌃ or ⌥."); and a refusal stays showing (stopping the field registered every key again and wiped it).
- **F29:** ⌘K and ⌘1…⌘9 work with Caps Lock on and on AZERTY (`AppDelegate.tabKey`: ⌘ alone, digits by key).
- **F30:** ⌃A, ⌃E, ⌥↑, ⌥↓ and ⇧⌥↑↓ move every caret (paragraph moves); they collapsed them to one.
- **F36:** each editor is named for VoiceOver by its file; every one was "Scratch".
- **Found by F7's check:** a version iCloud already had was uploaded again: the upload waiting for typing's pause still went after one made at once (⌘S, the test's flush, another app in front), 2.8 s and then 0.8 s more for the same version, and iCloud's daemons write for each. `upload` now sends nothing when nothing was saved since the last upload began and iCloud took this version. A save since, even of the same text, still goes: the first try skipped any version iCloud had, and `undo`'s undo back to an uploaded version stayed "SAVED LOCALLY" (09:11), while only an upload can say whether another Mac's is newer.

- **What the suite found in it, fixed:** (1) F57's first version re-read History's entries on the document's journal queue after every History write; the test file has 527 versions, and a merge's save waited behind the read: in `external`, Keep Both's save then had its upload refused, a refresh brought iCloud's version with the block back, and the scenario quit while Nib waited for a note, so the next launches (`unsent`, the merge scenarios, `mergeview`, `duplicates`) opened the file with the block in it. The read is off the queue now (History has its own lock), and reruns had no refusals. The quit during the wait for a note, leaving iCloud's older version in the file, was a race of its own: fixed the same day (below, "Closing while a version waits for its note"). (2) F43's first version counted from UploadSchedule's due time, which after a long idle is long past: the first save marched at once; now from the save or the due time, whichever is later (`Document.uploadPendingSince`). (3) `duplicates` picked "an earlier version" as History's newest "opened" or "saved" one, which after F55 was the version as opened, the duplicate it had already dealt with; it takes any but the opened one now.

With the fixes (13:02-13:16): **all 518 checks passing**, 67 scenario runs, nothing left paused, the only refused uploads `refusal`'s own two; 221 NibKit tests.

**Closing while a version waits for its note (2026-10-07, the user's go-ahead; found by round 4's suite).** A version from iCloud with no proof of the version both had waits up to 10 s for its note (`awaitNote`, the 20:55 run of 2026-09-28), saving nothing meanwhile: the file holds that version, and the buffer this Mac's. When this Mac's text was saved before the version came (a refused upload, then a refresh bringing the server's), the buffer isn't dirty; closing in the wait (a quit, a tab's close) then saved nothing, took the journal for clean and removed it, logged "everything uploaded", and handed the file back with the other version in it. This Mac's text was left only in History. In round 4's suite it was `external`'s resolved block coming back: the file opened with it at the next launch, and every block scenario after inherited it.
- **Now:** closing settles the wait first (`Document.settleNoteWait`, at the start of `letGo`): the merge is made as the 10 s would have made it, from the version both had if the note came, else both whole versions between markers, and the close saves it (the Sync Log: "closing before its note came, so merged now", then "unsent edits" when it couldn't be uploaded). Nothing is lost, and the blocks say what happened.
- **Checks:** `merge-quit` (`merge -NibNoteTiming quit`, in the suite after `merge-late-note`): this Mac's line saved and not uploaded, the other Mac's version from iCloud waiting for its note, the document closed in the wait; the file has both versions between markers (it failed before the fix, "this Mac's line missing"), and the scenario tidies the test file for the ones after it. The block scenarios in suite order, `external` to `duplicates`: all 53 checks.
- **Suite (13:27-13:42):** 68 scenario runs, 519 of 520 checks, nothing left paused, `merge-quit` passing in place; 221 NibKit tests. The one was `find`'s new Match Case step, choosing the item before the menu was brought up to date (SwiftUI makes its items afresh); fixed, `find` passed twice (13:43).

Command-Return finishes."

And `find`'s Match Case from the menu, from round 4, requires the menu only when its item is on: with Nib behind, the menu has no window's document and its find items are off (the scenario's own log showed it).

The Layout has no state and no change of views; `findjump` passed twice after (a third run lost a ⌘G to the focus, as in round 1's suite).
