With the Layout: `find` and `findjump` passing (14:22-14:23), and the other scenarios that open the bar (`carets`, `palette`, `synchud`, `mergeview`, `large`) passing but for `synchud`'s F7 check, which caught an upload still under way ("▲ UPLOADING") and passed twice after (14:24-14:25).

**Copy and Cut left the pasteboard empty (2026-10-07, the user's report).** Copying from Nib into another app didn't work, and it never had on macOS 27: Nib's text view narrows what it writes to plain text (`writablePasteboardTypes`, so a copy never carries the scheme's colors as RTF), and TextKit 2's `NSTextView` writes no data for the plain-text type alone. Copy and Cut declared `public.utf8-plain-text` and left it empty, for Nib's own ⌘V too; the clipboard jobs that write the pasteboard themselves (Copy Block, a copy at several carets, Clean Clipboard) were unaffected. A bare TextKit 2 text view copies (as RTF and plain text); with the types narrowed, nothing, and `writeSelection(to:types: [.string])` fails outright. Now `NibTextView.writeSelection(to:type:)` writes the selection's text itself for the plain-text type, which Copy, Cut, a drag and the Services all go through. The override of `writeSelection(to:types:)` that sent only the plain type is gone (it didn't help: the types were already narrowed).
- **Checks:** `cleantext` reads the pasteboard from another process (`pbpaste`) after a copy from the text view (empty before the fix, in Nib too) and after Edit › Copy as ⌘C sends it (while Nib is in front), and after a cut, which ⌘Z puts back. `cleantext`, `carets`, `lines` and `services` pass (15:26-15:28).

**The files open at a quit, open again (2026-10-07, the user's call).** With a few files open beside the scratch file, a quit and a launch brought back only the scratch window (and any untitled buffers, ED3's hot exit). Now as a quit begins, before anything closes, `OpenTabs.record` keeps every Nib window's tabs in order (the front window's first; not the scratch file, which always opens) and the tab in front; at the next launch, once the scratch file is open, `OpenTabs.restore` opens them again, each placed after the one before (macOS tabs a new window in beside the selected tab, so they came back last first), and the one in front in front again. The list is used once. A file deleted since isn't made again; one over 100 MB isn't asked about again (it was open; `AppDelegate.openFile(_:reopening:)`, which also leaves Open Recent's order alone); an empty untitled buffer is let go as before (`Untitled.worthRestoring`), and any untitled buffer not among the tabs (after a crash, all of them) comes back as it did. Settings › Files: "Reopen the files that were open when Nib quit", on by default, as Sublime does (not tied to macOS's "Close windows when quitting an application", which Nib's untitled buffers never followed either). A test run's Nib does it only with `-NibReopenFiles YES`.
- **Checks:** `reopen` (two files outside iCloud and an untitled buffer, one in front, an ordinary quit) and `reopen-return` (the next launch: the three back in the order they had at the quit, the same one in front, the list gone), in the suite after `untitled-quit`; `untitled`, `untitled-return`, `untitled-quit`, `fileops` and `defaultapps` (Files 717 points with the switch) pass beside them (15:48-15:51).
- **New tabs at the end (the user's call, the same day):** a newly opened file's tab went in right after the scratch file's (macOS tabs a new window in beside the selected tab, and Nib's own join went beside its host), so opening A, then B, gave scratch, B, A. Now `FileWindow.joinTabs` puts each new tab at the end of its window's tabs: opened files, New, New Tab and reopened ones alike. `reopen` checks the order (A, B, the buffer: the order opened); `reopen-return`, `fileops` and the three `untitled` runs pass with it (15:59).

**The QA review's sixth round (2026-10-07, the user's go-ahead): §3, the polish.** F64-F85, all but two parts of F82:
- **Words:** a file's name in curly quotes wherever it sits in a sentence or a strip line, the notifications, the banner, the welcome window and the "Couldn't…" lines among them (`Alerts.quoted`; F64); "‖ A NEWER VERSION FROM ANOTHER MAC · CHECKING WHAT IT WAS MADE FROM, TO MERGE IT" (not Nib's word "its note"; F66); "THE CLIPBOARD WAS ALREADY PLAIN AND CLEAN · NOTHING CHANGED", "⌘Z UNDOES" everywhere, "SAVED LOCALLY · 10:42:05" (F67); Settings › Files' kinds in sentences ("plain text files", "shell scripts"), every extension a kind's types have (".md, .markdown"), "no app opens them now", and a kind partly Nib's said so (".zsh still with TextEdit"; F69); Go to Heading's line for a file that isn't Markdown (F77); a quit's first Sync Log line "Closing" (F79); History's sources with a capital (F80); "NONE IN THE SELECTION" in its slot (F81); the Shortcuts' descriptions as sentences, and Get Scratch saying why it failed (F75); the CLI's usage with `--scratch` and the exit statuses, "unknown option", and `-w` with `-a` refused, not ignored (F73); "Login Items & Extensions", macOS's name since 15, checked in macOS 27's own strings (F85).
- **Times:** the fixed 24-hour clocks pinned to `en_US_POSIX`, so the Mac's 12-hour setting can't add AM/PM (F65); the Sync Log's times as History's ("Today 22:29:37") and its sizes as Nib's ("3 KB"; F79).
- **The HUD:** a Sync Log detail's paths as their names and its semicolons and colons as dots (`SyncHUD.hudDetail`; F67); WARNING with more than one block says "FIRST AT LINES …", its words one element for VoiceOver and its MERGE VIEW button another (F68).
- **Commands:** a name starting with "." allowed in Rename… (`.env`), an empty one "A NAME IS NEEDED" (F70); Save… a sheet on the window, its name with the picked syntax's extension (`SyntaxLanguage.fileExtension`, `Untitled.suggestedName`; F71); a copy or a new buffer that can't be made says why (`Untitled.lastError`), Reopen Closed File with nothing left says so, and the Trash says "not allowed to control Finder" only when it isn't, its count said once (F72); "One Caret" (its key not in its title), Go to Heading… in the palette, Revert To's versions and the syntaxes named with their menu there, the Nib folder's files with a file's icon (F76); File › Close Tab in a window of tabs and Close Window ⇧⌘W (F78); the scratch-change alert a sheet (F74); History's Restore off for the version the text is now, and its folder as the window's subtitle (F80).
- **VoiceOver:** syntax names as written ("JavaScript", not "Javascript"), the gutter hidden (the strip says the line), the Toast interrupting only for a failure, About's name and version in words (F82); In Selection's label (F81). Left of F82: the menu bar panel by the keyboard, and words for the Dock ✕ (macOS gives a Dock tile no accessibility of its own but its red badge).
- **Layout:** the Sync Log's "Loading…" for "0 events" while it reads, and a selection a search hides let go (F79); Acknowledgements' list 280 points, its names on two lines with the whole name as a tooltip (F83); the menu bar switch saying why it's grey while Nib isn't in the Dock (F84).
- **Checks:** `synchud` (the HUD's detail, WARNING's words, the banner's quoted copy), `defaultapps` (a kind partly Nib's, the kinds' words), `fileops` (an empty name, a leading "." and back; Close Tab and Close Window), `untitled` (Save…'s names), `cli` (the usage, `-w` with `-a`), `palette` (Go to Heading…, "Syntax: …", One Caret), `headings` (the line for a file that isn't Markdown), `history` (sources with a capital), `deleted`, `full`, `icloudoff` and `merge-no-note` (quoted names in the notifications); NibKit: `AlertsTests`, `SpokenTests`, `NibSyncTests` (the quit's "Closing").
- **Found by the suite:** TextKit 2's undo and redo change the text without telling the text view's delegate (macOS 27; TextKit 1 tells it, as a small program showed: one call for typing, an undo and a redo, against three), so an undo made after a save was neither saved, journaled nor colored again until more typing came (`find`: an app switch saved its Replace All, and the file kept it after the undo). The editor now watches its undo manager and says a step that changed the characters and said nothing (`Editor.undoStepEnded`; Nib's multiple-caret steps say it themselves, and aren't said twice); `find` saves, undoes and redoes, each saved. The HUD cut a Sync Log path at its first space (Application Support, Mobile Documents): a path runs to the next "; ", ", ", ": " or ")" now. Close Tab read the key window only when SwiftUI made the commands again: `KeyWindowTabs` is observed, and the items are a view of their own (`CloseMenuItems`); SwiftUI brings a main menu's items up to date as it opens, so `files` has it do that before reading the title, as the palette does. A tab opened a moment after another put that one behind it (a second look 0.2 s on moved it to the end): each tab goes after those made before it (`FileWindow.joinTabs`). `palette`'s new check allows for Nib behind another app, the window's commands off.
- **Suite (16:20-16:34):** 70 scenario runs, 533 of 539 checks, the six those above; with the fixes `find`, `palette`, `synchud`, `files`, `reopen`, `reopen-return` and `fileops` pass alone (16:36-16:44); 221 NibKit tests (two `AlertsTests` expectations of names without quotes, updated).

**The last quit's tabs with the scratch file (2026-10-07, the user's report: the scratch window at once, then a pause, then the other files).** The launch reopened them only once the scratch file's open was done, and that goes on past its text: the hold (which may wait for an upload), an upload, and iCloud asked for a newer version and other Macs' notes, 1-2 s in the Sync Log (the scratch file opened at 23:53:46, the other file at :48, its own open 50 ms). Then each file was opened alone, its window waited for in the tabs, 0.2-0.4 s a file. Now `OpenTabs.restore` runs beside the scratch file's open, waiting only for its window, asks for every file at once and puts them in order after; scenarios still start once the scratch file is held. Asked for together, the windows came with their documents in any order, so a tab's place is the order its file was asked for (`FileWindow.noteAsked`, `joinTabs(order:)`), and the restore waits till `joinTabs` is done with each (`FileWindow.joining`): a later look had moved one and brought it to the front. `reopen-return` checks the tabs are in within a second of the scratch file's text: 121-135 ms in three runs, its open done at 1.2-1.8 s. `reopen`, `reopen-return`, `untitled`, `untitled-return`, `untitled-quit`, `files` and `fileops` pass (16:59-17:03).

**The QA review's seventh round (2026-10-07, the user's go-ahead): F82's last two parts, and §5's checks.**
- **The menu bar panel by the keyboard (F82):** it took no key but Escape (its rows are plain buttons, which only Full Keyboard Access reaches). Now a menu's keys: ↓ and ↑ light a row, round the ends, and Return or Space chooses it; the pointer lights the same row, one at a time; the lit row is selected for VoiceOver. The panel is one list of rows (`NibMenuBarPanel.Row`), its keys on SwiftUI's `onKeyPress`. `menubar` sends ↓, ↑ and Return to the panel and checks what's lit and that Show Scratch was chosen.
- **The Dock ✕'s words (F82):** macOS gives a Dock tile no accessibility of its own but its system badge, and Nib draws Blit's ✕ itself. So the Dock's menu (`applicationDockMenu`, Control-click or VoiceOver's VO-Shift-M on the icon) has a line for each file with conflict blocks, "Conflict in “scratch.md”, 2 places", opening it. SwiftUI's own delegate hands the Dock's question on to Nib's; `dock` asks it as the Dock does.
- **§5's checks:** ⌘F with the Sync Log in front reached nothing (Find… goes to the text commands, which nothing there takes); now it puts the caret in the Sync Log's search field (`.searchFocused`), and `synclog` checks it. The Sync Log's selected filter drawn blank in `snapshot-synclog.png` is the picture: `cacheDisplay` leaves a selected segment's word out, as it does for a stock AppKit segmented control in a dark toolbar (a small program drew one). F31 and F22 were already checked (`mergeview`, `themes`), F85 in macOS 27's strings. Three are a person's: listening with VoiceOver, macOS's folder prompts on a Mac not yet asked, and declining a default-app prompt.
- **Suite (17:35-17:49):** 70 scenario runs, 540 of 544 checks; 221 NibKit tests. The four: `find`'s first three, as it held the focus (typing elsewhere reaching its field, as before), and `uploads`', iCloud refusing its first upload ("NSCocoaErrorDomain 512", in 20 ms), which moved one of the uploads during typing to after it; both pass alone (17:50-17:51), `find` with its new undo check.

**F50: line endings and indentation built, the rest dropped (2026-10-07, the user's call).** The QA review's F50 asked for commands to change a file's indentation, encoding and line endings (View menus, and menus on the strip's slots) and for View › Hide Status Bar, or a record that they're dropped. With this the review is done but for §5's three checks that need a person at the Mac (VoiceOver by ear, macOS's folder prompts, a declined default-app prompt).
- **Built:** View › Line Endings (LF, CRLF, CR) and View › Indentation (Indent Using Tabs or Spaces, Indent Width 2, 3, 4 or 8, Guess Indentation from the File, Convert Indentation to Spaces or to Tabs), the same menus on the strip's indentation and line-endings slots, as the syntax slot has its own. A line ending is the file's form, not its text (Nib edits with "\n", ED5): changing it is an edit, saved and uploaded as any is, which ⌘Z takes back (`Document.setLineEnding`). Tabs or spaces and the width are kept for the file, by path as its syntax is (`indentation:`, carried by Rename and Move To, gone with an untitled buffer), over what's found on open; not an edit. Convert Indentation rewrites each line's leading tabs and spaces at the file's width (a tab to the next stop, what's left over in spaces; a tab further in stays), as one ⌘Z step, the carets moved with the text (NibText's `Indentation.converting`, `Carets.moved(by:)`; three NibKit tests).
- **Dropped:** converting the encoding (Nib keeps it as found; a conversion can lose characters the new one can't hold) and View › Hide Status Bar (the strip says whether the text is synced, the point of Nib). ED5, ED6 and §5.2 say so.
- **Checks:** `lines`: Convert Indentation to Tabs (the text, the caret, the strip's TABS, the undo step) and ⌘Z; CRLF saved as an edit and ⌘Z back to LF, each in the file's bytes; a chosen indentation kept through a close and an open, and Guess finding 2 spaces again; View's two menus. `lines`, `access` and `palette` pass (18:35).
- **Suite (18:36-18:50):** 70 scenario runs, 535 of 548 checks; 224 NibKit tests. The thirteen: `find`'s eleven and `findjump`'s keyboard phase, typing elsewhere reaching Nib while they held the focus (the find field's query a space, the text changed under the checks), and `merge-quit`'s one, iCloud still uploading after its 20 s; all three pass alone (18:51-18:52).

**A notarized release, DS1 (2026-10-07, the user's choice of the next round).** `scripts/release.sh`, from Blit's: a Release archive (its build number the time, as `install-nib.sh`'s, above the Debug build's and rising for Sparkle), exported with Developer ID by automatic signing (a cloud-managed certificate when the Mac has none: this Mac mini has only its Apple Development one), notarized with the team's notary credentials (Blit's `blit-notary` keychain profile, which answers here), stapled, checked by `spctl`, zipped; `INSTALL=1` puts it in `/Applications`, only over Nib and never while it runs, as `install-nib.sh` does.
- **iCloud under Developer ID, M0's open question:** Nib's folder needs its container (`iCloud.com.banastas.nib`) in the signature and a Developer ID provisioning profile that allows it. The script stops if either is missing, before notarizing, so the first run answers the question; Nib opening its scratch file from the installed copy confirms it.
- **What it checks:** Developer ID as the signing authority (`codesign -dvv`: Blit's `-dv` shows no authorities, so its line printed nothing), the container in the entitlements, the profile embedded with it, the sources not newer than the archive, About's acknowledgements current. Tried on the team-signed copy in `/Applications`: the container and profile found ("Mac Team Provisioning Profile: com.banastas.nib"), the authority not Developer ID, as it should stop.
- **The user's steps:** Xcode signed in to the team's account, as for Blit's releases; the first run makes a Developer ID provisioning profile with the iCloud container in the account and submits the build to Apple's notary service. The Developer ID copy has the same bundle ID, container, settings and History as the team-signed one; macOS may ask again for Finder (Empty Trash) and folders, its signature being another.
- **The first run (the user's, 20:17-20:19, on the Mac mini):** build 202610072017, signed "Developer ID Application" (Xcode's cloud-managed certificate: the Mac has no Developer ID one of its own), notarized, stapled (`stapler validate`), installed; a 5.8 MB zip in `build/release/`. Its Nib opened the scratch file at 20:20:52, iCloud's folder answering at once, and held it: **iCloud Documents works under Developer ID signing**, M0's open question answered. A save and upload from it, and the Air's copy (the same zip, no device registration needed), are next.
- **Not yet:** Sparkle's PUBLISH step (DS2, which waits on the name, question 14).

**The twenty-ninth two-Mac run (2026-10-07, 20:33, `8b8c994`; a on the Mac mini, b on the Air)** passed everything on both Macs, 17 checks, the same SHA-256 (`104b5892…53f4`), receipts included (each strip "✓ SYNCED · ON" the other Mac); the lead line reached the Air in 19.7 s, as before (19.6 s on 2026-10-06). The first since the twenty-eighth (2026-10-06, 21:04): with it, the QA review's seven rounds (F7's upload skipped when nothing was saved since, the strip's and HUD's words), closing during a note wait (`settleNoteWait`), the undo after a save, the launch's tabs, and line endings and indentation. The run uses each Mac's Debug build from the repository; the Nib in daily use on both is now the notarized release.

**Rune (2026-10-07, the user's call: question 14).** Nib is renamed Rune, Plan 9's word for a character, from a brainstorm for a name with hacker roots that puts raw text first; two 2026 notes apps for the Mac were already called Nib (`qa/nib-name-review.md`).
- **What people see says Rune:** the app (`Rune.app`: `PRODUCT_NAME`, `CFBundleDisplayName`), its menus, About's RUNE, the strip's, HUD's and notifications' words, Settings, the welcome window, Services (Append to Rune Scratch, `NSPortName`), the Shortcuts' descriptions, the `rune` command's usage and messages, the iCloud Drive folder's name (`NSUbiquitousContainerName`; the Debug build number raised to 4, as iCloud caches the name), the built-in scheme ("An Old Hope · Rune"), the acknowledgements and the README. 123 string literals in the app and 62 in the scenarios, changed only inside string text by a small tokenizer (comments, code and interpolations untouched), each read first; the command's lowercase `nib` by hand.
- **What stays Nib, inside:** the bundle ID and container (so settings, History, the folder's files and macOS's permissions carry over), the code's names (NibKit, NibText, NibApp…), defaults keys and launch arguments (`-NibScenario`…), `Application Support/nib/`, the hidden `.nib` folder of notes and receipts (renamed, it would part the two Macs), the scheme and source folder `nib`, the scripts' names (`install-nib.sh`…), and comments that record history.
- **The art (the user's pick of two drafts):** the rune ᚱ, raidō ("journey", R for Rune), for the pen nib: in the app icon over Blit's family mark (§5.11) and in the menu bar, eight rows over a blank one and its line (§5.7).
- **Installing:** `install-nib.sh` and `release.sh` install `/Applications/Rune.app` and move `/Applications/Nib.app` to the Trash (the same app, one bundle ID: two copies would leave macOS choosing between them). `install-nib-cli.sh` installs `rune` and leaves an old `nib`, which still reaches the app by its bundle ID; `EDITOR="rune -w"` is the user's step.
- **Checks:** the scenarios' expected strings follow the app's; NibKit's 224 tests (Alerts, Spoken and the scheme report's words).
- **Suite (21:24-21:38):** 70 scenario runs, all 549 checks passing, the first full pass since 2026-10-07 13:16; 224 NibKit tests.

**Updates and the site, DS2 (2026-10-07, the round after the rename).**
- **Sparkle in Rune:** Sparkle 2.10.0 (the version Blit ships) as a package of the app's project; Blit's `Updates` (`nib/Vendor/FromBlit/Updates.swift`, from `297871d`), so a released copy checks `https://rune.banast.as/appcast.xml` once a day and installs only updates signed with the EdDSA key; Rune › Check for Updates…; in Settings › General, a row beside the login item (the daily check and Check Now): a section of its own made General 870 points, over the Air's 860 (F22), and it's 815 so. Debug builds and a test run's copy have no updater, as in Blit. The KVO callback reads Sparkle's main-thread property under `MainActor.assumeIsolated` (Blit's copy warns there).
- **The key (the user's call):** Blit's, already in this Mac's Keychain as Sparkle's default (`ed25519`); its public half in Info.plist. One key for both apps: nothing new to make or back up, and losing it or its leaking touches both.
- **Publishing:** `PUBLISH=1 INSTALL=1 VERSION=0.2 NOTES="…" scripts/release.sh`, from Blit's: the notarized zip signed with Sparkle's `sign_update` (from the archive's own Sparkle package), into `site/downloads/` under a name of its own (`Rune-<version>-<build>.zip`, so a cached old file can't meet a new signature) and as `Rune.zip`, `site/appcast.xml` written, those two committed and pushed. `VERSION=` sets the version shown; Sparkle compares the build, the time.
- **The site:** `site/`, Blit's design (from `../blit/site/`, nothing loaded from other servers, Reduce Motion respected). `index.html`: the ᚱ icon, RUNE, "WRITE IT DOWN. LOSE NOTHING_"; a simulated scratch window typing a packing list, its strip going through Rune's own states (· EDITING, = SAVED LOCALLY, ▲ UPLOADING, ✓ SYNCED · ON AIR, ‖ A NEWER VERSION FROM ANOTHER MAC…, ✓ MERGED 1 CHANGE FROM AIR) with the sync HUD in Rune's words; then Nothing lost between Macs (the guarantees, the strip state by state, a conflict as Rune draws it and as `cat` shows it), Plain text in, Quick edits (a reference file colored as Sublime's dump colors it, the 33 languages), A real Mac app, a spec sheet with §6.5's measured numbers, and the price ($19, 3 Macs, the trial's rule). `legal.html`: terms, refunds, and a privacy policy that says what Rune sends: files only through iCloud Drive, the daily update check to rune.banast.as, the notes and receipts in the hidden `.nib` folder beside synced files (versions and times, never text), license checks once licensing is on; no analytics. `favicon.svg`, the ᚱ; `README.md`, how to preview, host and go live. While `LIVE = false` Download and Buy are inactive, and Buy has no checkout link (no Rune product in Lemon Squeezy yet). Made by an agent from Blit's site, then read here against the PRD and the code (Show Scratch's second press hides Rune; Import reads Sublime's line padding and word wrap) and looked at in the browser pane: no outside requests, no console errors; the tagline breaks after its first sentence.
- **Notices:** Sparkle's MIT notice in About › Acknowledgements (DS3, `scripts/acknowledgements.py`).
- **The user's steps:** a Cloudflare Pages project for `banastas/Rune` with `site` as its output folder, and a CNAME for `rune.banast.as`; then a release with PUBLISH, a second with a higher VERSION, and Check for Updates in the first: M3's "an update verified end to end through Sparkle".
- **Suite (22:41-22:55):** 70 scenario runs, 548 of 549 checks; 224 NibKit tests. The one: General 870 points tall with Updates as a section of its own; with the row moved, 815, and `defaultapps` passes (22:58).

**The site live and the first updates, DS2 (2026-10-07 to 10-08, the user's steps with this session).** M3's "an update verified end to end through Sparkle" is met: 0.1 → 0.2 → 0.3 on the Mac mini, each offered by Rune › Check for Updates…, installed and relaunched.
- **Hosting:** Cloudflare Pages, project `rune` (`rune-52e.pages.dev`), from `banastas/Rune`, no build command, output folder `site`, production branch **`main`** (the user's call). Its first two builds failed ("Output directory "site" not found"): it built `main`, which then had only the PRD, the README and `themes/`; `m0-spikes` went into `main` (PR #1), and the retry built the old commit again (a retry rebuilds its own commit, not the branch's newest). Work goes on `main` since; `release.sh`'s PUBLISH pushes the branch it's run on, so a release from another branch reaches the site only once merged. `rune.banast.as` is the project's custom domain (`banast.as` is on Cloudflare's DNS).
- **The site, shorter (the user's call, "it feels like a lot"):** four cards a section of a sentence or two (from 33 cards), one row of three numbers (300 ms on disk, 2.5 s to the other Mac, 30 days of History), the strip's state-by-state legend, the test counts and the paste section's HUD lines gone, the 33 languages a line of text, the spec sheet nine one-line rows and five measured ones; the simulation's sync HUD closed until the strip is clicked (it doubled the demo's height); the conflict figure kept. `site/README.md` keeps the rule: a card in only for one out.
- **`site/404.html`:** without it Pages served `index.html` with a 200 for every missing path, so `appcast.xml` before the first PUBLISH was a page of HTML to Sparkle ("An error occurred in retrieving update information", not "up to date"). In the site's look, absolute paths (served at any depth), `noindex`; Pages serves it with a 404.
- **The first Check for Updates failed on DNS, not the feed:** Sparkle's log said `NSURLErrorDomain -1003` (no such host). This Mac asks Tailscale's resolver (100.100.100.100), which asks the router (192.168.20.1), and the router had kept Cloudflare's "no such name" from a look at `rune.banast.as` before the domain was added, for the zone's 1,800 s negative TTL; Cloudflare's and Google's resolvers had the address. Flushing the Mac's own cache can't help with that. It cleared on its own.
- **Releases:** 0.1 (202610072333) and 0.2 (202610072336) with `PUBLISH=1`, the test note; 0.3 (202610080018) with the title-bar fix below, offered to 0.2 by Check for Updates. Sparkle compares the build (the time), so a newer `install-nib.sh` build (team-signed, 202610080011) is "up to date" against 0.2; the test went back to 0.1 from its commit (`git show 33f8aa9:site/downloads/Rune-0.1-202610072333.zip`), since `build/release/` keeps only the newest zip and the appcast only the current release.
- **Stale build caches:** the local folder's rename (`_GitHub/Nib` → `_GitHub/Rune`) left Xcode's caches listing hundreds of files under the old path ("Stale file … is located outside of the allowed root paths"), warnings only. `build/DerivedData-release` was deleted (the next `install-nib.sh` builds it afresh); the other caches made before the rename (`-archive`, `-writes`, `DerivedDataPerf`) may warn the same way, and deleting them stops it.
- **The Air** still has the Developer ID 0.1 without Sparkle (build 202610072017); it needs one install by hand, then updates come through Check for Updates.

**The trial and licenses, DS4's in-app side (2026-10-08, the user's go-ahead).** From Blit's `License.swift` (`43f1308`), Rune's own since: `nib/Model/License.swift` (`LicenseManager`, the Keychain's "Rune License" items, Lemon Squeezy's activate, validate and deactivate) and NibText's `Licensing` (the rules, tested apart: the seven days, the weekly check and its 30 days' grace, the minute's wait, keys that aren't Rune's, Lemon Squeezy's errors in plain words; `LicensingTests`, 5).
- **The lock is the text view's `isEditable`** (`Editor.setEditable`, which every place that made a buffer read-only now goes through, and `applyEditable`: what the document wants, unless the trial's lock is on). So typing, ⌘V, Paste Clean, multiple carets, the line commands, Replace and ⌘S's tidy stop with nothing new to gate, and a buffer opened while locked opens read-only. Rune's own changes that DS4 never locks go through `Editor.allowingEdits`: another Mac's or app's version and merges, Revert To and the merge view's result (`Document.apply`), and resolving a block (`Editor.resolve`).
- **Refused, saying why** (`refusedHUD`, `refusedSentence`): ⌘N and New Tab (a toast), Append Clipboard to Scratch, Services › Append to Rune Scratch, the Append to Scratch shortcut and `cmd | rune -a` (`Clipboard.Appended.locked`), `cmd | rune` (a new buffer), `rune -w` (exits 1 at once, so a git commit stops: §6.3), and Clean Clipboard; Services › Clean Text and the Clean Text shortcut too, read as Clean Clipboard's kin (not in DS4's list).
- **Never mid-sentence:** any change to an editor's text puts the lock a minute off (`noteTyping`); one-shot timers only, at the trial's end and the lock's minute, never repeating (§6.5).
- **Shown only while licensing is on:** Rune › Buy Rune… and Enter License…; Settings › License (status, the key, Activate, Deactivate This Mac, Buy Rune…); and on every strip, while locked, a red ✕ tag and "READ-ONLY · THE TRIAL HAS ENDED · BUY RUNE OR ENTER A LICENSE", a click to Settings › License.
- **Off until launch** (`LicenseConfig.enforced = false`); `-NibLicensing YES` turns it on in a Debug build to try it. No product yet, so no key unlocks Rune (`Licensing.mismatch`: any store's key would pass otherwise); Buy Rune… goes to the site's price. A test run's Nib keeps the trial and license in memory.
- **Scenario `license`** (`-NibLicensing YES`, with the `rune` tool): day 1 types; day 8 typed a moment ago still types; a minute quiet, read-only (a picture, `license.png`); typing and ⌘V change nothing; Append to Scratch, Clean Clipboard and ⌘N refused; `rune -w` and `cmd | rune -a` exit 1; another app's version comes in and ⌘3 resolves a block while read-only; a license lifts it; unchecked 36 days still licensed, 38 read-only.
- **Checks:** `license` alone, all 11 (01:17), and the suite after (01:18-01:32): 71 scenario runs, all 561 checks passing, nothing left paused; 229 NibKit tests.
- **The product (2026-10-08, the user's):** Rune in Lemon Squeezy, in test mode, in the store Blit's in too (484060; renamed banast.as, its address `banastas.lemonsqueezy.com`, `blit.` before, its logo the family mark), product 1423394 (one variant, 2223226), $19, keys for 3 Macs; its checkout link is Buy Rune…'s (`LicenseConfig.buyURL`) and the site's Buy button's. The product image and the site's link preview are `site/brand/`, drawn from `scripts/brand/rune-brand.html` by `scripts/make-brand.sh` (WebKit offscreen: headless Brave hung). `license` takes `-NibLicenseTestKey <key>` to run a real key through Activate, the weekly check and Deactivate, and a made-up one.
- **A real test-mode key (2026-10-08, 16:14):** `license` with `-NibLicenseTestKey`, all 15 checks: a made-up key refused ("That license key wasn't found…"), the purchase's key activated ("Licensed to" the buyer), the weekly check valid against Lemon Squeezy, Deactivate This Mac freeing it (0 of 3 in use after). Its first run refused the key, rightly: `LicenseConfig` had product 1423364, and Lemon Squeezy reported 1423394 (variant 2223226) for the product the checkout link sells; corrected everywhere.
- **Live (2026-10-08, the user's):** the live product is 1423364 (variant 2223174), the test-mode one 1423394 (variant 2223226), so 1423364, given earlier as the product, was the live one all along. `LicenseConfig` keeps both: a released copy takes only the live product's keys, a Debug build the test product's too, as it takes test keys (`productIDs`; `Licensing.mismatch` takes a set; `LicensingTests` two more cases). `license` with the test key, 15 of 15 after it.
- **The checkout link is the same in both modes** (`…/checkout/buy/ff20f57a-…`), as Buy Rune… and the site's Buy button already have it: the storefront lists it, and in a browser it opens Rune's live checkout ($19, card and PayPal, no test-mode banner; looked at, nothing entered). A checkout is a page that runs in the browser: `curl` gets "not found" for it, live or test, which was misread on 2026-10-08 as test mode turning visitors away.
- **Not yet:** `LIVE = true` and `LicenseConfig.enforced = true`, and a live purchase on a released copy (launch day). The Keychain items are the legacy keychain's, as Blit's: a copy with another signature (Developer ID against a team-signed Debug) may be asked about them once.

**Find in History, SY18 (2026-10-08, the user's call: in 1.0).** When Find has nothing in the text, Rune looks in the file's History and says which version still has it.
- **The search** (NibSync's `HistorySearch`, tested apart: `HistorySearchTests`, 5): History's versions newest first, each SHA-256 once, with the find bar's options (NibText's `TextSearch`), stopping at the first version with a match; read without History's SHA-256 check (`History.unverifiedData`: it only looks), off the main thread, and stopped between versions when the search or the text changes. A bad pattern says nothing more.
- **In the find bar** (`FindSession.lookInHistory`, `InHistory`): only with NO MATCHES for 3 or more characters, not in the selection, the bar still 0.4 s. `NO MATCHES · LOOKING IN HISTORY…` dim (a wait, V5), then `NO MATCHES · IN HISTORY: 14:02 ›` bright and underlined, a button, or `NO MATCHES · NOT IN HISTORY EITHER` in the problem red; said to VoiceOver as a sentence ("… in the version from Today 14:02. Click to see it."), and the help says History is this Mac's, for this file.
- **The click** (`FindSession.showInHistory`; the bar hands the session SwiftUI's `openWindow`) sets the document's `historyFocus` and opens the History window on that version. There the search's matches are drawn in the scheme's find colors as the editor draws them (TextKit 2 rendering attributes, not the selection, whose inactive grey covered them), the current one in view (`reveal`, never `scrollRangeToVisible`), a line ("“…” · 1 of 3 in this version"), **Next Match**, and **Copy Paragraph** (the paragraph or code block round the match, `TextBlocks.block`, as Copy Block takes one; "Copied the paragraph, 1 line. ⌘V puts it in the text."). The search stays shown in whichever version is selected; closing the window lets it go.
- **Scenario `findhistory`** (7 checks, with `history`'s 5-second span): a paragraph kept in History and deleted; Find names the version; the History window opens on it with the match in view (a picture, `findhistory.png`); Copy Paragraph's work on the window's text puts the paragraph on the clipboard (SwiftUI's buttons can't be pressed from a scenario: it builds their accessibility only for an assistive app); closing the window lets the search go; a search that's nowhere; under 3 characters, no search of History. File › Revert To's submenu is SwiftUI's, filled only as it opens: a scenario can't open History through it.
- **Suite (13:07-13:21):** 72 scenario runs, 565 of 567 checks, nothing left paused; 234 NibKit tests. The two: `uploads` (iCloud answered 15 s after the typing stopped) and `findjump`'s keyboard phase (the focus with Safari, so its ⌘F never reached Rune and the query went into the text: the bar never opened); both passed alone after (13:22).

**Settings' Sync tab, and Sublime Text named as a trademark (2026-10-08, the user's calls).** Settings' "Scratch & Sync" tab is **Sync**: twice the others' width in the toolbar, and a toolbar item's label can't wrap to two lines (AppKit draws it on one, truncating). The two strip lines that sent people there say `SETTINGS › SYNC` (moving the scratch file; another app moving it). Themes' note on Import from Sublime Text is shorter and names Sublime Text in full ("your changes to it included" for the override in `Packages/User`, "tidying on save" for ⌘S's). Naming Sublime Text to say what Rune reads is nominative fair use (the name only, no logo, nothing suggesting Sublime HQ's endorsement); a notice says so on the site's legal page (Trademarks, dated 2026-10-08) and at the top of About › Acknowledgements (`scripts/acknowledgements.py`): "Sublime Text is a trademark of Sublime HQ Pty Ltd. Rune isn't affiliated with or endorsed by Sublime HQ…". The acknowledgements' first line said "NIB — ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS", missed by the rename: RUNE now.

**A drag on the title moved nothing (2026-10-08, the user's report).** The window dragged by the empty title bar, never by the file name or folder on it. SwiftUI's `.toolbarBackground(_:for: .windowToolbar)` (from 2026-09-28, keeping the title bar dark over a light scheme) puts a hosting view in the title bar, and with it AppKit's title label takes the click and the window doesn't move. Found by hit-testing Rune's window through Accessibility (the label on top at the title; a SwiftUI hosting view over the rest of the title bar) and a SwiftUI app of a few lines, dragged by posted mouse events: the title moved the window in every run without the modifier (with a subtitle, with native tabs) and in none with it. Now the editor's background reaches under the title bar only for a dark scheme (`.background(_:ignoresSafeAreaEdges:)`: a light one stops at the title bar, which stays the system's dark), and there's no toolbar background (`DocumentView`); in the test app that keeps the title bar dark over white and drags by the title. Confirmed by the user in 0.3. No scenario: dragging needs the real mouse, which a suite run mustn't move.

**HTML out of Settings › Files (2026-10-07, the user's call, after the QA review's F27).** macOS has no viewer apart from an editor for a file type: every page on the Mac opens with the app for `public.html`, whoever asks (`open page.html`, Python's `webbrowser`, the coverage and documentation reports build tools open, a link in Terminal or Mail). With Nib as it, those came up as source, and a caption could only say so. So HTML isn't a kind any more: Settings › Files has six (Markdown, plain text, JSON, CSS, JavaScript, shell scripts), and its note says why HTML isn't there and how Nib still edits it (Open With, ⌘O, `nib page.html`). Nib still claims `.html` in its Info.plist as an alternate, for Open With, and edits it as before, highlighted, with its tags matched. A Mac where Nib had HTML gives it back once, at the next launch, to the app Nib took it from (remembered when it took it), or the Mac's web browser if that's gone, and the strip says so (`HTML FILES OPEN IN SAFARI AGAIN · NIB STILL EDITS THEM: OPEN WITH, ⌘O OR NIB PAGE.HTML`; `DefaultApps.giveBackHTML`, once, `htmlGivenBack`). The test harness's Nib never does it at launch; `defaultapps` checks it on its stand-in (given back to the app it was taken from, and once only), and that the six kinds have eight types and not HTML's.

**Three grammars' warnings (2026-10-06).** Python's, YAML's and SQL's scanners narrow a `size_t` to 32 bits where it can't pass tree-sitter's 1 KB buffer for a scanner's state (the state's size, a dollar-quote tag's length), and Xcode's default `-Wshorten-64-to-32` warned about it in every build, `install-nib.sh`'s included. NibKit's manifest turns that one warning off for those three targets (`quietScanner`); their sources are as upstream has them. Debug and Release build with no warnings.

**The suite with all of today's work (2026-10-06, 17:38-17:50):** 62 scenario runs and 448 checks, 446 passing, nothing left paused; 217 NibKit tests. The two that failed need keystrokes, and the screen was locked (posted keys reach only an active app): `findjump`'s keyboard phase and `files`' Close. The test runs' refusals got their new words after its build, so `firstrun`, `fileops`, `cli` and `untitled` ran with the old ones; their checks look for the words both share. Debug and Release compile with the new ones, with no warnings. With them, and the screen unlocked (18:52-18:53): `findjump`, `files`, `firstrun`, `cli`, `fileops`, `untitled`, `untitled-return` and `untitled-quit`, all 82 checks passing, nothing left paused.

**Notifications, SY15 (2026-10-01).** What notifies: a conflict (both versions between markers), a save Nib couldn't make (the file, the journal, the Recovery folder; a full disk), and, the user's call past A8's first list, a file deleted or moved to the Trash by another app, iCloud Drive turned off, and iCloud storage full. Not saves, syncs or merges.
- **When:** only while Nib isn't frontmost (there the strip and the banner say it), and once while it lasts, per file: a full disk retried every 15 s, or iCloud's storage every few minutes, is one notification. It's said again only once it has ended and come back, and one seen while Nib was frontmost isn't said later. A save that lands ends a failed save, and a deletion (restored); synced ends them all; the blocks gone (resolved, or merged away by a late note) end a conflict; closing the file ends everything, and its notification leaves Notification Center. The states a retry passes through (uploading, waiting) end nothing, or a full iCloud would be news at every retry.
- **The words** are the strip's, as sentences (NibText's `Alerts`): "Conflict with Air in scratch.md" · "In 1 place: both versions are in the text, between markers. ⌘1, ⌘2 or ⌘3 keeps one."; "Couldn't save scratch.md" · "This Mac's disk is full. Your latest changes are only in memory: free some space, or copy them."; "scratch.md was moved to the Trash" · "Another app did it. Your text is still in Nib, and Restore puts the file back."; "iCloud Drive is off on this Mac"; "iCloud storage is full" · "scratch.md is saved on this Mac only, and won't reach your other Macs until there's room." The default sound (V13); a click brings Nib forward with that file's window (`Notifier`).
- **Asking:** the welcome window's step 3 says what Nib's notifications are for and that macOS asks at Done (AP5). A Mac past its first run is asked at its first launch with this; macOS keeps the answer, and System Settings › Notifications changes it.
- **The test harness's Nib** never asks or posts (the permission is the bundle's, the user's Nib's too). It keeps what it would have said, which `full`, `deleted`, `icloudoff` and `merge-no-note` check: one notification each, through the retries, in its words, and taken back once it's over.
- **Not yet:** the Dock's ✕ badge (M3).
- **Checks:** `AlertsTests` (4: once while it lasts, never while frontmost, closing, the words), and in the suite after the day test every new check passed: storage full and disk full one notification each through their retries, the conflict, the file moved to the Trash and then deleted (news again once it was over), iCloud Drive off, each in its words and taken back when it was over.

**Empty Trash for a full disk, §4.9 row 13 (2026-10-01).** While this Mac's disk is full (the journal, the file or the Recovery folder refused for want of space), the strip offers EMPTY TRASH beside its red line: with what it would free when Nib can see that ("EMPTY TRASH · 3.2 GB"), and not at all when the Trash is empty.
- **How:** Blit's `TrashService` (R5.9), copied into `nib/Vendor/FromBlit/` and adapted. A drive's own Trash (`/Volumes/<drive>/.Trashes/<uid>`, for a file on another drive) Nib measures and empties itself. The Mac's own, `~/.Trash`, where iCloud Drive's files and Nib's journal are, Finder empties over Apple Events, as Finder › Empty Trash does; macOS asks once whether Nib may (the `com.apple.security.automation.apple-events` entitlement, and `NSAppleEventsUsageDescription` in `Support/Info.plist`). Nib can't look into `~/.Trash` without Full Disk Access, so there the offer has no size, and Finder counts the items on the click.
- **Every empty asks first**, with the count and size: it can't be undone. Once it's emptied, the journal and the save try again at once, not at the next 15 s retry; the offer goes when a save lands (not on the typing in between, which sets the strip's state each keystroke).
- **The test harness's Nib** offers and empties only a Trash folder of its own, never the Mac's, and never asks Finder. `full` checks it: the offer with its size, Cancel erasing nothing, and Empty erasing it, the text then saved within 5 s, nothing typed meanwhile.
- **Checks:** both configurations compiled (Release carries the entitlement and the usage text); in the suite after the day test, the offer ("EMPTY TRASH · 3 MB"), Cancel erasing nothing, and Empty erasing it, the text saved 0.1 s after.

**Disk writes (2026-10-02, macOS's disk-writes report).** `/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports/Nib_2026-10-02-131010_m2pr0m4cm1n1.diag`: "8618.11 MB of file backed memory dirtied over 51768 seconds", 22:47 to 13:10, over macOS's limit (8 GiB a day; the report before it, at 22:47, was over 2 GiB from 14:32), naming a Debug Nib, PID 9616, its samples in `History.record` from `Document.open()`.
- **Whose writes they were:** not the Nib in daily use's. macOS counts writes by resource coalition, an app with everything it starts, and both reports name coalition 58896: the Claude app, started at 14:32:45 on 2026-10-01, the first report's start. In it are Claude itself, its sessions' builds, tests and tools, and every scenario's Nib (the scripts start them as children). A report names whichever process was writing when the limit was crossed: both times the `large` scenario's (PID 9616's samples are in `TestHarness.largeScenario`, `Untitled.create`, and History keeping its 42 MB buffer). Only this Mac's own disk counts: the repository and `build/` are on an external drive. Measured with `coalition_info_resource_usage`, and `proc_pid_rusage` on each of the coalition's processes every half second for two hours: by 16:21, 13.8 GB in 26 hours and 140,609 processes. One suite run's scenario Nibs wrote 355 MB, 232 MB of it `large` (a 4.9 and a 42 MB untitled buffer, each written, opened into History, journaled, saved and closed into History); 13 `large` runs fall in the report's window, about 3 GB. Claude itself wrote 1.4 GB in its first 24 hours, up to 290 MB an hour while sessions worked; a NibKit test run 11-14 MB. The Nib in daily use (the Release, 13:08 to its quit at 16:04, with the 223 KB scratch file) wrote 1.9 MB.
- **`large` left 92 MB in the scenarios' History every run**, and History thins only a path that's opened again: `nib-scenarios/History` was 1.8 GB, 1.37 GB of it 15 `large` runs. `large` removes its buffers' History now. In daily use an untitled buffer's History is left the same way when Rename… or Save… lets it go, or when Reopen Closed File's list passes 20: not changed.
- **What Nib writes, and which code:** a `writes` scenario (`scripts/run-writes.sh [bytes]`, six to eleven minutes, never beside the suite) opens a generated Markdown file in the test folder, types at its end and then near its top as a person types (12 sentences each, about 2.5 minutes, a save every 4-5 s), sits a minute, checks the note as when Nib comes forward, takes in another Mac's version (its note, then the file) and types two sentences after it, and closes the file. NibSync's `WriteMeter` counts each writer's writes and the kernel's count while they ran; the test's own writes are counted apart. Today's code and the fixes below, on the same scenario (Debug; MB as the disk-writes report counts):

  | | 2.4 MB, before | after | 223 KB, before | after |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Opening | 3.7 | 2.8 | 0.6 | 0.6 |
  | Typing at the end | 118 | 13 | 30 | 8.0 |
  | Typing near the top | 92 | 108; held, 11-24 | 34 | 15 |
  | A minute idle | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.3 (the 5 minutes' save into History) |
  | Another Mac's version, two sentences after | 29 | 4.5 | 7.3 | 2.0 |
  | Closing | 2.6 | 2.6 | 0.4 | 0.4 |

- **Saves wrote the whole file:** the 41 saves of typing at the end of 2.4 MB wrote 98 MB. A save now writes from the first byte that differs from the file on disk (at the same length, to the last that differs), compared a megabyte at a time, in place and compare-and-swap as before; identical bytes aren't written (`FileAccess.overwriteInPlace`, §4.3 step 5). The same 41 saves wrote 1 KB of the file, 1.3 MB as the kernel counts (the fsyncs' metadata). Near the top everything after the caret still goes.
- **The journal wrote a whole snapshot at every pause below a million characters**, and above it one a minute: at 223 KB, 59 and 71 snapshots in the two typing phases (17 and 24 MB); at 2.4 MB, 3 in each (8-10 MB). Every text logs its edits now (§4.3 step 1): one snapshot as its journal starts, a line a pause, a new snapshot at 1 MB of lines, none by the clock. The log keeps 64 KB of room ahead of its lines (zeros, read as its end), since a line written into room the file has counts 2-6 KB and one appended about 20.
- **A save that wrote much holds the next back** (the user's call; SY2, §4.3 step 2): the next waits at least its bytes ÷ 80 KB/s (`SaveSchedule.rate`). Typing near the top of 2.4 MB had written 2.4 MB every 4-5 s, about 2.6 GB an hour; held, a save every 30 s: 4 saves in 106 s in one run and 8 in 235 s in another (the typing's pace follows the Mac's load in Debug), 11 and 24 MB, about 0.36 GB an hour. The end of a file, and small files, aren't held; the triggers don't wait.
- **The note went back and forth to iCloud for good.** A check for another Mac's note set off by the `.nib` folder or the note changing read it fresh, evicting it and downloading it again, which changed them again. From the first eviction that took, that was every 0.5-2 s for as long as the file was open, frontmost or not: in the scenario, 56 fresh reads in an idle minute and 30 in the 30 s after a check; in the Release in daily use, from at least 14:30 to its quit at 16:04 (the note's flags flipping between downloaded and evicted). Nib wrote nothing for it, but its notes queue was never idle and so never drained its autorelease pool: the Release grew 3 MB a minute, idle in the background (184 to 219 MB from 14:55 to 15:04, then 132 MB at once as the queue went idle for a moment; §6.5's unexplained growth). Now a check set off by those changes reads the note as it is here, and only Nib's own checks (open, held, coming forward, every 30 s in front, the network back, a receipt) evict it; one check runs at a time, another asked for meanwhile once after it; every IOQueue drains a pool after each job. The scenario: 1 fresh read and 2 local in the 30 s after a check, none idle.
- **Per save, what's left:** a note (about 70 KB as the kernel counts a 220-byte coordinated, atomic write), a receipt (at most every 5 s, about 55 KB), two Sync Log lines (about 20 KB each), History's seen line (about 20 KB): about 6 MB in each typing phase, most of what typing at the end costs now, about 240 MB an hour of steady typing at 2.4 MB and 190 MB at 223 KB. Up to 3 MB a phase isn't placed by the meter (it isn't AppKit's saved state: there's none for Nib on this Mac).
- **iCloud's daemons write far more than Nib for each upload:** while the scenario typed near the top of 223 KB (a save, an upload and a note every 4-5 s), bird, fileproviderd, nsurlsessiond and cloudd wrote 122 MB in 70 s, against 3-7 MB a minute otherwise, in their own coalitions. The hold helps where saves write much; uploading less often while typing (§4.3 step 7: every 3 s) would help everywhere: not changed then, done 2026-10-06 (below).
- **Memory (Debug, the scenario's 2.4 MB file and its scratch file):** open 148 MB, typing 188-198, the file closed 96, the most 311 (before: 197, 218-223, 130, 350). The Release's 168 MB the user saw at 14:15 had the note loop's growth in it; its settled size with the 223 KB scratch file is still to be measured optimized (`run-perf.sh`).
- **Finding the writes yourself:** `sudo fs_usage -w -f filesys Nib` lists each write call and `sudo fs_usage -w -f diskio Nib` each write to disk with its file (the user's step: they need sudo); `proc_pid_rusage` (`ri_logical_writes`, what the report counts) and `coalition_info_resource_usage` need nothing.
- **Checks:** 203 NibKit tests, new: `FileAccessTests.aSaveWritesOnlyWhatChanged`, `JournalTests.smallTextsLogTheirEditsToo` and `.aSnapshotOnceTheLogIsFull` (in place of the minute's test), `ScheduleAndRecordTests.aBigSaveHoldsTheNextBack`; the torn-line and big-text tests read a log with room. The `writes` scenario's checks (the file holds exactly the buffer after each typing phase, one journal snapshot, the end's saves writing only what changed, the note check) pass at both sizes, and with the hold (17:39); today's code fails the last three, as it should. `crash-big` watches the log's lines (`Journal.logBytes`), not its file's size. The suite with all of it (17:00, this worktree's build, the hold included): 60 scenario runs and 431 checks, all passing; `type`'s keystroke to the journal 237 ms, now through the log.

## 9. Success criteria

How to know Rune has replaced Sublime:

1. **Sublime goes unused.** Sublime not opened for 30 consecutive days of normal work, checked with `mdls -name kMDItemLastUsedDate "/Applications/Sublime Text.app"`. After that, Sublime is removed from the Dock (and eventually deleted).
2. **Zero lost edits across Macs** over the same 30 days: every divergence in the Sync Log ends as merged or resolved, History holds every input, and no `scratch <n>.md` file is left in the folder (`ls`).
3. **No stale-copy surprises:** every time a Mac merged remote changes into local edits, it's in the Sync Log (a count, not a failure). If the count is above zero, it's the input to V2 (§4.10).
4. **Plain paste works every time:** 100% of the paste corpus comes out as expected, and J1's old routine (new tab, paste, select all, copy, close, "Don't Save") is gone.
5. **Highlighting matches:** at least 98% of non-whitespace characters in the reference files (one each of HTML, CSS, JavaScript, JSON, Markdown with fenced code, and a zsh script, from the user's real work) colored as Sublime colors them, with An Old Hope · Rune in both.
6. **As fast as Sublime:** the §6.5 targets met, and keystroke latency within 1 ms of Sublime's on the same Mac (Typometer). Subjectively: after two weeks, no moment of "Sublime would have been faster".
7. **Small:** idle at 0% CPU, and every shipped feature traceable to a job (J1-J4) or a guarantee (§4.1).

---

## 10. Open questions

1. ~~**Where does scratch.md live today, and must it stay there?**~~ **Decided (2026-09-27):** `iCloud Drive/_scratch2.md`, opened where it is (SC1). That settles §4.2 on option (a), a plain iCloud Drive path. Still open: when a scratch file is archived and a new one started, should Nib do the rollover (a command that archives and starts the next file), or is it done by hand as now?
2. **How many Macs, and are they usually on the same network when you switch between them** (desk and laptop at home), or apart (home and office)? This decides V2: LAN peer or CloudKit (§4.10).
3. **How fast is a typical switch?** Seconds (lid closes, turn to the desktop) or minutes? It sets the latency bar for V2.
4. ~~**Do all your Macs run macOS 27 on Apple silicon?**~~ **Answered (2026-09-27):** yes. Blit's platform rule (T1) holds.
5. **Merges:** should clean merges apply on their own with a notice (recommended), or always ask? And should overlapping edits be written into the file as conflict markers (recommended: durable, synced, visible anywhere), or held inside Nib until resolved?
6. **Autosave for every file, or only files in iCloud Drive?** Autosaving everything follows "no unsaved state in memory", but autosaving files under a live-reload dev server or a config file a daemon watches fires those tools on every pause. Local History is the safety net either way.
7. **What does "clean" mean for ⌘V?** Plain text only (recommended for ⌘V), or also: remove invisible characters, straighten smart quotes and dashes, trim trailing spaces, unwrap hard line breaks from PDFs? Paste Clean (⌥⇧⌘V) would do the rest.
8. **Does "paste anything" include images (recognize the text) and files (paste their path, or their contents)?** And where should a plain paste land when Nib isn't frontmost: the clipboard in place (Clean Clipboard), scratch at the caret, or a new untitled buffer?
9. **Theme details.** ~~Light mode, and the purple~~ **Decided (2026-09-27):** Nib is dark only, always (A17), and the purple is removed in An Old Hope · Nib (HL10). Still open: match Sublime's actual rendering, or the scheme's intent, where they differ (JSON keys, HL7)? ~~Is An Old Hope customized in Sublime's `Packages/User`?~~ No: Sublime's copy is identical (Reference setup). And do the HL10 changes look right in use, or should numbers take another color than orange?
10. **Which Sublime commands do you actually use?** In particular multiple carets (⌘D, ⇧⌘L, column select) and any ⌘K chords (⌘K ⌘U upper case, ⌘K ⌘B sidebar). Multiple carets are the most expensive P1 item. **Multiple carets decided (2026-10-01): in** (ED8, M2). The user doesn't use them, but Nib will be sold, and buyers coming from Sublime or VS Code expect ⌘D and column select. Still open: the ⌘K chords.
11. ~~**Tabs:** native window tabs (recommended, A5), or a Sublime-style tab bar? Should files open as tabs in one window by default?~~ **Decided:** native window tabs, files as tabs of one window (built 2026-09-28), and since 2026-10-02 the scratch file a tab with them, as in the user's Sublime (A5).
12. **Untitled buffers:** local only (recommended), or synced so they follow you like scratch.md? *Built local only (2026-09-28, §8); syncing them can still be added.*
13. ~~**Is Nib a personal tool, given away, or sold like Blit?**~~ **Decided (2026-09-30): sold like Blit**, of three choices written up the same day (a personal tool, a free download, sold). That brings in DS2 and DS3, Settings' Updates and License panes and §2's secondary users: a notarized build from `nib.banast.as`, a single page in Blit's design (`../blit/site/`), Sparkle updates from an EdDSA-signed appcast there, Blit's licensing (Lemon Squeezy keys, a 7-day trial, no Nib server; T6), and the terms, privacy and refund pages Lemon Squeezy's store review asked Blit for (`site/legal.html`, 2026-09-26). It also means support on other people's Macs, when Nib's sync rules (§4.3) were learned on two, and the name has to be cleared (question 14). **Price (2026-09-30): $19, one-time,** as Blit, on Blit's terms (updates to 1.x, 30-day refunds). **Activations (2026-09-30): 3 Macs a license,** set on the Lemon Squeezy product (Nib's point is two or more Macs). **The trial (2026-09-30):** DS4, read-only after 7 days, never stopping saving, sync, History or the journal.

    **Checked (2026-09-30): An Old Hope · Nib may ship built in.** It comes from your Sublime port (`../an-old-hope-theme`, built on Jesse Leite's Atom theme), whose license is MIT, © 2015 Jesse Leite. MIT allows selling on one condition: the copyright and permission notice goes with every copy. `themes/an-old-hope-nib.tmTheme` credits him ("Based on @JesseLeite's theme for Atom") but doesn't carry the notice, so it goes in the app's notices (DS3), with the dependencies'.

    **When:** Sparkle's feed URL and public key have to be settled before the first build that goes to a Mac other than yours. A build's are fixed in its `Info.plist` (`SUFeedURL`, `SUPublicEDKey`, as in Blit's `Support/Info.plist`), and every copy installed from it looks there for updates. On your own two Macs, adding Sparkle later costs one install by hand on each. The site waits for M3, which builds the HUD, icon and About it would show.
14. ~~**The name.**~~ **Decided (2026-10-07): Rune** (renamed that day; §8, "Rune"). Before: ~~Casing~~ **Decided (2026-09-28):** Nib, capitalized like Blit (lowercase `nib` from 2026-09-27 until then); the bundle ID stays `com.banastas.nib`. Still open, and needed before Nib goes on sale (question 13): "Nib" collides with Apple's `.nib` Interface Builder files and `NSNib`, which makes it hard to search for and possibly to trademark. **Screened (2026-10-07, `qa/nib-name-review.md`):** worse than that. Two notes apps for the Mac are called Nib in 2026: Palú Labs' (a quick-capture window behind a global shortcut, Markdown files, a notarized download from `nib.palu.io`) and FIVENINE DESIGN's (notes for iPhone, iPad and Mac), besides the health insurer nib and Apple's `.nib`. The write-up recommends renaming before sale: Spool (no Mac app under it), or Stet (the proofreader's "let it stand", but a Mac dictation app launched under it the same day) or Tittle (the dot over *i*, near a golf brand); Plume, Stylo, Penna, Quire, Margin and Kept are taken by notes or writing apps. **The user's pick (2026-10-07): Rune**, from a second brainstorm for a name with hacker roots that puts raw text first: Plan 9's word for a character (Pike and Thompson's, as Blit was Pike's terminal). Screened the same way: no current Mac notes or text app under it (a dormant Java editor on SourceForge, 2022; Sticky Runes, Mac sticky notes; a Rust scripting language), no US class 9 RUNE mark found; Roon, the music software, is said the same, a point for the lawyer. A USPTO search and a lawyer before sale; the rename itself is a round of its own.
15. ~~**Should Nib become the default app** for `.md`, `.html`, `.css`, `.js`, `.json` and shell scripts, or register only as an alternate for Open With?~~ **Decided (2026-10-06): the default app, a kind at a time.** Settings › Files has a switch for each of those and plain text (§5.8); off gives a kind back to the app it had. **HTML taken out (2026-10-07, the user's call):** macOS opens every page on the Mac with the app for `.html`, so with Nib as it, links and reports came up as source (§8). Nib's own Info.plist still claims them as an alternate, so nothing changes until the user turns one on (§8, "Nib as the default app").
16. ~~**Nib as `$EDITOR`** (`nib -w`) for git commit messages and `crontab -e`: wanted?~~ **Decided (2026-10-06): wanted.** `EDITOR="nib -w"` in the user's shell, with the `nib` tool installed (`scripts/install-nib-cli.sh`): the user's step. It opens any file since the safety rule went.
17. **Largest files you edit?** Big logs or data dumps would move ED9's thresholds.
18. **Should caret and scroll positions follow you between Macs?** (Recommended: no; each Mac keeps its own.)
19. **Blit and Nib together:** should Blit get an "Open in Nib" for text files (its Quick Look already handles `.nfo` and subtitles), or a Nib-backed quick edit for small remote files? Blit lists remote editing as a non-goal, so this would change Blit's PRD too.
20. ~~**Shared package:** name, and public or private?~~ **Decided (2026-09-29):** no shared package; each app keeps its code in its own repository, and either copies from the other what it needs (§7).
21. ~~**Receipts in the user's folder**~~ **Decided (2026-09-28):** not a hidden folder at the root of iCloud Drive. Nib gets its own visible folder there (`iCloud Drive/Nib/`, §4.2), the scratch file moves into it, and the notes live hidden inside it.
22. ~~**History of a file up to 5 MB keeps every save for a day.**~~ **Decided (2026-10-01): (b).** Autosave comes 1 s after typing stops, or every 5 s of steady typing, and each save was a whole copy: for the 2.4 MB scratch file, up to 720 copies an hour, 1.7 GB an hour of steady typing, kept for a day before the hourly thinning. History now keeps the newest save of each 5 minutes, plus every version opened, merged, from another Mac, restored or closed with (at most 12 saves an hour, 29 MB an hour at 2.4 MB). Built the same day (§8). The other options were (a) every save, as it was, and (c) every save kept as the change from the one before (as the journal's edit log), a bigger piece of work.
